# Plans & Rates (code: rating) — Business Rules

Format: BR-XXX-### · rule · source (spec/decision §) · status (CONFIRMED / PLACEHOLDER / OPEN) · test ref.
Rules CITE B-docs; they never copy values out of them (one source of truth).

| ID | Rule | Source | Status | Test |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| BR-RAT-001 | Meter multiplier applies BEFORE tier evaluation | B4 §Consumption | CONFIRMED (2026-08-07) | GT-5 |
| BR-RAT-002 | Tier thresholds reset each bill; thresholds scale with prorated period | B4 §Tiered | CONFIRMED | GT-2* |
| BR-RAT-003 | Round per line item, half-up, 2dp, at finalization only | B4 §Rounding | CONFIRMED | all GTs |
| BR-RAT-004 | Proration = calendar days; base AND usage both prorated. **EXTENDED 2026-08-12 (PO, OQ-155) — A TARIFF CHANGE INSIDE A BACK-BILLED PERIOD IS SPLIT BY DAYS.** PO verbatim: _"Split by days"_. The period is priced as **two day-weighted segments, each at the version in force on those days** — not the whole period at one version, and not refused. Consistent with B4.3's calendar-day proration, which is frozen. **Worked, on WE-10's February water reprice (6 kgal, 3 each half, 14/28 days each side): v3 gives base 4.00 + tier-1 10.00 + tier-2 3.25; v4 gives base 4.25 + tier-1 10.63 + tier-2 3.43 → group 35.56.** ⚠ **THE CENT IS DELIBERATE AND IS NOT A BUG: the group is 35.56, not the 35.55 the QUESTION illustrated.** The question summed v4's two tier amounts before rounding (10.625 + 3.425 = 14.05); **BR-RAT-003 / B4.4 is finance-signed and rounds PER LINE**, so 10.625 → **10.63** and 3.425 → **3.43** separately (**14.06**). The ruled RULE is implemented exactly; only the question's illustrative arithmetic differs, and the cent was reported rather than hand-adjusted to match the text — the same shape as the **$8.30 → $8.31** tax-split cent the PO ruled on earlier the same day. **THE BILL IS STILL TAXED EXACTLY ONCE:** the rating seam takes ONE billing period per call, so unequal segments force one call per proration factor; every non-tax-bearing call's finalized lines are carried into the tax-bearing call and the tax pass runs once over Σ(rounded taxable lines), which is B4.2 exactly — worked on a Jan–Jun sub-period split 90/181 and 91/181: subtotal **75.59**, tax **6.24** in one pass, where taxing each segment apart and summing gives **6.23**. **WHAT THE RULING DOES NOT DO: it day-weights the BASE CHARGE and the TIER LADDER; it does not invent usage.** Each segment is measured from its OWN reads (B4.3 as amended 2026-08-11 — metered usage WITH a read is never prorated). **A segment with NO read on the cut day (OQ-191, CLOSED PO 2026-08-12): _"B is correct; and if required give option C also as backup ; I will make B default and C as option for user"_ — the DEFAULT is a day-weighted usage estimate (a tariff change on 15 Feb over a 1–28 Feb period of 6 kgal gives 14/28 × 6 = 3 kgal each side) and the segment is MARKED AS ESTIMATED on the bill, because it is one; asking METERING for an estimate under its own rules (BR-MET-026) is offered as a TENANT OPTION.** This SUPERSEDES the refuse-by-name behaviour first implemented under OQ-155; refusal remains only where neither estimate can be produced | B4 §Proration + PO 2026-08-12 (OQ-155 · OQ-191) | CONFIRMED; split-by-days CONFIRMED 2026-08-12 — **the 35.56 figure is HUMAN-REVIEW-PENDING (the PO was shown 35.55)** | GT-3 · WE-10 · B4.2/B4.3/B4.4 ❄️ |
| BR-RAT-005 | Tax base = usage + base charge only (fees/late never taxed) | B4 §Tax | CONFIRMED | GT-10 |
| BR-RAT-006 | Tax exemption is account-level, regardless of service | B4 §Tax | CONFIRMED | GT-11 |
| BR-RAT-007 | Historical invoices reprice against tariff version in force on service date. ⚠ **CONFIRMED AND MADE PRECISE 2026-09-30 (PO ruling) — FOR A CYCLE RUN AND A ONE-TIME BILL THE SERVICE DATE IS THE PERIOD END, NOT THE BILL DATE** (BR-BIL-1055). Put as: *"The period end: $3.25. Customers pay the price that applied while they used the water, and both kinds of bill agree."* PO verbatim: *"this si correct"* (decision log 2026-09-30, *"ONE-TIME BILL: SIX ANSWERS"*, item 6). Worked: $3.25 until Oct 1, $3.50 from Oct 1; period ends Sep 30, bill date Oct 1 → $3.25. A period containing a version boundary still splits per BR-RAT-114. BR-BIL-070's *"the bill date IS the run's service date"* is superseded for pricing | A8 §Versioning | CONFIRMED (schema) | — |
| BR-RAT-008 | TOU/demand measurement + ratchet rules | B4 §TOU | CLOSED — superseded by BR-RAT-021/033 (TOU Option B) + BR-RAT-014 (demand v1, ratchet off per RD-4) | blocked |
| BR-RAT-009 | Minimum-bill interaction with tiers + base | B4 §Base | CLOSED — superseded by BR-RAT-016/028 (both minimum-bill modes, PO-confirmed combination rule) | blocked |
| BR-RAT-010 | Sewer "% of water": volume vs charge basis | A8 open Qs · **REOPENED and answered THE OTHER WAY 2026-09-07, re-signed 2026-09-08** (`CR-081`) | **CLOSED — VOLUME basis.** PO verbatim: *"we ahve to apply this method - Way 2 … a percentage of the gallons"*, and the six ❄️ frozen figures re-signed under HUMAN-GUIDE §6: *"C — factor 90%, rate $5.00 → $36.00, which is what a real sewer customer actually pays"*. ⚠ It read **CLOSED — answered by BR-RAT-015 (PO R1 Q8: % of water usage-charge dollars)** from 2026-08-12 until then; the supersession is STATED, never assumed. | — |
| BR-RAT-011 | Meter rollover: consumption = (max−prior)+current | B4 §Consumption | CONFIRMED (PO R7, 2026-08-08 — rollover confirmed, B4 R7 addendum) | GT-4* |

> Note (2026-08-08): BR-RAT-008/009/010/011 were superseded by PO closures — TOU → BR-RAT-021 (demand measurement still PROPOSED per B4 addendum), minimum bill → BR-RAT-028, sewer → BR-RAT-015, rollover → CONFIRMED per B4 R7 (GT-14). Rows kept for historical reference; cite the successors.

## PO Q&A round 1 (2026-08-08) — Plans & Rates
| ID | Rule | Source | Status | Test |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| BR-RAT-012 | Base/fixed charge is defined **per service within a plan**; multi-service plans sum their service bases | PO Q&A R1 (Q2) | **SUPERSEDED (PO ruling 2026-08-20 → BR-RAT-088: the CHARGE CODE owns the base amount; a plan displays it, never defines it)** · was CONFIRMED | ⚠ **AND THE CODE STOPPED IMPLEMENTING IT ON 2026-08-31 (`DEF-2823`), WHICH IS ELEVEN DAYS AFTER THE SUPERSESSION REACHED THIS TABLE.** `apps/billing/gather._base_charge_for` went on reading `plan_services.base_charge` as the SOURCE the whole time, and its own docstring cited this rule by number to justify it — so a lane reading the code learned the superseded rule and a lane reading this table learned the ruling. The live path now asks the `is_base` CHARGE CODE first (`rating.selectors.base_charge_from_charge_code`) and — **until 2026-09-28** — REFUSED a plan figure that disagreed with it, naming both amounts. **Measured before switching: all 47 ready accounts on the seeded tenant state the SAME figure on both objects, so no bill moved** — `10-scripts/def2799_live_base_charges.py` diffs empty account by account, `$8.00` on `AC-2026-00001` and `$390.00` across the run. ⚠⚠ **AND THAT MEASUREMENT IS EXACTLY WHY THE REFUSAL WAS WRONG — `DEF-BIL-7170`, critical, PO ruling 2026-09-28.** *"All 47 state the same figure"* means the refusal branch **was never exercised by a real account**: it was unreachable on the data it was proved against, which is hard-won law 7's *incapable of failing* one step removed. Any tenant whose migration left a DIFFERENT figure had every bill for that service stopped, and was told to clear a value on an object the product itself says does not define it. **PO, verbatim:** *"we ahve to take ot from Charge code - whya re you confuse about it - charge code binded in plan - unitl it is part of plan we cannot take amount from charge code - we will never take default value from plan $8.0 - that is retired"*. **THE REFUSES HALF IS SUPERSEDED; THE DISPLAY HALF STANDS.** `gather._base_charge_for` takes ONE input from that day — the bound charge code's amount — and where no bound code answers it refuses rather than falling back. The plan's column is still written and still shown, and whether it should stop being written and shown is explicitly NOT RULED. |
| BR-RAT-013 | Rate schedules support a **usage allowance included in the base** ("first X units"); tiers price beyond the allowance | PO Q&A R1 (Q5 — interpretation, explicit confirm pending) | SUPERSEDED by BR-RAT-026 (PO R5 Q5 — pure tiers, no allowance) | — |
| BR-RAT-014 | **Demand billing is in scope for v1** (measurement = max-demand register read, ratchet off by default per B4 addendum, still PROPOSED) | PO Q&A R1 (Q7) | CONFIRMED (scope) | — |
| BR-RAT-015 | ❄️ **SUPERSEDED 2026-09-08 by `CR-081` / BR-RAT-1015.** Read: *"Sewer = **% of water usage-charge dollars** (excl. base, excl. tax); cap/winter-averaging deferred v1.1"* ⚠ **2026-09-30: the PO's ruling that sewer has NO SERVICE POINT of its own is recorded on `BR-RAT-1015`, the live row; this row stays SUPERSEDED.** | PO Q&A R1 (Q8 "yes" to proposal); superseded by PO ruling 2026-09-07 + HUMAN-GUIDE §6 finance re-sign 2026-09-08 | **SUPERSEDED** — cite `BR-RAT-1015`. The row is kept because six ❄️ frozen figures and a dozen docstrings cite it by name and a reader meeting `$15.80` needs to be able to date it. | — |
| **BR-RAT-1015** | ⚠⚠ **AMENDED 2026-09-30 (PO ruling, option B) — SEWER HAS NO SERVICE POINT OF ITS OWN; IT IS BILLED WHENEVER ITS BASIS SERVICE (WATER) IS BILLED.** PO verbatim: *"for % of service there will not be any service point"*, then choosing (B) *"No service point for % of services · Sewer is taken whenever its basis service (water) is on the bill"* over (A) keeping a sewer point with no meter and no asset. **It supersedes our 2026-09-07 reading** that sewer *"takes a service point with no meter and no asset"* — a misreading of the PO's *"this % of service"*, recorded as corrected rather than as a reversal. **The arithmetic below is untouched:** 90% of the water quantity at sewer's own price, water 8 kgal → 7.2 kgal → **`$36.00`**. **Why it matters in money, as shown to the PO:** today a plan service with no service point is billed `$0.00` *not connected* (`BR-BIL-301`) with no exception for a `%` service, so a unit with no sewer point loses its whole sewer charge — **`$76.50` a month** on `BR-BIL-1048`'s measured account. Billing's side is `BR-BIL-300` and `BR-BIL-1048`, amended the same day. **NOT BUILT — PO: *"don't fix anything yet"*.** **Sewer = a percentage of the water QUANTITY, priced on sewer's OWN rate schedule.** The percentage is a **RETURN-TO-SEWER FACTOR** — the share of metered water that physically reaches the sewer (79–95% across North American municipal utilities, clustering on **90%**) — and **not** the price ratio BR-RAT-015's 40% was. The sewer line carries a QUANTITY and the BASIS SERVICE'S UNIT (a utility metering water in `ccf` bills sewer in `ccf`) and sewer's own price per that unit. Seeded default **90% @ $5.0000/kgal**; **FLAT, not tiered** — 66 of 88 municipal wastewater utilities surveyed bill a uniform rate, and inclining tiers exist to curb *outdoor* use, which is exactly the water that never reaches the sewer. **Worked, ❄️ re-signed:** water 8 kgal → sewer 7.2 kgal → **$36.00** (was $15.80). ⚠ **THE IRRIGATION ALLOWANCE IS NOT MADE BY LOWERING THE FACTOR** — the industry's answer is **winter-quarter averaging** (B4's own *"winter-averaging v1.1"*, still deferred and still not built); lowering 90% to allow for gardens would under-bill every apartment and every commercial account that waters nothing. **The road not taken is recorded so nobody later "fixes" the 90 down.** | `CR-081`; PO ruling 2026-09-07 + HUMAN-GUIDE §6 finance re-sign 2026-09-08 (decision log, *"THE PO RE-SIGNS SIX FROZEN SEWER FIGURES"*) | **CONFIRMED (2026-09-30) · the no-service-point amendment is NOT BUILT** — the formula itself: **CONFIRMED** | — |
| BR-RAT-016 | **Minimum bill is v1**; placement per-service proposed (consistent with per-service base) | PO Q&A R1 (Q9) | CONFIRMED (in-scope) / placement PROPOSED | GT-13 |
| BR-RAT-017 | Tariff versions stay reproducible for **7 years** (rebill/audit) | PO Q&A R1 (Q12) | CONFIRMED | — |
| BR-RAT-018 | **Solar & Battery bill as capacity charges in v1**: tenant-set rate per capacity unit × account-level capacity (ex: solar $10/unit, battery $12/unit). NOT net-metering kWh credits — export-credit math deferred to v1.1 (net-meter read capture continues per assets module) | PO Q&A R1 (Q10) | CONFIRMED (capacity-unit definition = OQ-051) | — |
| BR-RAT-019 | New tariff model **`capacity`** (fixed rate × account capacity qty) added to flat/tiered/TOU/demand/seasonal | derived from BR-RAT-018 | CONFIRMED (Lane C r5 approved by PO, 2026-08-08 — B2 r5 items 4/16) | — |
| BR-RAT-020 | ⚖️ **SUPERSEDED IN PART 2026-10-03 (`OQ-RAT-12786` CLOSED, PO verbatim: *"(a) Class or size: the deposit table gets a size column, like charge codes have."*): a deposit is a fixed $ amount per consumer category OR per meter size** — the same choice a charge code has; a sized deposit code is asked at the connection size of the service the code names (worked: 5/8" **$150.00**, 2" **$600.00**; by class Residential **$150.00**, Commercial **$300.00** unchanged). Built `CR-167-BRC10` §15. **AMENDED 2026-10-04 — where NO size is recorded (no meter fitted, or no connection for the code's service), the clerk picks the size on the Deposit step** (PO verbatim *"(c) Let the clerk pick the size on the Deposit step."*, `DEF-CUS-9521`, built `2.492.0`, `CR-167-BRC10` §21): the plan's priced sizes are offered, the deposit follows the pick (no meter, 2" → **$600.00**), the account keeps the pick and the first bill reads it; a recorded size always wins. *Was:* **Deposits v1: fixed $ amounts per consumer category** (Res/Com/Ind/Gov). Still OUT of v1: 2×-average-bill, per-service deposit amounts, credit-tier waivers | PO Q&A R1 (D6) · PO 2026-10-03 | CONFIRMED-AS-AMENDED 2026-10-03 (by size added; schedule placement is the plan, `BR-RAT-023`) | — |

## PO Q&A round 3 (2026-08-08)
| ID | Rule | Source | Status | Test |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| BR-RAT-021 | **TOU v1 = Option B**: up to 3 windows (on/mid/off) · 2 seasons with date ranges + per-season prices · weekday/weekend/holiday day-types (tenant holiday calendar; holidays bill off-peak) · reads arrive pre-split per window · optional interval-CSV import aggregated to windows. ⚙️ **2026-10-01: a period that CROSSES the season boundary now prices each window's register pro-rata by calendar days at each season's price (`BR-RAT-034`, `OQ-234` closed 2026-09-07) — WE-18 usage $82.72; before that date the whole period priced at the service-date season** | PO Q&A R3 (Q6) | CONFIRMED | `tests/test_us_rat_012_tou.py` · `tests/golden/test_gt34_season_crossing_period.py` |
| BR-RAT-022 | Capacity charges (solar/battery): **unit label + rate are tenant-defined** per service (e.g., "panel", "kW block") · **prorated calendar-days** on mid-cycle start per B4.3 · taxability = residual (default proposal: taxable like base) | PO Q&A R3 (OQ-051) | CONFIRMED (taxable pending) | GT-15 |
| BR-RAT-023 | ⚖️ **AMENDED 2026-10-03 (`CR-167-BRC10` ruling 9, task 3): the plan still names its deposit, and now NAMES IT AS A CHARGE CODE** — a `refundable` code on the plan version's Deposits table (a `plan_charge_rules` row), its amount read from the code's price by CUSTOMER CLASS (PO 2026-09-08 *"a plan names charge codes; the charge code carries the money"*; 2026-09-23 *"plan will have deposite chanrge code as per customer categoris"*). **By size is BUILT since 2026-10-03 (`OQ-RAT-12786` CLOSED (a), `BR-RAT-020` superseded in part, `CR-167-BRC10` §15): a sized deposit code is asked at the connection size of the service it names. *(Read "By size is NOT built" until then.)* A plan that names no deposit code keeps reading its typed `plan_deposit_rules` rows exactly as before — not a cent moves (no seeded tenant holds one; measured). Plan versions now have dates (`OQ-324` CLOSED (B), `BR-RAT-1066`), so the deposit is the one named by the version in force on the day. **Deposit schedule is PLAN-level**: `plan_deposit_rules` (consumer category → fixed $) — supersedes the tenant-level proposal. ⚠ **BUILT END TO END ON 2026-08-28 (`DEF-202`, lane RATING-WRITE-PATHS) — AND THE STORAGE, THE VALIDATION AND THE ENDPOINT HAD ALL EXISTED FOR WEEKS WHILE THE SCREEN POSTED NOTHING.** PLN-03 step 6 drew a stated absence whose stated reason was *"the category list is set up in the Accounts module, which is not built yet"* — false the whole time: the consumer category IS the account type (`billing/release.py` maps `consumer_category` to `account__account_type__code`), the wizard already received that list for step 1's customer-class picker, `PLAN_FIELDS` already accepted `deposit_rules`, and `PLAN_DEPOSIT_RULE_FIELDS` was already `("account_type_id", "amount", "currency")`. `DEF-445` corrected the sentence to *"not built on this screen"* — true, and still an absence — and the PO's report stood through both: *"Deposite unable to add"*. **Both versions passed their test, because a `toContain` over prose can only ever check that the prose is still there.** ⚠ **A BLANK BOX WRITES NO ROW AND NEVER A `0.00`:** `selectors.deposit_rule_for` answers `None` for *this plan asks no deposit of this category* and the caller then emits NO deposit line, where a stored zero says a deposit was assessed and waived — two different facts, and emptying the box is the only way a utility can stop asking. ⚠ **NO PER-DEPOSIT CURRENCY CONTROL, AND THE ABSENCE IS THE RULE:** the server defaults the column and CLAUDE.md hard rule 2 makes it `CHAR(3)`; a picker would let one plan hold deposits in two currencies, which no rule permits and no bill could add up. | PO Q&A R3 · `DEF-202` · `DEF-445` | CONFIRMED · **BUILT 2026-08-28** | `PlanWizard` step 6 · `plan_deposit_rules` · `07-frontend/src/features/rating/PlanWizard.test.tsx` |
| BR-RAT-024 | ⚠ **AMENDED 2026-09-02 (`CR-063`): THE CATALOGUE IS **ELEVEN** SERVICES, NOT TEN — `fire` IS APPENDED AT POSITION 11 AND NOTHING IS RENUMBERED.** PO verbatim, 2026-09-02: _"We will need Fire as utility Service - Capacity based - this will behave exactly like trash - we will ahve to define sizes in settings"_, and the priority half: _"we have to do it ; curenlty we need it for one of the customer we want to go live"_ — **a real utility is being onboarded and it sells fire protection, so this is a product path and not demo data.** **Fire's arithmetic is TRASH's and not SOLAR's**, which is what _"exactly like trash"_ settles: the size class PICKS the price and the COUNT multiplies it (`BR-RAT-090`, unchanged), where solar, battery, storm water and broadband SUM their installed capacity. **Worked, and the figures are asserted:** one 2" fire line bills **$12.00**; one 6" line bills **$60.00**; one 6" line with two 2" lines bills `$60.00 + 2 × $12.00` = **$84.00**, on two lines. ⚠ **LAYOUT SUPERSEDED 2026-09-30, AMOUNT UNCHANGED** — PO ruling 2026-09-30, *"per service"* (decision log *"ONE-TIME BILL: SIX ANSWERS"* (5)): a counted service prints ONE line per service, count and sizes in its quantity/description, so this becomes **one fire line of `$84.00`**, not two. Re-checked on the code 2026-09-30 under base-per-asset: still **`$84.00`** (`tests/test_cr063_c3_fire_priced.py`, fire carries no base — decision log 2026-09-30 *"FIRE'S `$84.00` DOES NOT MOVE"*). **The one-line layout is BUILT** (2.411.3, `DEF-BIL-8151`, `apps/billing/counted_service_line.py`): the bill carries ONE fire line of `$84.00`, quantity 3 connections, its wording naming both sizes, and `tc2b` asserts one (it asserted two until 2026-09-30). ⚠ **AMENDED 2026-09-03 (`CR-063` C3/C5 SUPERSEDED IN PART) — PO verbatim: _"remove private hydrants from list"_.** `HYD` _"Private hydrant"_ at **$25.0000** has LEFT fire's size list — a hydrant is an ASSET, not a utility-service size — so fire is **SIX** bands and the second worked figure moved: **the warehouse with a 6" line and two hydrants was `$110.00` and now bills `$60.00`, `−$50.00` a month on that customer.** It supersedes a figure put to the PO in plain English and approved on 2026-09-02, and it is a deliberate reversal rather than a correction. **THE SIX DIAMETERS AND THEIR PRICES DO NOT MOVE** — a 2" line still bills **$12.00** — and fire still COUNTS rather than SUMS. Fire's unit is **`connection`** (originally because it covered a line and a hydrant equally, where `line` would not; it does not move now the hydrant is gone, because a fire line IS a service connection and `BR-TEN-072` forbids renaming a seeded code) and its revenue account is **`4155` Fire**, which was free and sits correctly in the ladder between `4150` Capacity and `4160` Storm water. ⚠ **THE SIX SIZES AND THEIR PRICES ARE OURS, NOT THE CUSTOMER'S, AND ARE SEEDED DEFAULTS A TENANT EDITS ON SETTINGS — NEVER SIGNED LAW.** (The 2026-09-03 ruling removed a row; it did NOT ratify the six that remain or any amount beside them, so `OQ-1360` stays OPEN.) The going-live customer's real list is a fact only they hold and is `OQ-1360`, OPEN; the build does not wait on it, because a seeded default a tenant overwrites is exactly what a seed is for. **NO ❄️ FROZEN B4/B5 FIGURE MOVES** — Fire is new money on new lines, and `GT-17`'s water **$39.50** and sewer **$15.80** are asserted untouched. ⚠ **THE FRONT END TYPES THIS LIST OUT BY HAND IN TWO PLACES** (`SignupWizard.tsx`'s tick-list, `ratingShared.tsx`'s badge map), **so an eleventh service does NOT reach a screen on its own and the failure is SILENT** — hard-won law 2; `SignupWizard.test.tsx` now asserts eleven. **Service catalog v1 = ~~10~~ ELEVEN services**: Water, Electric, Gas, Sewer, **Trash** (Waste merged in), Storm water, Solar, Broadband, Thermal/Heat, Battery, **Fire** — platform catalog + per-tenant enablement. ⚠ **AMENDED 2026-08-21 (PO ruling): ON PLN-03 STEP 2 A SERVICE THE TENANT HAS NOT ENABLED IS NOT DRAWN AT ALL.** PO verbatim: _"Second page - only services that are selected for this utility - you are showing all and UI does not match"_. The ten-service catalogue and per-tenant enablement above are UNCHANGED; what is reversed is the rule's step-2 clause in `EP-RAT-01-plans.md`, which read _"disabled services render disabled, never hidden"_ and is now **SUPERSEDED**. **Worked:** Silver Creek Water enables water, sewer and trash, so step 2 draws **3** tiles where it drew **10**. **Scope is PLN-03 step 2 only** — `BR-MET-003` (premises/unit service chips) and `BR-SHL-025` (role-gated actions) keep disabled-never-hidden untouched, because a signature covers only what was shown. The CANONICAL row for this ruling is **`BR-RAT-095`** below (*a service this utility does not sell is hidden, not disabled*); this stamp exists so a reader who arrives at BR-RAT-024 first is not left following a superseded clause. The reason the old half existed is on the `EP-RAT-01` row. | PO Q&A R3 (OQ-052 + D4) · **PO ruling 2026-08-21** (decision log) | **CONFIRMED-AS-AMENDED (2026-08-21)** | `PlanWizard.test.tsx::US-RAT-002 TC-4 / BR-RAT-024` (amended) |
| BR-RAT-025 | **D1–D9 ratified as written**: rate structure not service-tied · one schedule per service per plan, seasonality inside · seasonal first-class model · plan-as-bundle (deposit/charge/penalty/tax rules plan-scoped, tenant defaults as fallback) · category targeting via plans · charge codes = global catalog + plan attachment with amounts · tax rules via plan + account-level exemption · versioned rate changes with history/audit/compliance log + single AND bulk updates | PO Q&A R3 (blanket) | CONFIRMED | — |

| BR-RAT-027 | Base rate: assigned **per service**; the plan's total base is **derived** (sum of service bases) and displayed at plan level | PO Q&A R5 (Q9) — extends BR-RAT-012 | **SUPERSEDED (PO ruling 2026-08-20 → BR-RAT-088: the CHARGE CODE owns the base amount; a plan displays it, never defines it)** · was CONFIRMED | ⚠ **AND THE CODE STOPPED IMPLEMENTING IT ON 2026-08-31 (`DEF-2823`), WHICH IS ELEVEN DAYS AFTER THE SUPERSESSION REACHED THIS TABLE.** `apps/billing/gather._base_charge_for` went on reading `plan_services.base_charge` as the SOURCE the whole time, and its own docstring cited this rule by number to justify it — so a lane reading the code learned the superseded rule and a lane reading this table learned the ruling. The live path now asks the `is_base` CHARGE CODE first (`rating.selectors.base_charge_from_charge_code`) and — **until 2026-09-28** — REFUSED a plan figure that disagreed with it, naming both amounts. **Measured before switching: all 47 ready accounts on the seeded tenant state the SAME figure on both objects, so no bill moved** — `10-scripts/def2799_live_base_charges.py` diffs empty account by account, `$8.00` on `AC-2026-00001` and `$390.00` across the run. ⚠⚠ **AND THAT MEASUREMENT IS EXACTLY WHY THE REFUSAL WAS WRONG — `DEF-BIL-7170`, critical, PO ruling 2026-09-28.** *"All 47 state the same figure"* means the refusal branch **was never exercised by a real account**: it was unreachable on the data it was proved against, which is hard-won law 7's *incapable of failing* one step removed. Any tenant whose migration left a DIFFERENT figure had every bill for that service stopped, and was told to clear a value on an object the product itself says does not define it. **PO, verbatim:** *"we ahve to take ot from Charge code - whya re you confuse about it - charge code binded in plan - unitl it is part of plan we cannot take amount from charge code - we will never take default value from plan $8.0 - that is retired"*. **THE REFUSES HALF IS SUPERSEDED; THE DISPLAY HALF STANDS.** `gather._base_charge_for` takes ONE input from that day — the bound charge code's amount — and where no bound code answers it refuses rather than falling back. The plan's column is still written and still shown, and whether it should stop being written and shown is explicitly NOT RULED. |
| BR-RAT-028 | Minimum bill: **both modes built** — (A) per-service minimum and (B) plan-level minimum floor; plan config chooses either or both. Combination rule when both set: per-service minimums apply first, then the plan floor tops up the total. ⚠ **AMENDED 2026-09-10 (PO ruling) — MODE (A)'s SOURCE MOVES ONTO THE RATE SCHEDULE. THE ARITHMETIC DOES NOT MOVE AND THIS IS NOT A RE-SIGN.** PO verbatim: _"Even if per service rate schedule has it per service - base rate, Rates and Minim rates - you just need to attache dit ti plan, lets not override that at plan level again. it complciated for user"_, then _"set it in rates and use it in plans - thats it - this is rule - when you do the final review on plan you show all values - coming from rate schedule; and when we view plan details you show the details again coming from rate schedule"_. **THE RULE: one place to SET, everywhere else SHOWS.** Mode (A) was `plan_services.minimum_amount`; it is now **`tariff_versions.minimum_bill`**, the column the rate schedule wizard has always written and that `rating.selectors.pricing_inputs` has always published. ⚠ **A RE-SIGN WAS ARGUED FOR AND WITHDRAWN, AND THE REASONING IS THE PART THAT GENERALISES.** It was put to the PO that removing mode (A) leaves this row's combination clause with nothing to combine. **The PO's reading is better and is the one recorded: the per-service minimum still EXISTS and still applies FIRST; it is READ FROM THE SCHEDULE instead of from the plan.** `GT-13` (_usage $6.10 + base $12.00 = $18.10 < minimum $25.00 → adjustment $6.90; tax on $25.00_) and `GT-19`/`WE-3` (_1.60 + 8.00 = 9.60 < 15.00 → +5.40, total **$16.42**_) were both re-read before anything moved: **neither states a SOURCE**, both state the arithmetic, and the arithmetic is untouched. So this is an amendment naming the new source, not a finance re-sign (HUMAN-GUIDE §6 not entered). ⚠ **AND IT CLOSES `DEF-2446` RATHER THAN LEAVING IT OPEN.** `apps/billing/gather._tariff_config_for` deliberately did not read the schedule's figure because _"nothing signed says what a schedule that ALSO carries one means"_ — which was `OQ-238`'s shape one rule along. **This ruling is that missing signature, and it removes the ambiguity instead of resolving it:** there is no longer a plan column to disagree with, so no precedence exists to get wrong. ⚠ **THE MIGRATION IS THE SAFETY STEP AND IT IS NOT OPTIONAL.** On the shipped fixture the figure existed ONLY on the plan — `RES-STD` water stated `$15.00` and its schedule stated none — so `rating/0030` copies every stated figure onto **every version** of the attached tariff (the plan's column was version-independent; the schedule's is not, so a back-dated re-bill would otherwise lose the floor) and `rating/0031` then removes `plan_services.minimum_amount`. **Drop it without moving it and that plan under-bills by `$5.40` a month on `WE-3`'s own case, with every check green.** **Mode (B) is untouched:** `plans.minimum_bill_amount` is a floor on the WHOLE BILL, which no schedule could carry, so it stays the plan's — recorded rather than re-asked. ⚠ **AMENDED AGAIN 2026-10-01 (PO ruling, option A) — MODE (A) IS SET PER METER SIZE, AND `tariff_versions.minimum_bill` IS NO LONGER BILLED** (`DEF-RAT-8737`, `CR-157-BRC10`). PO verbatim, quoting the question put: _"The question: should a service's minimum charge be set per meter size or once for the whole schedule? Or should rate schedules have no minimum at all? A · Minimum per size - this is correct"_. **THE RULE:** for a connection of size S, mode (A)'s minimum is the rate schedule's minimum for S (`rate_size_rows.minimum_bill`, step 2's base table); a size stating _No minimum_, a size with no row, and a connection with no recorded size are **not topped up**; there is **no fallback** to the version's figure. Worked, Water — Residential at 1 kgal: 5/8" `$8.00 + 1 × $4.4096 = $12.41` under a 5/8" minimum of `$15.00` → **`$15.00`**; 2" `$28.00 + $4.4096 = $32.41` under `$40.00` → **`$40.00`**; a size with no minimum stays **`$12.41`**. The per-version _Minimum rate on this version_ is removed: the API refuses a stated figure by name, and `rating/0053` copied every stated version figure onto that version's size rows that had none (a size that stated its own keeps it), then cleared the version's; a version with **no size rows** keeps its figure, unbilled, as the clerk's list. **Unchanged:** the arithmetic, the order (mode A first, then mode B), mode (B) `plans.minimum_bill_amount`, the `OQ-1166` base-only exemption, and proration (the stage applies whatever `config.minimum_amount` arrives, as before). **No ❄️ figure moves:** `GT-19`/`WE-3` (`$16.42`) and `GT-13` state arithmetic and no source; the seeded `$15.00` moves onto every water size row. Judgment recorded in the decision log the same day: on a capacity unit with several sizes the floor is the first size group's (the group that already carries the service minimum). | PO Q&A R5+R6 (D2 OK) · **AMENDED by PO ruling 2026-10-01** (decision log, _"RATE SCHEDULES: THE MINIMUM IS SET PER METER SIZE AND BILLING CHARGES IT"_, option A; `DEF-RAT-8737`) · **AMENDED by PO ruling 2026-09-10** (decision log, _"A RATE SCHEDULE IS THE ONE PLACE A SERVICE'S FIGURES ARE SET; A PLAN SHOWS THEM"_ and _"THE PER-SERVICE FIGURES MOVE TO THE SCHEDULE — AND NO BILL MOVES WITH THEM"_) · closes `DEF-2446` | CONFIRMED (amended 2026-10-01) | GT-13 · GT-19/WE-3 · `06-backend/tests/test_def_rat_8737_minimum_per_size.py` ($15.00 / $40.00 / $12.41, base-only, plan floor, migration) · `06-backend/tests/test_cr090_minimum_reads_from_the_schedule.py` (the MONEY at the end of the hop — `$16.42` with the schedule's floor, `$10.51` without) · migrations `rating/0030` + `0031` + `0053` |
| BR-RAT-029 | Capacity charges taxable **like base** — DEFAULT IN EFFECT (asked 3×, no objection); flips to CONFIRMED on PO nod or objection | PO Q&A R6 (D1 OK) | CONFIRMED | GT-15 |

## StorySmith epic pack (2026-08-08) — UI-flow & validation rules (EP-RAT-01…06)
Substance stays in the confirmed rules above; these add the flow/validation layer the screens need. New IDs from the allocated BR-RAT-030..040 range.

| ID | Rule | Source | Status | Test |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| BR-RAT-030 | Plan lifecycle draft → published → archived: only published plans are assignable; drafts never billed; archiving blocks new assignment, keeps history and existing accounts. **A PLAN THAT HAS BEEN BILLED CANNOT BE HARD-DELETED — and as of 2026-08-12 THE GUARD IS LIVE, not merely written.** It was dark from W1 to 2026-08-12 (DEF-079): `apps/rating/services.delete_plan` refuses a hard delete unless *accounts = 0 AND never billed*, and the never-billed half asked `apps.billing.services.plan_invoice_count`, which **billing had never authored** — so the seam returned **0** every time and a billed plan was permanently deletable together with its rate schedules and every charge, deposit, penalty and tax rule. `units.rate_plan_id` is `PROTECT`, so a plan LIVE on a unit was safe; a retired one was not, and invoices snapshot rather than reference a plan, so nothing else refused. **The counter is now published** (`apps/billing/selectors.plan_invoice_count`, re-exported from `apps.billing.services`) and the delete REFUSES; the test is written from the guard's side, not the counter's (`tests/test_def_079_dark_counters.py`) | B2 r5 #18 (enum) + S10 step-6 copy + IS-1; guard wired 2026-08-12 (DEF-079) | CONFIRMED (PO R5-4) — **enforcement LIVE since 2026-08-12** | US-RAT-001/003 TCs · US-RAT-001 TC-5 |
| BR-RAT-031 | Rate schedule: internal name, prices exactly one service with one model; Save-draft resumable; activation needs complete structure + effective_from; "Board approval required" holds the version Awaiting-approval until a System Admin/Executive approves (`tariff_versions.approved_by`) | A8 §Plan-vs-Rate + S11 steps 1/3/4 + OQ-042 supervisor precedent | CONFIRMED-AS-MODIFIED (PO R5-1: **board-approval workflow REMOVED — approval happens outside the system**; rule reduces to save-draft resumable + activation requires complete structure + effective_from; `tariff_versions.approved_by` stays as optional metadata only) · ⚠ **THE DISCOUNT BORROWS THIS RULE'S SHAPE AND PARTS FROM IT ON ONE POINT — `DEF-RAT-5817`, 2026-09-19.** `uq_discount_versions_one_draft` is this rule's partial-unique index on `discount_versions`, and it had no handler at all: the SECOND *Save new version* on any discount arrived as a raw HTTP 500 and the edit door then stayed shut for ever, because nothing listed the blocking draft and nothing could discard it. **It now refuses in words and the way out exists** — `GET /discounts/{id}/versions` serves the chain, `POST …/versions/{id}/discard` removes a draft, and DIS-03's Audit tab draws both. ⚠ **IT REFUSES RATHER THAN REPLACING, WHICH IS WHERE IT DIFFERS FROM `_save_schedule_draft`:** a schedule's draft is the one DATELESS row and carries no number, so overwriting it loses nothing anyone can cite; a discount version is NUMBERED and append-only (`BR-RAT-037`), so overwriting `V2` would destroy a half-finished filing and leave a gap in the history | US-RAT-005 TC-3 |
| BR-RAT-032 | Tier validation: seq-ordered, contiguous (tier n+1 From = tier n To), From < To, only last tier open-ended (To NULL = ∞), rates ≥ 0, NUMERIC(18,6); flat = single 0→∞ tier row. **AMENDED 2026-08-21 (DEF-433): every clause above is applied PER SIZE GROUP, not over the stored list.** `rate_tiers.size_class` NULL means *every size* and is the REQUIRED fallback group; a `size_class` value is an override (r10's 2″ water row prices `3.45 / 5.10 / 7.70` where every other size prices `3.20 / 4.80 / 7.20`). Two ladders read as one list have `from_qty`s `0, 4, 10, 0, 4, 10`, which a single running contiguity check reads as a gap and refuses on a correct rate; and *"only the last tier may be open-ended"* over a flat list means *the last row of the payload*, which leaves every group but the final one unable to have a top block. **A payload of size overrides with no NULL group is REFUSED** (`rating.tiers.size_override_without_base`) — the exception without the rule leaves every unnamed size with no price at all. The stored `seq` stays globally unique per version (`uq_rate_tiers_version_seq` kept verbatim), so a group's position is its own, not its `seq` | S11 tier grid + B2 `rate_tiers` DDL · `rate_tiers.size_class` (migration 0010) · DEF-433 · design §A3 | CONFIRMED (PO R5-5) · **AMENDED 2026-08-21 (DEF-433, per-size groups)** | US-RAT-005 TC-4/5 · `tests/test_wave_e_filing_record.py` |
| BR-RAT-033 | TOU config validation: per (season × day-type) windows cover 00:00–24:00 with no overlap (windows may wrap midnight); ≤3 windows; 2 seasons cover the year without overlap; holidays resolve to the season's off-peak window via the tenant calendar | BR-RAT-021 + prototype TOU table | CONFIRMED (PO R5-6) | US-RAT-006 TC-4..7 |
| BR-RAT-034 | Seasonal model validation: `rate_seasons` ranges cover the year without overlap; each season carries its own price set; season boundaries split the bill period pro-rata by calendar days (B4.3). ⚙️ **BUILT 2026-10-01 (`DEF-RAT-8832`), THREE WEEKS AFTER THE PO SWITCHED IT ON** — *"please split  swithch it on"*, 2026-09-07, closing `OQ-234`. Until then the engine priced a whole period at the season of its service date and the `seasonal` model was not registered. **How it is built:** rating counts the calendar days of the account's served span in each season (`selectors._season_days_between`, beside the one-season lookup) and hands the counts to the engine, which holds no calendar; the TOU and `seasonal` plugins split each measured quantity by them (`pricing/season_split.py`). A share of a quantity is held at 6 decimals with the remainder on the last season, so the shares add back to the read. **Worked (WE-18 = GT-34, engine-verified):** 15 Sep – 14 Oct, 16 summer + 14 winter days; on-peak 120 kWh → 64 × 0.22 = $14.08 + 56 × 0.18 = $10.08; usage **$82.72**, total **$103.67** (one-season pricing gave on-peak `120 × 0.18 = $21.60`). A period inside one season is unchanged. ⚙️ **AMENDED 2026-10-01 — A METER READING ON THE SEASON-CHANGE DAY REPLACES THE DAY SPLIT (`DEF-RAT-8849`; PO verbatim *"yes build the season-change day reading"*; WE-18's own *"an ACTUAL read dated Oct 1 would replace the split"*). BUILT the same day.** Where an ACTUAL reading is dated on the FIRST DAY OF THE NEW SEASON inside the period, each register's usage before and after it is priced at its own season's rates instead of by days. **Worked:** 120 kWh on-peak, 15 Sep – 14 Oct, season changes 1 Oct, reading on 1 Oct shows 80 before and 40 after → `80 × 0.22 = $17.60` + `40 × 0.18 = $7.20` = **$24.80** (the day split, 64/56, is $24.16). The whole WE-18 meter cut at 80/110/210 before and 40/90/170 after bills usage **$83.50**, total **$104.53**. **Rules of the cut** (decision log *"…SEASON-CHANGE DAY READING BUILT"*): an estimate on that day does not cut (no dial value, BR-MET-028); several actual readings that day → the last; the later season is `(closing dial − day's dial) ×` the factor the closing reading used and the first season is the remainder of the bill's own quantity; anything that makes the subtraction a fiction (no factor, an estimated closing reading, another meter, a part below zero, a TOU window the day's reading did not cover) leaves the day split. The engine still holds no calendar: billing `gather` makes the cut from metering's `period_registers` and hands per-season quantities (`Quantity.season_parts`). On a `seasonal` TIERED ladder, each season's block bounds scale by that season's share of the period — BR-RAT-002 applied per share; a lane judgment with no signed figure, flagged in the review ledger | B2 r5 #2 (D3) + B4.3 · PO 2026-09-07 | CONFIRMED (PO R5-7) · **BUILT 2026-10-01** · **AMENDED + BUILT 2026-10-01 (season-change-day reading, PO)** | US-RAT-007 TC-7 · `tests/golden/test_gt34_season_crossing_period.py` · `tests/test_oq234_season_split.py` · `tests/test_def_rat_8849_season_change_read.py` |
| BR-RAT-035 | Charge-code identity: code unique per tenant; service scope Any or one service; IS-1 deactivate-first (audit-lock while referenced by plans/invoice lines); prototype amounts (NSF $30, reconnect $45) are seeds never constants. **THE AUDIT LOCK OVER ISSUED INVOICE LINES IS LIVE AS OF 2026-08-12 — before that it never engaged.** Same defect as BR-RAT-030's (DEF-079): the lock asked billing how many issued lines named the code, billing published no such counter, the seam answered **0**, and **a charge code appearing on real bills read as never used and stayed freely editable**. `apps/billing/selectors.charge_line_count` is now published and re-exported, so a code on issued lines is audit-locked and deactivate-first is the only way out | B2 `charges` UNIQUE + S12 + B4 §NSF (CONFIRMED "samples only"); guard wired 2026-08-12 (DEF-079) | CONFIRMED (PO R5-8) — **enforcement LIVE since 2026-08-12** | US-RAT-008 TC-5/7 · `tests/test_def_079_dark_counters.py` |
| BR-RAT-036 | **AMENDED 2026-08-21 — FOUR FORMS, NOT THREE (PO ruling, option C, closes DEF-386).** PO verbatim: _"(C) Per-meter now (it's a small change), stacking later — because that one needs care to avoid a tax that taxes itself in a circle."_ Tax forms: percent-of-subtotal (rate × B4.2 base) · flat-per-bill · per-unit (rate × billed usage qty) · **per-meter (amount × the COUNT of meters at the premises)**. **Worked, ratified:** a `$1.25` state regulatory fee on a duplex with two meters is **`$2.50`**; before the column existed it billed **`$0.00`**, because CR-013 part 4 named the form and nothing could store it. ⚠ **PER-METER TAKES NO UNIT OF MEASURE, AND THAT ASYMMETRY IS THE RULE.** `per_unit` prices billed USAGE — a measured quantity, so hard rule 3 makes its unit mandatory. `per_meter` prices a COUNT, and a meter is not a measure of anything; a UoM box would invite *"meters"* as a unit and the next reader would multiply a fee by kilowatt-hours. It is the same distinction BR-RAT-090 draws for an unmetered service's cart count. ⚠ **THE COMPOUNDING HALF IS DEFERRED, NOT DELIVERED (DEF-381, still OPEN).** A franchise fee taxed by a sales tax cannot be expressed and deliberately still cannot: compounding needs a cycle guard, because A taxing B while B taxes A is a non-terminating bill and no gate in this repo would catch one. A test asserts the absence of `compounds_on` so a later reader cannot mistake the fourth FORM for a fourth BASE. Taxable-base selectors offer ONLY usage + base — the prototype's "Fees"/"tax-on-tax" checkboxes are removed (B4.2 wins: fees/late never taxed; no tax compounding) ⚠ **THE PER-UNIT FORM IS BUILT AND PRICING AS OF 2026-08-31 (`CR-052` W5 task 16, `v2.95.0`).** The ratification status of this rule is UNCHANGED — percent CONFIRMED, flat and per-unit PROPOSED — because building a form is not signing it; what changed is that the third form now produces a line instead of nothing. **The threshold is read as `BR-RAT-042` rules it: MARGINAL.** Worked, run and asserted: a Water Conservation Surcharge of `$0.25 / kgal` above `15` on a meter reading `18.72 kgal` is taxed on `3.72 kgal` and bills **`$0.93`**; read as a whole-bill levy the same rule bills **`$4.68`**, which is the customer paying a conservation surcharge on the first fifteen thousand gallons the rule exempts. `per_unit_uom` is MANDATORY and is CHECKED against the unit the usage was measured in — a rule levied per `kWh` against a meter read in `kgal` REFUSES and names both, because this engine holds no conversion factor (`BR-RAT-038`). **Whether the threshold PRORATES on a part period is unsigned and is `DEF-2702`**: built unscaled, which under-charges rather than over-charges. **PER-METER is still not priced anywhere** and the compounding half is still `DEF-381`. | B4.2 (percent base) + S13 step 2 (forms) | percent CONFIRMED / flat & per-unit PROPOSED | US-RAT-009 TC-3, GT-10 |
| BR-RAT-037 | ⚖️ **NARROWED 2026-10-03 by PO ruling 1 of the plans redesign (*"Name and details (same version)"*, `CR-167-BRC10`; coordinator-agreed §7.5 B2) FOR SIX PLAN FIELDS ONLY → `BR-RAT-1067`:** a live plan's name, code, description, customer classes, intended-for ticks and notes are saved on the SAME version with a before → after Audit line, because none of them prices a bill. **Every other change to an in-force plan is still a new version (now DATED — `OQ-324` CLOSED (B), `BR-RAT-1066`) or a correction, exactly as below.** Worked: renaming *Residential City Plan* keeps it v1; its minimum bill $10.00 → $12.00 from 01/01/2027 makes v2. In-force plan/tariff versions are never edited in place: every change = new version row (windows non-overlapping per B2 EXCLUDE) + append-only `rate_change_log` row with before/after jsonb, actor, and required reason | B2 DDL + r5 #14 + BR-RAT-007/017 | CONFIRMED (PO R5-9) | US-RAT-003/010 TCs |
| BR-RAT-038 | UoM gate: read/register unit must match the billed unit or a per-service conversion factor must be defined; mismatch with no factor → clear user message + rating blocked for that read (no silent conversion); default billed water unit kgal. ⚖️ **EXTENDED TO EVERY METERED SERVICE AND TO THE SAVE SCREEN BY PO RULING 2026-09-02** — verbatim: _"for Gas unit on rate schedule and unit on meter read should match otherwise it is a problem ; same principle is applied to all meter services and hence we have unit and multiplier factor"_. It is `BR-MET-143` (the READING side, PO 2026-08-27) turned round onto the PRICE side, and it is CLAUDE.md hard rule 3 applied to the other factor in the product: rule 3 forbids an assumed unit on a QUANTITY, and this forbids one on the PRICE that quantity is multiplied by. **THREE THINGS THIS ROW NOW STATES THAT IT DID NOT (`DEF-3108`, 2026-09-02).** **(1) WHICH UNIT THE SCHEDULE IS COMPARED AGAINST.** It is `tenant_services.billed_uom` **or, where that is NULL, the register's own `primary_uom`** — because NULL MEANS _the register's own unit is what is billed_ (`tenancy.selectors.service_billed_uoms` says so in those words) and NOT _unconfigured_. `rating.selectors.uom_gate` read `billed_uom` alone until 2026-09-02, and **nothing seeds that column**, so on every tenant this product has ever provisioned the gate answered `satisfied` over every mismatch there was. **Worked:** the billing workbook signs _"Gas flat $1.15/ccf"_ while `SERVICE_DEFAULT_UOMS["gas"]` plants a register reading **therm**; a `ccf` schedule over that register returned `{satisfied: True, reason: 'unconfigured'}` and the wizard drew no warning. One therm of pipeline gas is about `0.9643 ccf`, so **42** units priced at `$1.15` bill **`$48.30`** where the honest figure is about **`$46.58`** — roughly **4% over, `$1.72` a month on one meter**. ⚠ **THE EXAMPLE IS HISTORY FROM 2026-09-02 AND THE RULE IS NOT (stamped 2026-09-07, `DEF-MET-4502`):** `SERVICE_DEFAULT_UOMS["gas"]` plants a register reading **`ccf`** now, not `therm` — `BR-TEN-1180` swapped the pair precisely so the signed `$1.15/ccf` could be applied as signed, and it was seeded on 2026-09-07 (`DEF-RAT-4516`). So the seeded gas tenant no longer illustrates this gate; the gate itself is unchanged and still fires the moment a utility types a `ccf` tariff over a `therm` register by hand. **(2) THAT A CONVERSION FACTOR CANNOT CROSS A DIMENSION.** A factor is a SCALE. `therm` is declared ENERGY and `ccf` VOLUME (`metering.register_units.DIMENSION_OF`), so **no factor reconciles them and none ever can** — the conversion needs a heating value, which is a physical fact about the gas and not a column. The gate's new `dimension_mismatch` verdict is NOT satisfied even with a factor on file, and the scale-versus-dimension answer is metering's single implementation reached through `rating.seams.unit_reconciliation` — rating does not re-decide it. **(3) THAT THE GATE'S `conversion_factor` VERDICT IS A PROMISE NOTHING KEEPS (`DEF-3107`).** `conversion_factor` is read by `metering.validation` for the high-usage floor and by **NOTHING** in `apps/billing/` or `apps/rating/pricing/`, so a tenant told at SAVE that its factor satisfies the gate still has every bill refused by this same rule at `pricing.stages.normalize._gate_uom`. Filed, not closed: closing it means converting at rating input (`B2 r6.4`'s register→billed direction), which is money arithmetic on a B4-governed path. ⚠ **WHAT DID NOT MOVE:** a SCALE mismatch with no factor (`kgal` against `ccf`) is still a WARNING and not a refusal — **US-RAT-005 TC-7 is ratified as _warn now, rating blocked later_** and reversing a signed test case is a re-sign (HUMAN-GUIDE §6). ⚠ **AND THE SAVE-TIME REFUSAL IS SIZED AND NOT BUILT (`DEF-3106`):** written, it turned **18** cases of `tests/test_us_rat_schedules.py` red in one file, because the shared fixtures price an ELECTRIC schedule per `kgal`. The fixtures are wrong and the refusal is right; sweeping them is what closes it. | PO Q&A R6 (OQ-004 closure, B4 R6 §) · **PO ruling 2026-09-02** (decision log) · `BR-MET-143` (PO 2026-08-27) · `B2 r6.4` · `OQ-1290` · CLAUDE.md hard rule 3 · `DEF-3106`/`DEF-3107`/`DEF-3108` | CONFIRMED (clause 1 and clause 2 BUILT 2026-09-02, 🔒 HUMAN-REVIEW-PENDING; clause 3 filed) | US-RAT-005 TC-7, US-RAT-012 TC-7, `06-backend/tests/test_schedule_meter_unit_invariant.py` tc1–tc10 |
