| BR-RAT-040 | Bulk rate update: one `rate_change_batches` row = one reason + one effective date + one actor; per-entity `rate_change_log` rows reference the batch; apply is atomic (all-or-none); batches append-only, never re-run (re-apply = new batch) | B2 r5 #14 (D9 substance) + IS-6 bulk pattern | CONFIRMED (PO R5-9) | US-RAT-011 TCs |
| BR-RAT-041 | Charge amount rules v1 = flat / percent / percent-with-minimum; "Formula" visible but disabled (covers NSF, late, reconnect, service call, deposit return) | PO OQ-053 | CONFIRMED | — |
| BR-RAT-042 | Per-unit tax "applies above N units" is MARGINAL: only units above the threshold are taxed | PO OQ-054 (option a) | CONFIRMED | — |
| BR-RAT-043 | Plan discounts = negative-amount charge rules on the plan; budget billing = plan flag (level-pay mechanics in EP-BIL-06); eligibility (senior/CAP/low-income) is ADVISORY-WITH-AUDIT in v1 — staff attests, audit row records who/when; enforced verification + document uploads = v2 | PO OQ-055 (adopting PM recommendation; sub-parts 1–2 per recommendation — flag if unintended) | CONFIRMED | — |

## StorySmith pack (2026-08-10) — modular engine + tenant-authored calculation rules (EP-RAT-05 · EP-RAT-07)

Two PO requirements of 2026-08-10: (1) *"how do we write billing code modular, maintainable"*; (2) *"how do we allow business users to define some calculation rules that are very common and can change utility to utility — so that we don't have to do code changes every time"*. **BR-RAT-044/045** answer (1) on the rating side (the billing-side pipeline is BR-BIL-037..040). **BR-RAT-046..055** answer (2).

Three boundaries this block does not cross, stated before the table so no reader has to infer them:
- **BR-RAT-041 is untouched and still CONFIRMED.** BR-RAT-046 is a **PROPOSED AMENDMENT** to it. Nothing in EP-RAT-07 is buildable until the PO ratifies that amendment (candidate **OQ-095**) — every story there ships `status: blocked`.
- **B4/B5 are ❄ frozen.** No rule here changes a signed figure or rule; **BR-RAT-055** makes that claim testable instead of asserted (hard-won law 1).
- **BR-TEN-048 / DB-17 stands absolutely.** A tenant-configured *rating object* (plan, tariff, tier, TOU window, season, charge code, tax) legitimately drives money — that is the product. A tenant-defined *custom field* never does. A tenant-authored **formula** is a third category: rating configuration, but an *expression* rather than a *parameter*, which is a different risk class — **BR-RAT-048** bounds the expression and **BR-RAT-053** proves it can never reach `custom_data`.

| ID | Rule | Source | Status | Test |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| BR-RAT-044 | **Pricing models are PLUGINS behind one interface.** Each `tariff_model` value — flat · tiered · TOU · demand · seasonal · capacity · percent-of-service (sewer) — is its own module implementing one contract, `price(subscription_ctx, measured_usage, model_config) → line candidates`; the pipeline's price-usage stage holds **no per-model branching**, so a new model is a new module plus a registry row, never an engine edit. The registry is **closed at import time** — models are code, never tenant data. The extension point is already proven: BR-RAT-019 added `capacity` to the enum after v1 scoping and the surrounding rules did not move | derived from B2 r5 #16 (`tariff_model` enum) + BR-RAT-019 + EP-RAT-05 scope + PO request 2026-08-10 (1) | PROPOSED | US-RAT-015 TC-1/2/4/7 |
| BR-RAT-045 | **Charge amount rules are plugins of the same shape.** flat · percent · percent-with-minimum (BR-RAT-041) are three implementations of `amount(charge_ctx) → NUMERIC(18,6)` resolved through one registry; a fourth rule adds a module and a registry row and changes no pipeline stage. No rule implementation rounds its own return value — rounding stays at line finalization (BR-RAT-003, B4.4) | derived from BR-RAT-041 + US-RAT-008 §7 (the three forms already share one call site) | PROPOSED | US-RAT-015 TC-3/6 |
| BR-RAT-046 | **PROPOSED AMENDMENT to BR-RAT-041 — enable the fourth amount rule, `formula`.** BR-RAT-041 (CONFIRMED, PO OQ-053) ships the S12 "Formula" radio **visible but disabled**; the PO's 2026-08-10 request is precisely to switch it on. The amendment adds `formula` as a fourth `charges.amount_rule` value and changes **nothing else** in BR-RAT-041: flat / percent / percent-with-minimum keep their exact v1 semantics, and the charge families BR-RAT-041 names (NSF, late, reconnect, service call, deposit return) keep working with no formula in play. Until the PO ratifies, BR-RAT-041 stands as written | PO request 2026-08-10 (2) vs BR-RAT-041 CONFIRMED (PO OQ-053) | **PROPOSED — AMENDMENT, needs PO ratification (candidate OQ-095)** | US-RAT-016 TC-1 |
| BR-RAT-047 | **Closed, versioned variable vocabulary.** A formula may reference ONLY names on a published allow-list; every name resolves to a quantity the engine already computes, carries an explicit unit or currency, and is bound before evaluation. An unknown name is a **save-time** validation error echoing the token — never null, never 0. The allow-list is versioned (`vocabulary_version`) and stamped on every formula version, so adding a name later cannot change what an existing formula meant. **v1 proposal** (contents ratified by candidate OQ-096): `consumption.<service>` (post-multiplier, billed UoM — BR-RAT-001/038) · `days_in_period` · `days_in_full_period` (B4.3) · `base_charge.<service>` (BR-RAT-012/027) · `usage_amount.<service>` and `usage_amount.total` (the BR-RAT-015 basis — usage dollars, excl. base, excl. tax) · `service_subtotal.<service>` (usage + base + minimum adjustment — BR-RAT-028 mode-A basis) · `bill_subtotal_pre_tax` · `minimum_amount.<service>` / `plan_minimum` (BR-RAT-028) · `capacity_units.<service>` (BR-RAT-018/022) · `tier.<service>.<n>.from_qty` / `.to_qty` (BR-RAT-032) · `prior_balance` · `overdue_portion` (B4.1) · `days_past_due` (BR-BIL-008 grace basis). Dotted names are **single tokens** on the allow-list — the grammar has no attribute or index operator (BR-RAT-048). `custom_data` and every tenant-defined key are absent by construction and cannot be added (BR-RAT-053) | derived per name from the cited CONFIRMED rules; each is an existing determined engine quantity | PROPOSED (contents = candidate OQ-096) | US-RAT-016 TC-2/3/8 |
| BR-RAT-048 | **Safe evaluator — this is a 🔒 money path.** Grammar: numeric literals, allow-listed variables, `+ − × ÷`, parentheses, and a **closed function set: `min`, `max`**. Nothing else exists — no identifiers off the list, no attribute access, no indexing, no assignment, no conditionals in v1, no loops, no user-defined functions, no string type, no imports, no I/O, no database access at evaluation time, and no host-language `eval`/`exec` anywhere in the implementation. The stored artefact is the parsed **AST** plus a hash; evaluation walks the AST — the source string is never executed. Arithmetic is `Decimal`/NUMERIC(18,6) end to end (CLAUDE.md #2). **No rounding function is offered**: rounding stays at line finalization (BR-RAT-003 / B4.4) and a second rounding law is exactly the defect B4.4 exists to prevent. Division by zero, overflow, an unbound variable, or a non-finite result **blocks** with a named error — no path silently returns 0 or NULL. Determinism: same inputs ⇒ same output, no clock, no random, no locale — property-tested | derived from CLAUDE.md 🔒 sensitive paths + hard rules #2/#3 + BR-RAT-003 (B4.4) + hard-won law 3 (defaults fail closed) | PROPOSED | US-RAT-016 TC-4..9, TC-12 |
| BR-RAT-049 | **One evaluation point, one total order, no chaining.** A formula charge is evaluated exactly once per bill, inside the apply-charges stage (BR-BIL-037 stage 4) — after usage/base/capacity pricing and after the minimum-bill adjustment, and **before tax** (stage 5), so the B4.2 tax base is unaffected by construction. A formula may **not** reference another charge's amount, so no cycle can exist; where a bill carries several formula charges they evaluate in `charges.code` ascending order, which is total and stable | derived from B4.2 base + BR-RAT-005 + BR-RAT-028 ordering + BR-BIL-037 | PROPOSED | US-RAT-016 TC-10, US-RAT-019 TC-5 |
| BR-RAT-050 | **A formula versions like a tariff.** Publishing writes an immutable `charge_formula_versions` row with an effective window; in-force versions are never edited in place and windows never overlap (BR-RAT-037 discipline + the B2 EXCLUDE precedent). Every invoice line a formula produces stamps `formula_version_id` — the same stamping move as `gl_account_id` (DB-8) and `template_id`+version (DB-9) — so a bill records which formula priced it. Editing or superseding a formula NEVER re-prices an issued bill (BR-BIL-004/030) and never re-prices history (BR-RAT-007); versions stay reproducible for 7 years (BR-RAT-017) | derived from BR-RAT-007/017/037 + BR-BIL-004/030 + DB-8/DB-9 | PROPOSED | US-RAT-017 TC-1..6 |
| BR-RAT-051 | **Test before publish — a formula that has never been evaluated cannot be published.** The author supplies a sample value for every declared variable the expression references; the system evaluates and shows the result, and publish is refused while any sample is missing, any variable is unbound, or any sample errors. The passing sample set, its result, the actor and the timestamp are stored on the version row, so "what did we test?" is answerable years later; because the set is stored it re-runs against every later version of the same formula and a changed result is shown as a diff before publish. A rule that silently mis-prices is worse than one that cannot be written | derived from IS-6 (confirm before a money action) + PO requirement 2026-08-10 + hard-won law 7 (prove the guard RED first) | PROPOSED (regression-set scope = candidate OQ-099) | US-RAT-018 TC-1..8 |
| BR-RAT-052 | **Every formula create · edit · publish · supersede · suspend · test-run writes `audit_log`** (CLAUDE.md #7 — a formula is a money action), carrying the expression source, the AST hash, the vocabulary version, the actor and the timestamp. A **reason is REQUIRED on publish, supersede and suspend**, matching the rate-change reason discipline (BR-RAT-037; `rate_change_log.reason NOT NULL`). Formula changes appear in the S9 History view (EP-RAT-04) as entity type `formula`, so the compliance trail stays one trail | CLAUDE.md #7 + BR-RAT-037 + EP-RAT-04 History view | PROPOSED | US-RAT-017 TC-7/8 |
| BR-RAT-053 | **A formula can never reach `custom_data` — and the gate proves it.** The resolver is a *positive allow-list* (BR-RAT-047), not a lookup over model attributes, so a tenant key is not merely forbidden but unreachable; the grammar has no attribute or index operator, so `account.custom_data.x` cannot even parse. Three machine checks: (1) G6 keeps `custom_data` out of `apps/rating/` **absolutely** — already law (BR-TEN-048 / DB-17); (2) a resolver test asserts every allow-list entry maps to a B2 column or an engine-computed quantity and that none is sourced from `custom_data`; (3) an expression naming a tenant key fails validation with `UNKNOWN_VARIABLE` and the token echoed. B2 r17.2 already excludes `tariffs` / `rate_tiers` / `charges` / `taxes` from custom fields — "the calculation boundary" — so no custom field can be attached to a formula row either | BR-TEN-048 · DB-17 · B2 r17.2 exclusion list · G6 | PROPOSED | US-RAT-016 TC-8, US-RAT-019 TC-6 |
| BR-RAT-054 | **Blast radius — a published formula found wrong after bills have issued.** No new repair path is invented: (1) the wrong version is **superseded** by a new version, never edited (BR-RAT-037); (2) issued bills are **not** re-priced — the bill is locked at issue (BR-BIL-004/030) and corrections are new rows (CLAUDE.md #4); (3) repair runs through the existing approval-gated correction workflow, single or BULK by run/cycle/plan/service/date-range with impact preview and System-Admin approval (BR-BIL-014); (4) the impacted set is **derivable, not guessed**, because every affected line stamps `formula_version_id` (BR-RAT-050); (5) a formula may be **suspended**, after which the next run raises an S31.3 exception for that charge rather than repeating the error — never a silent skip (billing model §2) | derived from BR-RAT-037 + BR-BIL-004/014/030 + billing model §2 | PROPOSED | US-RAT-019 TC-1..5 |
| BR-RAT-055 | **A tenant formula can never alter how GT-1..61 evaluate.** The guarantee is structural, not procedural: a formula is an amount rule on a **charge code**, so it can only add a charge line — it cannot replace, intercept or modify usage pricing (stage 3), the minimum-bill adjustment, or tax computation (stage 5); and charge lines sit outside the B4.2 tax base by rule (BR-RAT-005 — US-RAT-013 TC-7 already refuses non-usage/base line kinds structurally rather than by data). The golden fixtures configure built-in amount rules and contain no formula charge, so their evaluation path is untouched. **Proof, not assertion:** the full GT-1..61 suite runs twice — once with the formula subsystem absent, once with a tenant formula published and evaluating in the same process — and both runs must produce identical output. B4/B5 stay frozen; nothing in EP-RAT-07 edits a signed figure or rule (changes there need a finance re-sign via HUMAN-GUIDE §6) | derived from the B5 ❄ freeze banner (GT-1..61 = the billing-math contract) + BR-RAT-041 scope + BR-RAT-005 | PROPOSED | US-RAT-016 TC-11, US-RAT-019 TC-7 |
| BR-RAT-056 | **`percent` takes an EXPLICIT, user-chosen base (PO ruling 2026-08-10, OQ-096).** The `percent` amount rule gains a REQUIRED "percent of" selector — `usage_only` · `usage_plus_base` · `whole_bill` · `overdue_portion` — instead of a default nobody wrote down. The ambiguity being closed is live and inside an already-CONFIRMED rule: US-RAT-008 §7 says "percent × base_amount" and `base_amount` is defined nowhere, so a 3% surcharge on the reference bill yields **$2.46** (usage $82.08), **$3.02** (usage+base $100.58) or **$3.26** (whole bill $108.71) — three implementers, three different bills, each believing they followed the rule. **Migration consequence, not optional:** every existing percent charge code must be assigned a base. `LATE` is already pinned to the overdue portion by B4.1 ❄ and is set to that, never defaulted. | Product Owner 2026-08-10 (OQ-096) | **CONFIRMED** | to author |
| BR-RAT-057 | **Business-user-definable rules stop at CHARGE AMOUNTS (PO ruling 2026-08-10, OQ-097).** They never reach usage pricing (stage 3, where GT-1/2/3/5 live) or tax (stage 5, B4.2 ❄). A tenant-authored expression inside either would put user arithmetic within the finance-signed billing-math contract. Widening this scope is a separate PO ruling, never an implementation choice | Product Owner 2026-08-10 (OQ-097) | **CONFIRMED** | to author |
| BR-RAT-058 | **Admin-only publish, and the screen MUST SHOW THE EFFECT before it (PO ruling 2026-08-10, OQ-098).** No second approver — in a 1–2 staff utility the second person is often nobody. The preview REPLACES that missing second pair of eyes: publishing renders a worked example computed live from the author's own sample input, so `0.15` typed for `0.015` shows as a $75 late fee on screen instead of reaching 1,247 customers. The preview is the control, not decoration | Product Owner 2026-08-10 (OQ-098) | **CONFIRMED** | to author |
| BR-RAT-059 | **Formula test samples are a PERMANENT REGRESSION SET (PO ruling 2026-08-10, OQ-099).** Samples are stored with the formula and re-run on every edit; a publish that breaks a stored sample is refused. A one-time pre-publish check proves only that the author looked once, and the dangerous edit is the one six months later. The same samples feed BR-RAT-058's on-screen effect, so authoring the guard and authoring the preview are one act | Product Owner 2026-08-10 (OQ-099) | **CONFIRMED** | to author |
| BR-RAT-060 | **A formula must be UNIT-COHERENT, refused at BOTH save and evaluation (PO ruling 2026-08-10, OQ-100).** `consumption + prior_balance` is 684 kWh + $177.88 and is meaningless. Save-time refusal is for the human, who fixes it while looking at it; evaluation-time refusal is the backstop, because the data reaching a formula changes and the failure would otherwise land mid bill-run. Refusing only at save fails OPEN at run time (hard-won law 3). Decidable because CLAUDE.md rule 3 already requires every consumption to carry its unit | Product Owner 2026-08-10 (OQ-100) | **CONFIRMED** | to author |
| BR-RAT-061 | **A charge evaluating to exactly zero RENDERS as `$0.00` (PO ruling 2026-08-10, OQ-101).** A conditional charge that did not fire still appears on the bill rather than vanishing, so the customer can see it was considered and not applied. No rule in the corpus previously covered zero-amount invoice lines. Applies to every bill where a conditional charge evaluates to zero | Product Owner 2026-08-10 (OQ-101) | **CONFIRMED** | to author |
| BR-RAT-062 | **A bill covering SEVERAL services is taxed ONCE, over the combined base — tax is a BILL-level concept, never per-service.** Each service's lines are priced and rounded per line (BR-RAT-003, unchanged); the B4.2 tax base is Σ of the ROUNDED taxable lines **across every service on the plan**; one tax pass then emits **one line per authority**, not one per authority per service. Fees are likewise bill-level and are added once, after the services — charging them per service would multiply a late fee by the number of services. The rule was already frozen and machine-verified, and had simply never been written as a numbered rule: B5 **GT-17** (= WE-1, the reference bill: five services → one base of 221.90 → four tax lines, verified by `10-scripts/03-golden-verify.py`), EP-BIL-01 §7's stage trace of the same bill ("stage 3 Σ … = 221.90 (all taxable) → stage 5 tax … split by authority"), and the PO ruling of 2026-08-11 — *"Tax rates are for bill not for service, it can be conditional but it is for bill."* **B4.2 as re-signed 2026-08-11 is what settles the arithmetic**: the base is summed from the ROUNDED line amounts *because a customer who adds up the printed lines and applies the rate must get the printed tax*. Per-service taxation breaks exactly that — Σ(rate × base_i) rounded per service need not equal rate × Σ(base_i), so the document would not foot against itself | B5 GT-17 ❄️ · B4 §Tax (re-signed 2026-08-11) · EP-BIL-01 §7 · PO ruling 2026-08-11 (decision log) · BR-RAT-025 plan-as-bundle | CONFIRMED (the sources are signed; only the numbering is new — 2026-08-11) | GT-17 · US-RAT-013 TC-11..15 (`tests/test_us_rat_013_multi_service.py`) |

## PO rulings 2026-08-12 — Plans & Rates

Three rulings of the ~55-question batch of 2026-08-12 land in this module. Two are stamped in place above — **BR-RAT-004** (a tariff change inside a back-billed period is SPLIT BY DAYS; worked group **35.56**) and **BR-RAT-030 / BR-RAT-035** (the billed-plan and issued-line guards are LIVE since the DEF-079 counters were published). The third has no existing home and takes the next free ID. Figures are copied from `12-governance/02-decision-log.md` (2026-08-12), never recomputed.

| ID | Rule | Source | Status | Test |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| BR-RAT-063 | **A MID-CYCLE PLAN-CHANGE PREVIEW SHOWS THE BASE HALF ONLY, AND STATES THAT USAGE IS EXCLUDED.** PO verbatim: _"Base only"_. The base charges are stored figures the frozen engine prorates (**GT-3: 15/30 × 30.50 = 15.25**). **The USAGE half is not forecast, not averaged and not shown as a rate comparison** — the preview says usage is excluded, so nothing quotes a customer a number for days that have not happened. The gap this closes is real: BR-RAT-004 prorates usage as well as base, and a preview has no consumption because the days have not occurred; the engine prices consumption it is handed and no signed document says what a forecast would be. **Worked, on the EP-CUS-05 fixture:** the preview renders the base delta **−$18.50** (Senior **12.00** − RES-STD **30.50**, two of the tenant's own stored base charges) and NAMES every line it left out; the story's **−$68.50** net is NOT rendered, because a net that quietly summed a shorter list would be the most dangerous number on the screen. ⚠ Read with the ruling of the same day (OQ-179) that **there is no mid-plan deposit differential at all — the deposit settles only at closure**, so no deposit line belongs in this preview either (BR-CUS-042 / BR-CUS-062 differential clauses SUPERSEDED; customers-owned) | Product Owner 2026-08-12 (OQ-180) | **CONFIRMED** | GT-3 · US-CUS-016 TC-3 (customers-owned surface) |
| BR-RAT-064 | ⛔ **SUPERSEDED 2026-08-16 by BR-RAT-067 (PO ruling closing OQ-236: _"make the rate wizard say which register is which"_). PRICE-RANK MATCHING IS NO LONGER THE DESIGN AND NO CODE PERFORMS IT — `time_of_use_windows.channel` is stated by the utility in the S11 wizard and read by rating; a window with no stated register REFUSES by name. The superseded text is kept verbatim below because the reasoning was cited in four files and a reader arriving from one of them must find it, not a gap.** ~~A configured TOU WINDOW is matched to the METER REGISTER it is measured on BY PRICE RANK — dearest = `tou_on`, cheapest = `tou_off`, the middle one = `tou_mid`.** BR-RAT-021 says reads arrive PRE-SPLIT per window, and metering splits them by CHANNEL (`tou_on | tou_mid | tou_off`, B2 r8 / BR-MET-059). Nothing in the schema joins a `time_of_use_windows` row to a channel, and without the join no kWh can be priced at all. The precedent for identifying a window by its price is already signed: **BR-RAT-033 says every holiday resolves *"to the off-peak (lowest-price) window of its season"***, and BR-RAT-021 caps Option B at *"up to 3 windows (on/mid/off)"*, which fixes the other two. Ranking is PER SEASON (prices are per season). A season with exactly one window maps to `tou_off`. **Where the ranking is not decidable — two windows priced the same in one season, or more than three windows — rating publishes no channel and the engine REFUSES by name**; nothing prices against a window chosen by position. Matching on the LABEL is explicitly rejected: a label is display text a tenant may rename or translate, and BR-BIL-069/OQ-116 already record what placing money by display text costs. **Worked:** the demo tenant's summer schedule (on $0.22 / mid $0.14 / off $0.09) against registers of 120, 200 and 380 kWh gives **$88.60** joined correctly and **$120.20** with off-peak's 380 kWh priced at the on-peak rate~~ | derived from BR-RAT-033 (CONFIRMED, PO R5-6) + BR-RAT-021 (CONFIRMED, PO Q&A R3 Q6) + B2 r8 channel enum; pricing-engines lane 2026-08-16 | **SUPERSEDED 2026-08-16 (OQ-236 CLOSED) → BR-RAT-067. It was never ratified and is now withdrawn; nothing in the tree implements it. The red-proof that the inference is gone is `test_us_rat_012_tou.py::test_us_rat_012_tc5_price_order_does_not_decide_the_bill`** | GT-17 · US-RAT-012 TC-5 (`tests/test_us_rat_012_tou.py`) |
| BR-RAT-065 | **The BASIS of a percent-of-service charge is the sum of the basis service's FINALIZED (printed) usage lines, not the unrounded intermediates behind them.** BR-RAT-015 is CONFIRMED and says *"% of water usage-charge dollars (excl. base, excl. tax)"* without saying which dollars. Two things settle it and neither is new: **`10-scripts/03-golden-verify.py`, the FROZEN harness, computes GT-17's basis as the sum of the ROUNDED tier lines**, so the other reading would move a machine-verified figure; and **B4.2 as re-signed 2026-08-11** settled the identical question for the tax base with a reason that is not about tax — *"a customer who adds up the four printed lines and applies the rate"* must reach the printed figure. A customer adding up the printed water lines and applying 40% must likewise reach the printed sewer line. **No frozen figure distinguishes the readings** (every water tier amount in the corpus is exact at 2dp), so the reading is REPORTED rather than presented as settled. **Worked (GT-17):** water 5 kgal x $4.00 = $20.00 and 3 kgal x $6.50 = $19.50 -> basis **$39.50** -> sewer 40% = **$15.80**. Including the $8.00 water base would give $19.00; the base and the tax are both out | derived from BR-RAT-015 (CONFIRMED, PO Q&A R1 Q8) + B4.2 re-signed 2026-08-11 + the frozen harness's own computation; pricing-engines lane 2026-08-16 | **PROPOSED — needs PO ratification (OQ-232); non-blocking, the difference is at most one cent** | GT-17 · GT-25 · US-RAT-012 TC-6 (`tests/test_us_rat_012_percent_of_service.py`) |
| BR-RAT-066 | **A pricing model REFUSES BY NAME whenever a measurement it prices was not taken, and never prices it as zero.** The general form of a posture the corpus already states case by case (BR-BIL-015 makes a missing read a human decision; `apps.billing.contracts.ServiceReading` says a zero *"would be a claim that the meter was read and showed no usage"*), written down because four new rate shapes each needed it and each could have quietly answered $0.00 instead. It binds four concrete cases: a **TOU window** the tariff prices with no register read (BR-RAT-021); a **peak-demand** register that was not read (BR-RAT-014 — on WE-5 that line is **$2,002.00**); a **percent-of-service** tariff whose basis service is not on this bill or billed base-only (BR-RAT-015 — CLAUDE.md's seam rule: report that it cannot answer, never answer 0); and a **demand** tariff with a ratchet or power-factor penalty enabled, whose measurement rules are still PROPOSED (US-RAT-007 TC-8, *"no silent math"* — pricing the raw peak instead would under-bill every bill this tariff prices, invisibly). **A metered ZERO is not covered and must not be**: a customer who genuinely used no water owes no sewer, and 40% x $0.00 = $0.00 is the right answer. The distinction the rule turns on is *unknown* versus *known to be none* | generalised from BR-BIL-015 + BR-RAT-014/015/021 + CLAUDE.md hard-won law 3 (defaults fail closed) and the seam rule; pricing-engines lane 2026-08-16 | **PROPOSED — states existing practice; no figure changes if the PO declines it, only the refusals would become zeros** | US-RAT-012 TC-5/TC-6 · US-RAT-014 TC-6 (`tests/test_us_rat_012_tou.py` · `..._percent_of_service.py` · `test_us_rat_014_capacity.py`) |
| BR-RAT-067 | **THE UTILITY STATES WHICH METER REGISTER EACH TIME-OF-USE WINDOW IS MEASURED ON. Rating never infers it.** PO ruling 2026-08-16, verbatim: _"make the rate wizard say which register is which"_ (OQ-236 CLOSED). `time_of_use_windows.channel` holds metering's own vocabulary — `tou_on | tou_mid | tou_off` (B2 r8 / BR-MET-059) — chosen per window on the S11 Time-of-use card and stored with the tariff VERSION, so a seven-year reprice reads the mapping the utility stated then (BR-RAT-007). **Three clauses, and each one is a refusal rather than a fallback:** **(1)** a window whose register is not stated is REFUSED BY NAME at pricing (`TariffConfigError` naming the window and the schedule) — never resolved by price, by label, by row order or by position, and never priced at $0.00, because a window priced at zero is a charge the bill forgives every month until somebody notices; **(2)** two windows in one season may not name the SAME register (the second would silently overwrite the first's price); **(3)** the register is REQUIRED to activate a time-of-use schedule — a draft may be saved without it, an in-force version may not exist without it. **Worked, and it is why this was the first question the PO answered:** the demo tenant's summer schedule prices on-peak $0.22, mid-peak $0.14 and off-peak $0.09 against registers reading 120, 200 and 380 kWh. Stated correctly the electric usage is **$88.60** (26.40 + 28.00 + 34.20) — the 4100 posting of the frozen reference bill, ❄️ B5 GT-17. Paired the wrong way round the same meter bills **$122.40** (380 x 0.22 + 200 x 0.14 + 120 x 0.09 = 83.60 + 28.00 + 10.80), a **38% overcharge with nothing on screen looking wrong.** **Windows configured BEFORE the column existed are left with NO register and refuse by name** — they are NOT backfilled by the old price rank, because a backfilled value is indistinguishable from a stated one and would read as the utility's choice when it was the machine's (CLAUDE.md hard-won law 3, defaults fail closed). The migration reports how many it left unset so the gap is a task, not a surprise on run day | Product Owner 2026-08-16 (OQ-236), implemented by the register lane 2026-08-16 | **CONFIRMED** — supersedes BR-RAT-064 | GT-17 · US-RAT-012 TC-5 (`tests/test_us_rat_012_tou.py`, incl. the price-order-reversal proof) · US-RAT-006 TC-9 / TC-10 (`tests/test_us_rat_006_tc9_window_register.py`) |

## CR-013 part 3 — DISCOUNTS (2026-08-19, EP-RAT-12)

Twelve rules, and every one of them is a sentence the Product Owner supplied — in
CR-013 part 3 (`DISCOUNTS-BUSINESS-SPEC.md`, captured verbatim in
`12-governance/11-change-requests.md`), in the three rulings of 2026-08-19
(`12-governance/02-decision-log.md`), or read out of r10's own drawing
(`04-docs/05-modules/06-rating/07-r10-plans-rates-readout.md` §4). **Nothing here
is a lane's invention**; where an implementation had to choose something no
document states, the choice is marked as a FIXTURE in the seeder and not written
as a rule.

⚠ **THE STATE THESE RULES LAND ON: THERE WAS NO DISCOUNT OBJECT AT ALL.** On
2026-08-19, `\dt *discount*` on the live database returned 0 rows, a grep for a
Discount model across every `models.py` in `apps/` returned no match, and r10
drew five discounts with nothing behind them. `apps/rating/resolution.py` said so
in its own docstring, returning R6 as an explicitly unmodelled step. So these are
not amendments — they are the first statement of what a discount is in this
product.

| ID | Rule | Source | Status | Test |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| BR-RAT-068 | **A DISCOUNT IS AN OVERLAY, NEVER A RATE.** r10's own detail copy, verbatim: _"An overlay, not a cloned rate: it references the plan's normal schedules and reduces the subtotal by a percentage before taxes. Removing a customer from the program restores full price with no rate change."_ It therefore owns no tier, no schedule and no connection-size row. **And the bill shows BOTH numbers** — part 3: _"the bill shows the full tariff amount and the reduction as its own line, so subsidy granted is reportable."_ A discount is never a smaller number on the tariff line. Consequence for scope: a scoped percentage (`usage_only`, `base_only`, `one_service`, `named_codes`) is a percentage of the TARIFF lines in its scope — of what is printed — while only `whole_bill` runs against the reducing subtotal | CR-013 part 3 + r10 `SMARTBillHub.dc.html:10552` | CONFIRMED 2026-08-19 | `test_cr013_discounts.py::test_a_scoped_discount_takes_its_own_scope_not_the_running_subtotal` |
| BR-RAT-069 | ⚖️ **NARROWED 2026-10-03 by PO ruling (3) (decision log *"GO ON THE DISCOUNTS REDESIGN"*) → `BR-RAT-1063`: for an AUTOMATIC discount the product WRITES the enrolment for every account on a carrying plan that meets a system-checked condition. The bill still reads enrolments and an attachment still grants nothing by itself.** **AVAILABLE ≠ GRANTED. A plan attachment discounts NOBODY.** Part 3, verbatim: _"plan attachment makes a discount available, not granted; Accounts holds enrolment"_, and part 5 step 6 repeats it in the wizard's own words. The plan says which discounts CAN apply; the ACCOUNT'S ENROLMENT says which DO, and the bill run reads enrolments. **Built as a plan-level flag this is a wrong bill for every account on the plan** — 842 of them on Residential Standard in r10's own fixture. Two tables, deliberately: `plan_discount_rules` (availability) and `discount_enrolments` (the grant) | CR-013 part 3 *Downstream* + part 5 step 6 + design §A9 | CONFIRMED 2026-08-19 | `::test_attaching_a_discount_to_a_plan_discounts_nobody` · `::test_enrolment_is_what_grants_a_discount` |
| BR-RAT-070 | **A PLAN MAY NOT OVERRIDE A DISCOUNT'S VALUE.** PO ruling 2026-08-19, verbatim: **_"no plan cannot override prices."_** r10 draws a per-plan discount-value override (`SMARTBillHub.dc.html:10805`) and its step 8 generates the sentence _"Override · {code} · discount value replaced with {value}"_; the ruling deletes both. The rule is enforced STRUCTURALLY rather than by validation: `plan_discount_rules` carries no value column, so the forbidden thing is unstorable. The general form is part 5's load-bearing sentence — _"a plan composes; it does not price"_ | PO ruling 2026-08-19 (decision log, ruling 1) | CONFIRMED 2026-08-19 — final | `::test_a_plan_discount_rule_cannot_carry_a_value` |
| BR-RAT-071 | **DISCOUNTS APPLY AT R6, BEFORE TAXES — BUT EACH TAX PICKS ITS OWN BASE.** The resolution chain (design §A2) puts R6 before R7. CR-013 part 4 then makes discount treatment **statutory and PER TAX**: some authorities levy on the pre-discount amount. **So no global "taxes computed on the discounted amount" flag exists on the discount**, even though r10's DIS-02 step 2 draws that checkbox — the answer belongs on `taxes.discount_treatment` (EP-RAT-13, unbuilt), and one flag here would hard-code one authority's answer for every authority in the product. R6 therefore returns BOTH subtotals and lets R7 choose | CR-013 parts 3 and 4 + design §A2 | CONFIRMED 2026-08-19 | `::test_both_subtotals_are_returned_because_the_tax_picks_its_own_base` |
| BR-RAT-072 | **A DISCOUNT HAS FOUR STATUSES AND ONE OF THEM IS SET BY A BILL RUN.** Part 3, DIS-01: _"Status (Active/Draft/Retired/Suspended-funding-exhausted)"_. The fourth carries money semantics — a discount that has spent its budget is neither Active nor Retired, and the difference decides whether next month's bill is reduced. Paired with part 3's _"Run bills … raises an exception when budget is exhausted mid-run"_, **`suspended_funding_exhausted` is the only status in this product that a BILL RUN can set**, and it is distinct from `retired` because a retired programme is a decision and an exhausted one is an accident of arithmetic | CR-013 part 3 DIS-01 | CONFIRMED 2026-08-19 | `::test_a_budget_that_runs_out_this_bill_is_reported_for_suspension` |
| BR-RAT-073 | **STACKING PRECEDENCE IS DATA, NOT CODE ORDER — AND EXCLUSIVITY MEANS THE LARGER APPLIES.** Part 3 gives the reason in its own words: _"percentage-of-usage after a usage allowance gives a different number than the reverse."_ Worked on §A4's S1: AUTOPAY at −0.5% run FIRST sees $189.90 → **$0.95**; run after PAPERLESS's flat −$1.00 it sees $188.90 → **$0.94**. One cent on every bill, decided by a number the operator types. **Exclusivity is r10's own sentence** — _"Not with CAP credit · One assistance program per account — the larger applies"_ — so two discounts in one `exclusivity_group` are both computed and the larger reduction wins, with the loser returned as a NAMED DENIAL rather than silently dropped | CR-013 part 3 + r10 `:10554` | CONFIRMED 2026-08-19 | `::test_precedence_changes_the_answer_which_is_why_it_is_data` · `::test_one_assistance_programme_per_account_and_the_larger_applies` |
| BR-RAT-074 | **A BILL FLOOR, SO BILLS DO NOT REACH ZERO UNLESS ALLOWED.** Part 3, DIS-02 step 2: _"a floor so bills don't reach zero unless allowed"_. One figure per discount, `bill_floor_amount`, defaulting to **0.000000**: a discount may take a bill to zero and **never below it**, because a negative bill is a CREDIT — a different object with a different ledger posting — and no discount may mint one. A programme that must leave the customer paying something types a positive figure. The trim is reported (`trimmed_by = "bill_floor"`) so the reduction the customer did not get is explicable | CR-013 part 3 DIS-02 step 2 | CONFIRMED 2026-08-19 | `::test_the_floor_stops_a_bill_going_below_what_the_programme_allows` · `::test_a_discount_never_makes_a_bill_negative_even_with_no_floor` |
| BR-RAT-075 | ⚖️ **"SUSPEND" SUPERSEDED 2026-10-04 — A BILL IS NEVER HELD FOR A DISCOUNT.** PO verbatim: *"We cannot hold the bill; we need to make discount value to 0 and move on - we cannot stop the billing"*. Shown: a $10.00/bill programme with a $45.00 yearly budget shared by two accounts gave −$5.00 each in March and then held BOTH accounts' bills from April. **Now, for every tenant and every exhaustion setting, a used-up budget or yearly cap makes the discount line $0.00 (or what is left, on the bill that crosses it) and the bill is priced, issued and sent as normal;** the stored value `suspend` stays readable and means "stop the discount" (its label already reads *Suspend the discount*). The rest of this row stands: caps trim, a budget trims, consumption is a SUM over `discount_grants` (written at issue since `DEF-2430`, 2026-10-04), and a yearly cap counts the CALENDAR year of the bill's period end (PO 2026-10-04, decision log *"THREE PO ANSWERS"* (1)). ~~**CAPS TRIM, A BUDGET TRIMS, AND AN EXHAUSTED BUDGET RAISES.**~~ *(the original heading, kept for the record — the "raises" clause is what was superseded)* Part 3 asks for _"caps per bill, per year, lifetime"_ and _"budget cap and exhaustion behaviour"_, and pairs them with _"Run bills applies in precedence order and **raises an exception when budget is exhausted mid-run**"_. Three exhaustion behaviours: `suspend` (the run RAISES rather than issuing a bill that quietly charges an enrolled customer full price), `refuse_new_enrolment` (existing enrolments keep billing) and `continue_over_budget` (the utility knowingly overspends). **Budget consumption is a SUM over the append-only `discount_grants`, never a decremented counter**, because part 3's Budget-&-usage tab reports _"granted vs budget, denials by cap or funding"_ and a counter cannot say which bill spent the money | CR-013 part 3 DIS-02 steps 2 and 4 | CONFIRMED 2026-08-19 · **"suspend" SUPERSEDED 2026-10-04 (PO)** | `test_cr013_discounts.py::test_an_exhausted_budget_gives_nothing_and_never_holds_the_bill` (was `::test_an_exhausted_budget_raises_mid_run`) · `test_def_2430_discount_grants.py` · `::test_a_budget_trim_names_what_trimmed_it` |
| BR-RAT-076 | **VERIFICATION, PROOF AND RECERTIFICATION ARE THE DISCOUNT'S OWN, AND ONE COMBINATION IS REFUSED.** Part 3: a discount exists _"because of a fact about the customer … which is why the object owns its proof and recertification rules"_. Four verification methods (r10's own select: one-time proof · annual recertification · agency file monthly · automatic), a recertification period with notice and grace, and three lapse behaviours (r10: notify 60 days ahead then drop · drop silently · never expires). **`proof_before_first_reduction` is a MONEY rule and is stored**: true means an unverified enrolment reduces nothing and the bill is charged in full. ⚠ **AND THE NAMED VALIDATION GATE: automatic enrolment with mandatory proof is REFUSED** — part 3 calls the combination _"contradictory"_, and the product refuses it rather than resolving it silently, because which half it honoured would otherwise be decided by whichever code path ran first ⚠ **THE TWO SHIPPED DEFAULTS ARE SET (PO 2026-08-25) AND THIS RULE IS NOT REVERSED.** The PO was asked two questions this rule already answers, and answered them with ONE value each where this rule stores a choice PER PROGRAMME. Recorded as **defaults**, because that reading honours the words and leaves the signed rule standing — and because answer 4 of the same message (*"each utility types its own list in Settings - matches how we did documents"*) shows the PO thinking in exactly those per-tenant terms, the `BR-CUS-095` pattern. **(a) `proof_before_first_reduction` ships TRUE.** PO verbatim: *"Hold it — bill $142.30"*. Worked: a **$142.30** bill with a 20% assistance claim is billed at **$142.30** while the claim is unproven, and the **$28.46** applies once proof lands. A utility may still configure a programme the other way. **(b) `lapse_behaviour` ships as DROP ON THE DATE.** PO verbatim: *"Discount stops on the date, bill goes to full price"* — the first of r10's three, minus its 60-day notice. The other two remain configurable. ⚠ **IF THE PO MEANT TO DELETE THE PER-PROGRAMME CHOICE** in either case, that REVERSES this CONFIRMED rule and needs a re-sign via HUMAN-GUIDE §6; it is put back to them as a narrow question rather than assumed. ⛔ **AND (b) HAS NO MECHANISM.** Measured 2026-08-25 across `06-backend`: `recertification_due_on` is written by `verify_enrolment`, serialised by `enrolment_row`, indexed once — and read by **no runner, job, queue, timer or command**. The date passes and the discount keeps applying. The panel states that absence (`recertification_has_no_chaser`) rather than implying a chaser exists; **building one is a task and it is not W5's.** | CR-013 part 3 DIS-02 step 3 + r10 `:1245-1250` · **defaults PO 2026-08-25** | CONFIRMED 2026-08-19 · **defaults CONFIRMED 2026-08-25** · ⚠ **THE PROOF-KINDS GATE WAS ENFORCED AND UNREADABLE UNTIL 2026-09-19 — `DEF-RAT-5816`.** *Apply and approve, with proof* with no document ticked was refused at the COMMIT by the literal string `rating.discounts.proof_kinds_required`, which existed in **none of the eleven catalogues** — so the clerk read the developer key itself (B14 rule 32), after walking two more steps past the screen where the choice is made. The sentence now exists in all eleven (`Name at least one accepted document.`), its line is deleted from `core/refusal_keys_parked.txt` — **thirteen parked keys become twelve** — and DIS-02 step 3 blocks Continue at the control that raised it, using the SERVER's own key so the two wordings cannot drift | `::test_automatic_enrolment_with_mandatory_proof_is_refused` · `::test_a_proof_first_enrolment_reduces_nothing_until_it_is_verified` |
| BR-RAT-077 | **THE SECOND-APPROVAL THRESHOLD IS A TENANT SETTING, DEFAULT $50,000 (RAISED FROM $10,000 BY PO RULING 2026-08-21, OQ-290), MEASURED AGAINST THE DISCOUNT'S OWN BUDGET CAP — AND NO BUDGET ALWAYS REQUIRES IT.** PO ruling 2026-08-19, verbatim: _"okay, but allow user to set it , we can provide default."_ It measures the BUDGET CAP typed on the discount rather than a forecast, because part 3 step 4 already asks for one, so the figure exists at the moment of approval and is a commitment rather than an estimate. Against r10's catalogue at the default: SENIOR (≈$12,312/yr) and CAP (≈$11,400/yr) each need the second approval; PAPERLESS (≈$7,332) and AUTOPAY (≈$2,383) do not. ⚠ **AMENDED 2026-08-21 — AT $50,000 NONE OF THE FOUR TRIP IT, AND THAT IS THE RULING'S STATED INTENT.** PO verbatim: _"I will increase the discount limit and accommodate it for time being"_, and, asked for the figure, _"$50000"_. **This CLOSES the figure half of OQ-290** — the quantity stays BR-RAT-077's (the discount's own budget cap, the only one the product can evaluate), and the bar moves so the gate stops firing while the BR-RAT-091 conflict is settled properly. The largest seeded discount is SENIOR at **$12,312/yr**, a quarter of the new bar. **The gate is not removed:** a $50,000.01 cap still trips it, $50,000 exactly does not (the rule says *above*), and a discount with NO budget cap still ALWAYS requires it, because unbounded is not a comparison. ⚠ **The UNIT half stays open**: the figure was given against a question framed in $/month and this Param is compared to a budget cap the fixture states annually. At $50,000 both readings clear every discount that exists, so no bill and no approval differs — recorded, not resolved. **A discount with NO budget cap always requires it** — unbounded is the largest commitment there is. ⚠ **AND BR-RAT-091 (2026-08-20) STATES A DIFFERENT TEST WITH A DIFFERENT FIGURE, WITHOUT WITHDRAWING THIS ONE — `OQ-290`, OPEN (stamped 2026-08-21 by the DIS-02 lane).** This rule measures the **budget cap** against a tenant setting defaulting to **$10,000**; BR-RAT-091 measures the **projected monthly subsidy** against **$500**. **PAPERLESS separates them: `$7,332` a year is `$611` a month — over BR-RAT-091's bar and under this one.** This is the rule the code implements (`second_approval_required(budget_cap)`), and nothing in the product projects a monthly subsidy at all (DEF-402), so the newer rule currently has no input. DIS-02 step 4 prints both sentences rather than a lane choosing. The requirement is computed at SUBMIT and STORED with the threshold that decided it (design §A7.6: an approver must see what the submitter saw). ⚠ **AND A SUBMITTER MAY APPROVE THEIR OWN FILING** (PO 2026-08-19, verbatim _"yes allow it"_) — Silver Creek has 1–2 staff — **with the fact recorded** on the row and in the audit trail, so an auditor sees it rather than inferring it | PO ruling 2026-08-19 (decision log, rulings 4 and 5) + design §A8 | CONFIRMED 2026-08-19 | `::test_the_threshold_measures_the_discounts_own_budget_cap` · `::test_no_budget_cap_always_requires_the_second_approval` · `::test_a_submitter_may_approve_their_own_filing_and_it_is_recorded` |

The versioning and deletion halves STAND, and so does *retiring blocks new enrolment*.** **ENROLMENTS SURVIVE VERSIONING, AND DELETION IS REFUSED ONCE BILLED OR EVER ENROLLED.** Part 3's shared rules: _"enrolments survive versioning; retiring blocks new enrolment and lists affected accounts before confirming; deletion refused once a bill was reduced."_ Part 5 widens the bar to _"once billed **or ever enrolled**"_ — an account that enrolled and left still blocks it. Structurally: `discount_enrolments` carries an FK to the **DISCOUNT, never to a version**, so a new dated version cannot end an enrolment. r10's SENIOR went v1 −10% → v2 −15% on 1 Jan 2026 and its 72 accounts did not re-enrol; a version FK would have ended all 72 that morning. Retiring sets a status and takes no new enrolment; no code path deletes a discount | CR-013 part 3 shared rules + part 5 | CONFIRMED 2026-08-19 | `::test_an_enrolment_survives_a_new_discount_version` · `::test_a_retired_discount_takes_no_new_enrolment` |
| BR-RAT-079 | **INCOME AND MEDICAL PROOF ARE RESTRICTED BY ROLE AND EXCLUDED FROM GENERAL DOCUMENT LISTS.** Part 3, verbatim, in its shared rules. A privacy rule with a storage consequence: a medical certification must not appear in the same list a clerk browses for a meter photo. **The `restricted` flag is DERIVED FROM THE DOCUMENT KIND and never accepted from a caller** — income statements, tax returns, disability awards and medical certifications are restricted; a government ID or a veteran ID is not — so a new restricted kind cannot be filed as an ordinary document by an endpoint that forgot the flag. ⚠ **The LIST-FILTERING half is NOT built by EP-RAT-12's backend slice**: the flag exists and is asserted, and the documents surface that must honour it is a separate story | CR-013 part 3 shared rules | CONFIRMED 2026-08-19 (storage half); the documents-surface half is OWED | `::test_income_and_medical_proof_are_restricted_by_their_kind` · `::test_an_ordinary_proof_is_not_restricted` |
| BR-RAT-080 | **THE RATE SCHEDULE PRICES A QUANTITY; THE CHARGE CODE PRICES A THING. (PO ruling 2026-08-20 — RATIFIED.)** The PO's words: _"The rate schedule prices a QUANTITY. The charge code prices a THING. - let go with it; I like it and it is correct."_ **The test:** if the amount is multiplied by something the meter measured — or otherwise counted — it belongs to the **rate schedule**. If it is charged because a connection, an account or an event **exists**, regardless of consumption, it belongs to the **charge code**. **Every amount must fall on exactly one side, and no amount may be expressible by both.** This generalises §A3, which ruled the single case of the base charge, into the rule that decides every future case — and it exists because **four** amounts were found expressible by both objects, **two of them stored at the same figure**: base `$12.00`/`$12.00` (§A3, resolved) · **minimum bill `$18.00`/`$18.00`** (`TariffVersion.minimum_bill` vs charge code `MIN-W`) · capacity (`CapacityRate` vs `CAP-W $22.00`) · fire line standby (a Standby schedule vs `FIRE $14.00`). §A3 priced this failure mode: `$18.50` charged twice is **`$23,069.50` per month across 1,247 connections**, _"and no test catches it, because both rows are individually correct and only the rule makes the total wrong."_ ⚠ **WHAT THE RULE DECIDES, AND WHAT IT DOES NOT.** It decides: **minimum bill stays on the SCHEDULE** — it is neither a quantity nor a thing but a **floor on the schedule's own computed result** — so `MIN-W` must not exist as a charge code (DEF-362); and **trash carts are a SCHEDULE** (CR-019), because a cart count is a quantity and the price multiplies, which a charge code never does. It does **NOT** decide **capacity**: the reading is *charge code* (a reserved unit is a thing), but `CapacityRate` is consumed by **GT-15** and B5 is ❄️ FROZEN, so that case needs a finance re-sign via HUMAN-GUIDE §6 and stays open (DEF-363). Fire line standby remains §A5's open question. ⚠ **TWO PAIRS OF HOMONYMS ARE NOT OVERLAPS AND MUST SURVIVE ANY CLEANUP:** `TariffVersion.minimum_bill` (a floor on the whole service line) vs `Charge.min_amount` (the floor on a **percentage** code — `LATE · 1.5% · $5 min`); and the schedule's optional **cap** vs `Charge.max_amount`. Same words, unrelated rules. | PO ruling 2026-08-20 (decision log) · design §A3 (the base case this generalises) · `09-screen-inventory.md` §16 (the four overlaps, with figures) · DEF-359 · DEF-362 · DEF-363 | **CONFIRMED (2026-08-20)** | `06-backend/tests/test_br_rat_080_081_separation.py` · CR-019 · EP-RAT-10 · EP-RAT-11 |
| BR-RAT-081 | **A SERVICE DECLARES WHETHER IT IS METERED OR UNMETERED, AND THE BILL RUN READS THAT DECLARATION INSTEAD OF DEMANDING A METER. (PO ruling 2026-08-20.)** The PO's words: _"yes we have to say service is meter or unmetered."_ Ruled in answer to CR-019 (trash is billed per cart) and it fixes the general case, not the trash case. **Today the run demands a meter for every service** — `DEF-161` (CRITICAL): _"A plan carrying any non-metered service cannot be billed at all."_ `resolution.py` refuses by name: _"the plan covers {service} and the unit has no meter for it, so there is no connection size to resolve a size row against."_ Trash, and every other unmetered service, is unbillable. **The declaration lives on the SERVICE** (`tenant_services`, SET-03 — the same row that already carries `enabled` and `revenue_account`), because it is a property of what the utility sells, not of one plan, one account or one premise. **R3b then branches on it:** a **metered** service takes its billable quantity from a read × the meter multiplier, unchanged; an **unmetered** service takes it from a count and **never consults a meter**. Both still refuse rather than default — an unmetered service whose count cannot be determined stops the bill exactly as an unstated multiplier does. ⚠ **THE SECOND HALF IS NOT RULED: WHERE THE COUNT COMES FROM.** Three options were put to the PO with the worked figure — three carts at `$15.00` must bill `$45.00` — and **only the metered/unmetered declaration was ruled**: **(A)** count the cart ASSETS on the unit (metering owns assets, B2 r4), so the number is observed and adding a cart changes the next bill by itself · **(B)** a `quantity` on the plan's service line, cheapest but typed by staff and able to disagree silently with the carts in the street · **(C)** model each cart as a service point with a read of 1, which puts fictitious rows in `meter_reads` against hard rule 3. **The lane recommends (A)**; it is **OQ-283** and must not be chosen by a lane. ⚠ **AMENDED 2026-09-02 — THE DECLARATION IS THREE STATES, NOT TWO, AND THE BOOLEAN THIS RULE ORIGINALLY NAMED IS SUPERSEDED (`DEF-2409`).** PO, verbatim: *"this says, metered or unmeters, but we agreed, we will have 3 states - meter unmetered and % oaf service"*, then *"Shall we consider Metered, % of service and Capacity based"*. The field is `billing_basis` and its three values are **`metered`** (a register is read) · **`share`** (a percentage of ANOTHER service's charge) · **`capacity`** (priced from an installed size or a count). **SEWER IS WHY TWO WERE NOT ENOUGH, AND IT IS A MONEY PATH.** The boolean's `false` side means *priced from a COUNT* — BR-RAT-090 says so in terms — and sewer is neither metered nor counted: it is **40% of the water usage charge** (BR-RAT-015 ❄️). So NEITHER value of the boolean was true for it, and a utility ticking *Unmetered* to stop the run demanding a read was telling the engine to price sewer by counting carts. **Worked, and neither figure moves:** a three-cart household still owes **3 × $15.00 = $45.00** — that is now `capacity`, the state BR-RAT-090 always described — and sewer on a **$109.00** water usage charge still owes **$43.60**, which the boolean could not express at all. **NOTHING ABOUT THE QUANTITY SOURCE IS RE-OPENED:** BR-RAT-090 (option A, the observed asset count) is untouched and is the `capacity` branch's rule. **WHAT IS NOT RULED HERE:** which service a `share` service takes its percentage OF is BR-RAT-015's own figure for sewer and is not a per-tenant choice on this screen — no control offers it and none may be added without a ruling. ⚠ **SUPERSEDED IN ITS VOCABULARY BY `BR-TEN-1180` (PO ruling 2026-09-02, closing `DEF-2409`) — THE COLUMN NOW CARRIES THREE VALUES AND IS NAMED `tenant_services.billing_basis`.** What survives untouched is everything this rule got right: the declaration lives on the SERVICE, the run reads it instead of demanding a meter, and an unmetered service still refuses rather than defaulting. What moved is that *unmetered* was TWO answers wearing one word. PO verbatim: *"Shall we consider Metered, % of service and Capacity based"*. **Sewer is the case that forced it:** it reads no register, so on the boolean it had to be `metered=False` — and the false side MEANS `BR-RAT-090`'s asset COUNT, while a sewer connection has no assets and sewer is in fact a percentage of the water usage charge (`BR-RAT-015`). Neither value was true for it. The three values are `metered` · `share` · `capacity`; `capacity` is exactly what the old `False` meant here, so **every figure this rule and `BR-RAT-090` signed is unchanged** — 3 carts × `$15.00` = `$45.00` still. `TenantService.metered` survives as a read-only property answering this rule's own question, so no reader moved. | PO ruling 2026-08-20 (decision log) · CR-019 · DEF-161 (CRITICAL — this is its fix) · design §A2 R3b as corrected 2026-08-19 · BR-MET-056 (the multiplier is metering's) · BR-RAT-024 (the ten services) | **CONFIRMED (2026-08-20), AMENDED 2026-09-02 to THREE states (`DEF-2409`)** — the declaration. The quantity source is **OQ-283, CLOSED by BR-RAT-090** | `06-backend/tests/test_br_rat_080_081_separation.py` · `apps/rating/resolution.py` R3b · SET-03 |
| BR-RAT-082 | **APPROVAL IS TWO DIFFERENT ACTS: THE REGULATOR SIGNS OUTSIDE THE PRODUCT AND WE RECORD IT; THE INTERNAL SUBMIT→APPROVE GATE IS OURS. (PO ruling 2026-08-20 — option A.)** PO verbatim: _"this is correct; it is system of record so we can collect the details and keep it."_ **Nothing signed is reversed.** The 2026-08-08 R5 sweep removed **BOARD** approval as *"out-of-system (workflow removed)"*, flipping BR-RAT-030..040 — and a board signing outside the product is **not** the same act as the internal gate part 1 states plainly: _"Owner/Admin activate, Billing drafts."_ **What the product does:** it **captures and keeps** the regulator's sign-off as DATA — filing reference (`PUC #W-2025-1184`), authority, submitted date, approved date, approver name — and **processes nothing**. Same shape the PO ruled for AP/AR on 2026-08-12 (OQ-188): *"we can make this system of record … we dont have to process anything just capture."* **Worked, ratified:** Billing drafts v4 at +6% → submits → Owner approves → status stays `Draft` until **Jan 1 2027** → activates on its effective date. The regulator's sign-off is **a date and a docket number typed in, not a workflow step**. **Consequences:** `EP-RAT-16` stands (6 stories, 22 TCs); RAT-03 step 4 keeps *Effective & approval*; the Versions & filings tab keeps `FILED AS` and `BY`; r10's toast *"Filed for approval · activates when the regulator signs off"* is correct copy. | PO ruling 2026-08-20 (decision log) · decision log 2026-08-08 (board approval out-of-system — NOT reversed) · CR-013 part 1 · OQ-188 (the system-of-record shape) | **CONFIRMED (2026-08-20)** · ⚠ **BUILT 2026-08-21 (CR-013 P1 · RAT-03), and only the CAPTURE half exists.** The five things the rule names now have five columns: `regulator_ref` already held the filing reference, and `filing_authority` · `filed_on` · `regulator_approved_on` · `regulator_approver` were added by `0014_cr013_p1_filing_capture`. RAT-03 **step 4** is where they are typed, which is where this rule says they belong. **NOT ONE IS REQUIRED, and that is the rule rather than an omission** — BR-RAT-031 as-modified removed the in-system board-approval workflow and *"Nothing signed is reversed"*, so a required regulator field would rebuild it sideways: a schedule with no docket would stop activating, which is a gate nobody ratified. `regulator_approver` is TEXT and not a `User` FK because the person who signs is a commissioner at the authority, not a user of this product; `approved_by` remains the internal Owner/Admin and is a different fact about a different person. Under (i) the stored value is `1.508700` and the bill reads `$1.51`; under (ii) the stored value becomes `1.5087` and the sixth decimal is gone forever. **SCOPE — RATES ARE NOT IN THIS RULING.** It names *amount and percentage*; CR-013 part 1's _"rates stored to 6 decimals"_ restates frozen B4 and **stands unchanged**, so tier and TOU rates keep six. | PO ruling 2026-08-20 (decision log) · CR-013 part 2 (*"amounts 2 decimals, percentages 4"*) · B4 ❄️ (`NUMERIC(18,6)`) · DEF-291 · DEF-364 · OQ-285 | **CONFIRMED (2026-08-20) for DISPLAY. | BR-RAT-090 | ⚠⚠ **AMENDED 2026-10-02 (PO, `DEF-MET-9037`) — A METER IS NEVER IN THE COUNTED OR SUMMED POPULATION.** Verbatim: *"we are not doing net metering right now - we are doing capacity based panel - A · The net meter doesn't count toward capacity. Capacity is the panels or battery only."* The population a capacity service counts or sums (`assets.selectors._billable_assignments`) leaves out every device whose type's category is `meters` or `sub_meters`, so three 4 kW panels and an installed, unsized meter on one solar point bill **12 kW × $14.00 = `$168.00`** and never refuse (`installed_capacity_undetermined`). Built `2.440.0`, proved on PostgreSQL 16. ⚠⚠ **AMENDED 2026-09-30 (PO ruling, option B) — A COUNTED SERVICE CHARGES ITS BASE PER ASSET, NOT ONCE.** Put as *"Trash, fire and other counted services: is the base charge per asset or once?"*, worked on 2 trash carts at `$15.00` with a `$3.00` base: (A) base once, 2 × `$15.00` + `$3.00` = **`$33.00`** — today · (B) base per cart, 2 × (`$15.00` + `$3.00`) = **`$36.00`**. PO verbatim: *"B. Base per cart: 2 × ($15.00 + $3.00) = $36.00"*. It matches the same day's metered ruling (`BR-BIL-1049`: each meter its own line, tiers and base), so **every asset is its own connection**. **What it changes:** `gather._per_size_configs` charges the base only on the FIRST size group (`base_charge=None` on the rest) and a count multiplies usage only; both become per asset. **No seeded figure moves:** trash's base is `$0.00` (`BR-RAT-832`) and fire's `BASE-FIRE` is `$0.00`, so `3 × $15.00 = $45.00` and fire's `$84.00` stand (decision log 2026-09-30, the fire re-check); the difference appears only where a utility sets a non-zero base. **NOT BUILT — PO: *"don't fix anything yet"*.** ⚠ **AMENDED 2026-09-02 (`CR-062` D6, PO ruling) — A CAPACITY QUANTITY NOW COMES FROM ONE OF **THREE** PLACES, AND THIS RULE DESCRIBED ONLY THE FIRST TWO.** PO verbatim: _"In case of strom water - we have to take it from Unit - B ⭐ Take the size from the property, not from equipment. Storm water becomes the one capacity service whose quantity comes from the premises"_ — **option B of three, CORRECTED IN THE SAME SENTENCE from the property to the UNIT**, and that correction is the substance: a bill is for an account, an account serves a unit, and on the premises storm water would be the only line needing to be divided down before it could be charged. **The three sources:** (1) a **COUNT** of the assets on the unit — trash and fire, the BANDED services, unchanged and still `3 × $15.00 = $45.00`; (2) the **SUM of the installed capacity** those assets carry — solar, battery and broadband, unchanged and still `2 + 4 + 8 kW × $14.00 = $196.00`; (3) **a capacity recorded ON THE UNIT ITSELF** — storm water alone. **WHY THE THIRD EXISTS, AND IT IS NOT AN INCONSISTENCY TO BE TIDIED AWAY:** every other capacity service is carried by a device that holds its own size (`BR-AST-500`), and **storm water has no device** — an ERU measures hard surface on a property and nobody installs one. So the sum was `0` on every unit, the line printed **`$0.00`**, and no check in this repo could see it because the line exists, foots and prints (`DEF-2388`, now closed). The number lives in `service_characteristics` under the key `assessment` with `ERU` in `unit_of_measure` (`BR-MET-108`'s shape, keyed by unit and service; the unit of measure is DATA and never part of the key name). **Worked, and asserted live on a seeded tenant:** a house **1 ERU × $4.50 = `$4.50`** a month, the apartment block **`$27.00`** across its six flats, a commercial suite **`$18.00`**, the demo estate **59 ERU = `$265.50`** of assessment — **`$270.00`** collected, because `BR-RAT-101`'s outside-city 1.5x lands on two of those houses (`$6.75` each). ⚠ **The estate figure read `54 ERU` / `$243.00` / `$247.50` until 2026-09-02, when `CR-062`'s addendum appended two premises with units under it (`DEF-2368`); the PER-UNIT figures and this rule are unchanged, and no ❄️ frozen figure moved.** **A surcharge multiplies the PRICE and never the MEASUREMENT**, so the ruled figure is the QUANTITY and both are asserted separately. **THE ALLOCATION IS EQUAL ACROSS A PREMISES' UNITS** — the posture `MASTER_METER`'s `allocation_rule: "equal"` already takes for a block's water — so the block's 6 ERU is **1 ERU on each of six flats, not 6 on each**, which is the difference between `$27.00` a month and `$162.00`. **WHAT DID NOT CHANGE, and a pure case asserts it mechanically rather than by this sentence:** `connections.unit_capacity_key` names storm water and answers `None` for solar, battery, broadband, trash and fire, so the other four capacity services still read installed EQUIPMENT; an unrecorded assessment is `BR-MET-108`'s ruled zero (`$0.00` on the document, never a refusal), while a value that is not a number or is recorded in another unit of measure REFUSES by name, because reading either as zero would discard an answer the customer gave us. **NO ❄️ FROZEN B4/B5 FIGURE MOVES** — `GT-17`'s water **$39.50** and sewer **$15.80** are asserted per line on a bill that now also carries a `$4.50` storm-water line. ⚠ **THIS IS NOT AN ANSWER TO `OQ-370`** (which characteristic keys each flat service has, and what a settings form offers): storm water's key follows from a ruling, trash's and broadband's are still unruled, and `required_characteristic_keys` still refuses. **AN UNMETERED SERVICE'S QUANTITY IS A COUNT OF ITS ASSETS ON THE UNIT. (PO ruling 2026-08-20, option A — closes OQ-283.)** The count is **OBSERVED, never typed**: metering owns assets (B2 r4), so three carts on a unit bill three carts, and adding a cart in the field changes the next bill by itself. **Worked, ratified:** 3 carts × `$15.00` = **`$45.00`**; remove one and the next bill is `$30.00`. **WHY THIS AND NOT THE CHEAPER OPTION.** Option B — a `quantity` column on the plan's service line — was shown and rejected: a typed number can disagree silently with the carts actually in the street, and nothing in the product could tell. Option C — one service point per cart carrying a read of `1` — was rejected because it writes fictitious rows into `meter_reads` against hard rule 3. **This makes DEF-161's fix GENERAL**, not trash-specific: every unmetered service in the ten-service seed becomes billable by the same path. **R3b, for the unmetered branch:** billable quantity = the count of ACTIVE assets of the service's asset type on the unit, resolved at the service-period end date. It **refuses rather than defaults** exactly as the metered branch does — a unit whose cart count cannot be determined stops the bill, because "no carts recorded" and "zero carts" are different facts and only one of them is billable. ⚠ **`tenant_services.metered` IS NOW `tenant_services.billing_basis` AND CARRIES THREE VALUES — `BR-TEN-1180`, PO ruling 2026-09-02, closing `DEF-2409`** (`metered` · `share` · `capacity`). This rule is unchanged in substance: `capacity` is precisely what the old `metered=False` meant, and `TenantService.metered` survives as a read-only property, so every reader and every signed figure stands. The value it must NOT be read as is `share` — sewer is a percentage of the water usage charge (`BR-RAT-015`) and has no assets to count. ⚠ **AMENDED 2026-09-02 (`CR-062` tasks D1 + H2) — THE COUNT IS ONLY HALF OF THIS RULE NOW. FOUR OF THE SIX CAPACITY SERVICES BILL THE *SUM* OF THEIR INSTALLED CAPACITY.** PO verbatim, 2026-09-02: *"In solar youa re still not doing it correctly - For example We have 10 Types of Solar panel with various capaity 1 Kw, 2Kw 3Kw 4Kw so and SO forth ; on given Unit we have installed 3 panels with 2Kw, 4Kw and 8 Kw , it means the totla capacity will be 14 Kw ; rate will be for 1 Kw = 14 $ per KW then bill should be 14 X 14 = 156 $"*. **Worked, with the arithmetic corrected: 2 + 4 + 8 kW is 14 kW, and 14 kW x `$14.00` is `$196.00`** — the PO was told so in the same session and did not dispute it, and `$156` is written nowhere in this repo. Under the COUNT this rule originally described, that same array billed **3 x `$14.00` = `$42.00`**, a **78% undercharge** on every solar customer, because a panel type is a LABEL and you cannot add labels together. **SOLAR, BATTERY, STORM WATER AND BROADBAND SUM** — in `kW`, `kW`, `ERU` and `Mbps` respectively. ⚠ **TRASH AND FIRE STILL COUNT AND NEITHER FIGURE MOVES.** PO statement (3): *"Trash - it is capacity based and Cart sizes will decide price X how many Cart"* — three carts x `$15.00` = **`$45.00`**, unchanged — and `CR-063` rules fire *"exactly like trash"*, so one 6" line plus two private hydrants is `$60.00 + 2 x $25.00` = **`$110.00`**, unchanged. ⚠ **STALE SINCE 2026-09-03 (stamped 2026-09-30):** the PO withdrew the private hydrant (*"remove private hydrants from list"*), so that warehouse is `$60.00` on one line, and the two-line case is a 6" line with two 2" lines, `$60.00 + 2 × $12.00` = **`$84.00`** (`tests/test_cr063_c3_fire_priced.py`). Base per asset (2026-09-30) does not move it: fire's seeded base is `$0.00`. Trash cannot be summed even in principle: three of its rows are CARTS in gallons and four are DUMPSTERS in cubic yards, and there is no unit in which their total is a quantity. **WHICH ARITHMETIC A SERVICE GETS IS READ OFF CONFIGURATION AND NEVER OFF A LIST OF SERVICE NAMES**, and it is two questions, both of which must answer yes: **(1)** do this service's ratified size classes carry a summable capacity at all — `BR-TEN-171` as `service_size_classes.capacity_uom_for_service` publishes it, which answers `None` for every BANDED service including trash, fire and water; and **(2)** is that the unit *this tariff* quotes its price per. **That second question is what leaves B5 `GT-15` untouched:** its utility prices solar per `panel`, which is not the unit solar's capacities sum in, so its quantity is still the COUNT and its **`$40.00` solar and `$24.00` battery are unmoved** — which matters because `GT-15`'s `$40.00` sits inside `GT-17`'s SNOWFLAKE-FROZEN taxable base of `221.90`. It is also why the ELEVENTH service landed on the counting side with no edit to any billing file, and a twelfth will land on whichever side its own rows put it. **A CAPACITY THAT CANNOT BE STATED REFUSES, exactly as an absent count does:** where an asset records no size class, or one this service does not have, the account stops by its own name (`installed_capacity_undetermined`) rather than totalling the equipment the product does recognise — three panels of 2, 4 and 8 kW with only one recorded would bill 2 kW for a 14 kW array, on a document that foots and prints. **A unit with NO capacity equipment sums to `0` and BILLS** (`BR-MET-108`'s ruled zero): the utility looked and there is nothing there. ⚠ **THE VISIBILITY HALF OF THAT RULED ZERO IS NOT DELIVERED AND IS `DEF-3084`** — the bill totals `$0.00` correctly and carries no `$0.00` LINE, for a capacity service and for trash alike, which predates this change. ⚠ **AMENDED 2026-09-02 (`CR-063` C3/C5) — FOR THE COUNTING PAIR THE COUNT IS PER SIZE, AND THE BILL CARRIES ONE LINE PER SIZE.** Decision log 2026-09-02, which narrowed *"one connection, one size"* to METERED connections and left this rule's arithmetic untouched: **a capacity service's quantity is a COUNT for trash and fire and a SUM for the other four, and for the counting pair it is a count PER SIZE, not a count per connection.** The reason is that the size belongs to the ITEM rather than to the connection — a cart is 96 gallons, a hydrant is a hydrant — so nothing picks a ladder and each item carries its own price. **Worked, on `CR-063`'s demo warehouse: one 6" fire line and two private hydrants bill `1 × $60.00` and `2 × $25.00` on TWO LINES, `$110.00` in total** ⚠ **STALE SINCE 2026-09-03 (stamped 2026-09-30):** the PO withdrew the | BR-RAT-091 | **THE SECOND-APPROVAL THRESHOLDS, RULED (PO 2026-08-20 — closes OQ-284).** BR-RAT-083 ruled the mechanism; these are the figures. **(1) DISCOUNT SUBSIDY: `$500` per month.** A discount whose projected monthly subsidy exceeds `$500` needs a second approval. **Shown to the PO with the fixture figures:** `SENIOR` costs **`$930`/mo** across 72 accounts and `CAP` at −$25.00 × 38 costs **`$950`/mo`**, so `$500` catches both and `$1,000` would catch neither — the PO chose the figure that stops both. ⚠ **AND IT DOES NOT SAY WHAT HAPPENS TO BR-RAT-077, WHICH IS WHAT THE CODE IMPLEMENTS — `OQ-290`, OPEN (stamped 2026-08-21 by the DIS-02 lane).** BR-RAT-077 (CONFIRMED the day before) makes the discount threshold a TENANT SETTING defaulting to **$10,000**, measured against the discount's own **budget cap**; this rule measures the **projected monthly subsidy** against **$500**. Different quantity, different figure, neither withdrawn. **PAPERLESS is the case that separates them: its seeded budget of `$7,332` a year is `$611` a month — over `$500`, under `$10,000`.** SENIOR (`$1,026`/mo) and CAP (`$950`/mo) trip both, which is why the figures shown when this rule was ruled did not surface the conflict. ⚠ **AND THIS RULE CANNOT BE EVALUATED BY ANY CODE PATH TODAY: nothing projects a monthly subsidy** (DEF-402), and `second_approval_required` reads the budget cap. ⚠ **Those two figures are r10's FIXTURE, not live data: the live tenant carries ZERO discounts**, and that was stated when the option was put. **(2) RATE CHANGE: worst-case bill change above `10%`.** Not annualised revenue and not accounts-affected — both were offered. **Worked against our own fixture:** the water +6% filing moves an 11 kgal household `$59.00 → $62.54`, `+$3.54`/month, `$38,826`/year across 914 accounts — and **it FLOWS**, because 6% is under the bar. The threshold is deliberately set where a routine annual filing passes and a repricing does not. RAT-03 step 4's mandatory impact analysis already computes the figure the gate reads. **(3) WAIVERS: NO GLOBAL THRESHOLD.** PO verbatim: *"understood; you can remove it if required."* Waivers stay governed **per charge code** by part 2's waiver policy — who may waive, whether a supervisor is needed, whether a reason code is required — so a `$30.00` NSF waiver answers to `NSF`'s own policy and not to a number here. The rule reads 'RAT-03 step 4's mandatory impact analysis already computes the figure the gate reads', and it does not: no endpoint computes a worst-case bill change, so the gate reads nothing and cannot fire (DEF-434). Clause 1 IS enforced … so a two-figure rule reads as done while half of it is a screen constant."* **WHAT MAKES IT TRUE NOW.** `GET /rate-schedules/{id}/impact` (`selectors.rate_change_impact`) prices every affected account **twice** — once under the version in force and once under the version being filed — through `engine.price_period`, the same call a bill run makes, configured by `selectors.pricing_inputs_for_version`, which is `pricing_inputs`' own body split out so ONE projection of a stored version reaches the engine. The figure the gate reads is that computation's `worst_case_pct`. **THE BAR NOW LIVES SERVER-SIDE**, `apps/rating/impact.RATE_CHANGE_SECOND_APPROVAL_PCT = Decimal("10")`, and `apps/rating/services._guard_rate_change_second_approval` REFUSES `POST /rate-schedules/{id}/versions` above it — mirroring how clause 1 is enforced by `discount_services.second_approval_required`, whose `(required, threshold)` tuple shape is copied rather than re-invented. **It is NOT a tenant Param**, deliberately: BR-RAT-077's threshold is one because the PO ruled it so in as many words; no such ruling exists for the rate half, and making a stated figure configurable would invent a rule. `RATE_CHANGE_SECOND_APPROVAL_PCT = 10` survives in RAT-03 as the documented MIRROR and the screen reads `second_approval_threshold_pct_display` off the endpoint. `second_approval_required(None)` returns **`null` rather than `false`** on BOTH sides of the wire, so nothing — screen, API answer or audit row — can report an unmeasured filing as clear. **THE RATIFIED WORKED EXAMPLE IS ASSERTED BY THE ENGINE AND NOT BY A LITERAL:** `tests/test_br_rat_091_rate_change_impact.py` builds a two-block inclining water rate (`0–5 kgal @ $4.00`, `5+ @ $6.50`), and the engine prices an 11 kgal household at **`$59.00`**; the **+6%** filing (`$4.24` / `$6.89`) prices the same household at **`$62.54`** — `+$3.54`/month, `6.000000%`, `$38,826.72`/year across 914 accounts — and it **FLOWS**. `10.5%` is **REFUSED**, `10%` exactly (`$59.00 → $64.90`) **FLOWS**, because the rule says *above*. ⚠ **AND THE REFUSAL IS PENDING `OQ-318`, NOT SETTLED.** This rule says such a filing *needs a second approval*; **BR-RAT-031 as-modified deleted the rate-schedule approval workflow**, so there is nowhere for one to land — no `status` on `tariff_versions`, no approve endpoint, and `approved_by` explicitly *"never a gate"*. One rule, no per-column exceptions to remember. **CHECKED BEFORE RECORDING, AND SAFE FOR STORED VALUES:** no figure in `core/sample_fixture.py` and **no expected value anywhere in `05-golden-tests.md` carries more than four decimals**, so narrowing persists every number the product holds today without changing one of them. At `NUMERIC(18,4)` it truncates to `0.0625`, which is only **two** decimals of percent, and the loss is silent — the column is well-formed, the bill is produced, and the rate is wrong in the fourth significant digit. `charges.percent` stores a fraction too (`0.015` = 1.5%). **AND THE PRODUCT ALREADY USES BOTH CONVENTIONS:** `tariff_versions.percent` stores percent **POINTS** (`40` = 40%, BR-RAT-015 / GT-17 ❄️) while `taxes.rate` and `charges.percent` store fractions — documented in `core/sample_fixture.py` lines 197 and 204. **So the ruling is recorded in full and the fraction columns are held back pending `OQ-286`**, which asks the one question that settles it: narrow those to 6, or convert them to percent points so 4 is uniform. | PO ruling 2026-08-20 (decision log) · BR-RAT-089 (widened) · B4 ❄️ · CLAUDE.md hard rule 2 (`NUMERIC(18,6)` — needs the same addendum) · CR-013 part 4 · BR-RAT-015 · OQ-286 | **CONFIRMED (2026-08-20). OQ-286 CLOSED the same day** — PO verbatim: *"for perctnage you can have 6 decimal places"*, i.e. option (A): fractions keep six and every other money column narrows to four. **No data conversion, and every fraction column stays exactly as it is.** ⚠ **THE RULE HAS THREE KINDS, NOT TWO**, because "finance field" is not one thing: **AMOUNT** (money you can hold) → 4 · **PERCENT** (a proportion) → 6 · **RATE** (a price per unit) → 6, the last because CR-013 part 1 restates ❄️ B4 for exactly those and because a rate is MULTIPLIED by a quantity before it becomes money — truncating a rate moves the bill, where truncating an amount only moves itself. All three now live in `06-backend/core/money_fields.py` and nowhere else, so the 56 columns stop being 56 chances to be wrong.** Needs a signed addendum to B4 and to CLAUDE.md hard rule 2, via HUMAN-GUIDE §6 — the PO's own signature | 56 money columns · `taxes.rate` · `charges.percent` |
