Sewer keys under `resolution_catalogue.NO_SIZE_AXIS` (the empty string, which is already what `resolution._size_key` produces for a fact carrying no size), so a blank size RESOLVES for it. **A consequence the PO ruled on with its cost stated — _"Read the second way, the system refuses bills that price perfectly well today"_ — is that a BANDED service whose schedule version configures NO size now serves none and refuses every meter by name.** That is why nothing is switched on: the chain still has no production caller, `06-backend/tests/test_br_rat_120_size_refusal.py` TC6 asserts the only `build_catalogue` caller in `apps/` is the shadow, and **no bill changed**. **THREE CASES THIS DOES NOT CHANGE, each already settled in `apps/tenancy/service_size_classes.py`, whose own docstring is the authority — _"an empty tuple means 'ruled to have none' (sewer) and a raise means 'nobody has said'… the two readings must never collapse"_:** a service **ruled to have no bands** (sewer) prices from the single ladder and a blank size is CORRECT; **battery** (`OQ-561`) and **thermal steam** (`OQ-560`) keep raising `SizeClassNotRuled`, which stays *money nobody has priced* and never becomes *no size*. ⚠ **SEQUENCING, AND IT IS PART OF THE RULE:** a banded service whose meter has **no size recorded** must refuse too — you cannot charge on a size you do not have — **but not one meter carries a size yet**, so the sizes are loaded FIRST (the import column and screen field the PO ruled the same day, `DEF-1900`) and the chain is switched in AFTER. Switching first would refuse every bill on every tenant. **No bill changes on the day of this ruling: the chain still has no production caller.** ⚠⚠ **AMENDED 2026-09-01 (PO ruling, `CR-057`, `DEF-2923`) — THE SEQUENCING CLAUSE IS SPENT AND THE CHAIN NOW HAS A PRODUCTION CALLER. NOTHING ABOVE IS DELETED; THE TWO SENTENCES THAT ARE NO LONGER TRUE ARE NAMED HERE.** The PO ruled the pair in — _"Can you wire them in now - R3 and R4"_, and after being told the first flip broke things, _"can you fix this probelm and again try to siwthicing - R3 and R4"_. **(1) *"the chain still has no production caller"* is SUPERSEDED**: `BILLING_RESOLUTION_STEPS` and `resolution_switch.SERVED_BY_CHAIN_DEFAULT` both ship as `"R3,R4"`, so `apps/billing/resolution_switch.py` is a real caller on every deployment that does not turn it off, and **R3 and R4 price real bills**. **(2) *"No bill changes"* is SUPERSEDED as a standing claim** and survives only as the measurement it was: on the three utilities measured (163 accounts) the switched fork returned the SAME base charge on 163 of 163, moved 0 and refused 0. **WHAT THE SEQUENCING CLAUSE ASKED FOR WAS DONE IN THE ORDER IT ASKED.** Sizes first: `BR-RAT-1010` (PO 2026-09-01) moved R3's axis onto the charge code, and the per-utility coverage report reads **0 (service · size) pairs with nothing pricing them** across all three utilities. Chain after: the switch was flipped only once that read clear. **THE COST CLAUSE IS NOW LIVE RATHER THAN HYPOTHETICAL, AND THIS IS THE SENTENCE A UTILITY NEEDS:** a BANDED service whose schedule version configures no size, and whose `is_base` charge code states no per-size table, serves no size and **refuses every meter of that service by name on a REAL bill run** — no fallback (`BR-BIL-872`). That is `BR-RAT-120`'s own accepted cost with the *"nothing is switched on"* qualifier removed. ⚠ **AND A SECOND PRECONDITION WAS FOUND BY DOING IT, NOT BY READING: `DEF-2923`.** A tenant that has declared NO `is_base` charge code for a service had every account of that service refused the moment R4 was served, because `BR-RAT-088` makes the code the only source of a base. That refusal now fires at **schedule activation** instead — `apps/rating/services.py::_guard_base_charge_code` consults `steps_served_by_chain()` — so the utility is told on a settings screen, with the fix in the sentence, rather than on the day the money is due. **ROLLBACK, one line, no release:** `SBH_BILLING_RESOLUTION_STEPS=` emptied; the setting is re-read on every call.  ⚠ **NARROWED 2026-10-01 TO SIZE-PRICED BASES — NOT SUPERSEDED (`DEF-RAT-8822`, PO option A, verbatim: _"If the base differs by size, it's still held as today, so nobody is billed the wrong size's rate"_).** A meter with NO recorded size on a banded service is refused only where the base charge code prices per size (worked: `BASE-W` 5/8" $8.00, 2" $34.00 → held, as before). Where the base code is one *All sizes* price, the unsized meter is billed with no minimum and the Check warning *Meter size not recorded* (`BR-RAT-1056`: $8.00 + 1 kgal × $4.4096 = **$12.41**). A size that IS recorded and is absent from the configured axis still refuses by name, exactly as above — this narrowing touches only the blank size. | PO ruling 2026-08-28 (decision log) · **confirms `BR-RAT-088`'s refusal clause** · `DEF-1810` (CLOSED) · `DEF-1900` (the doors) · `apps/tenancy/service_size_classes.py` (`OQ-560`/`OQ-561` untouched) | **CONFIRMED (2026-08-28)** | `apps/rating/resolution.py` R3 · `resolution_catalogue.schedule_version_facts` · `06-backend/tests/test_def_1810_disagreements.py` (pins the $8.00/$8.00 pair today) |
| BR-RAT-730 | **THE TENANT'S OWN DECLARATION DECIDES WHICH CHARGE CODES RIDE AN ORDINARY PERIODIC BILL — R4 READS `charges.charge_type` AND `charges.auto_apply`, AND `is_base` RIDES WITHOUT THEM. (Executes `BR-RAT-117`, PO ruling 2026-08-22; NO NEW PO RULING IS CLAIMED.)** PO verbatim (2026-08-22): _"ruccuring and Autom apply land on bill"_. ⚠ **THIS IS `BR-RAT-117` REACHING R4, NOT A NEW RULE.** `BR-RAT-117` was built into `gather.py`'s fee-line reader on 2026-08-22 and **never reached `resolution.resolve_charge_codes`**, which narrowed by service, customer class and connection size and by **nothing else** (`DEF-493`). **Worked, measured on a provisioned tenant's own rows:** 23 charge codes; R4 resolved **18 of them, totalling `$799.95` — a `$250.00` tamper fee, three `$45.00` fees, a `$30.00` returned-payment fee and thirteen more — onto a monthly bill whose base is `$8.00`, roughly one hundred times the base.** After this rule: **0 codes ride and `$0.00` is added**, because not one of that tenant's 23 codes is both `recurring` and `auto_apply`. **THE RULE, IN TWO PARTS.** **(1) A code rides when it is `charge_type='recurring'` AND `auto_apply` — BOTH, and the conjunction is the whole of it.** Recurring-but-not-auto-apply and auto-apply-but-one-time each wait for their own trigger, which is what `charge_type` is for. **(2) A code declaring `is_base` rides REGARDLESS of the two flags** — `BR-RAT-088` made the charge code the one source of a service's base and `resolve_base_charge` reads R4's OUTPUT, so gating the base on `auto_apply` is a base that does not exist. `BR-RAT-117` forbids it in terms: _"⚠ THIS DOES NOT MOVE THE BASE"_. **The figure for getting that wrong:** the seeder writes `BASE-W`, `BASE-W-COM`, `BASE-S` and `BASE-T` as `recurring` with `auto_apply` **unset**, so gating them takes `$8.00` residential water, `$15.00` commercial water and `$18.00` trash to **`$0.00`** — `DEF-154`'s exact shape. **NOT RIDING IS NOT AN ERROR.** A `one_time` connection fee is simply not selected and the bill proceeds; refusing it would stop every bill on every tenant, since 18 of the 23 provisioned codes are exactly that. **⚠ `auto_apply = False` IS READ AS A DECLARATION, NOT AS A GAP, AND NOTHING IS BACKFILLED.** The column defaults False and no seeded row sets it, so *nobody ticked it* and *the utility decided this does not auto-apply* are the same stored value. The safe direction is taken deliberately: under-billing a `$2.00` paper-bill fee is fixed by ticking a box next cycle (and `BR-RAT-117` already priced that direction at `$14,964.00` a month for a `$12.00` fee across 1,247 connections), while over-billing `$799.95` is a credit note per customer under hard rule 4, since an issued invoice is immutable. The checkbox is live on the charge-code wizard and the type is a column on the list, so the utility can express the answer today; nothing is unreachable. **⚠ AN UNSTATED `charge_type` DOES NOT RIDE AND IS NOT READ AS `recurring`** — the chain has no fallback and no default. **⚠ AND `auto_apply` ON A NON-RECURRING CODE IS A CONFLICT, NOT A CASTING VOTE:** both columns were filled in by a person and they contradict each other — *fire on an event* against *fire on every bill*, a `$250.00` tamper fee on one bill or on twelve — so R4 refuses by name (`ChargeCodeRecurrenceConflict`) rather than discarding one person's answer. It cannot fire on any tenant provisioned today. **⚠ NOTHING IS SWITCHED ON AND NO BILL CHANGES:** R4 still has no production caller (`BR-RAT-120`, and `test_br_rat_120_size_refusal.py` TC6 asserts it), so this is what R4 WILL do when step 5 of the cutover reaches it. ⚠ **AMENDED 2026-09-01 (PO ruling, `CR-057`): THE *"NOTHING IS SWITCHED ON"* CLAUSE IN THIS RULE IS SUPERSEDED — R3 AND R4 NOW PRICE REAL BILLS.** `BILLING_RESOLUTION_STEPS` ships `"R3,R4"`, so `apps/billing/resolution_switch.py` is a production caller of the chain. Nothing else in this rule moves — what it says R4 or R3 WILL do is what they now DO. Measured before the flip: 3 utilities, 163 accounts, **163 unchanged, 0 moved, 0 refused**. Rollback is `SBH_BILLING_RESOLUTION_STEPS=` emptied, one line, no release. See `BR-RAT-120` and `BR-BIL-870` for the full amendment. | **`BR-RAT-117`** (PO ruling 2026-08-22, the signed rule this executes) · decision log 2026-08-27 §3 (`DEF-493` decided without the PO — *"choosing between an existing signed mechanism and a new one is a build judgment"*) · `BR-RAT-088` (the base exemption) · `BR-RAT-120` (the same shape one step earlier: the tenant's configuration is the answer) · `DEF-493` (CLOSED) · `DEF-154` (what gating the base costs) | **CONFIRMED (2026-08-22, executed 2026-08-28)** | `apps/rating/resolution.py` `rides_a_periodic_bill` / `resolve_charge_codes` · `apps/rating/resolution_catalogue.py` `charge_code_facts` · `06-backend/tests/test_def_498_def_493_charge_code_recurrence.py` (TC7-TC15) |
| BR-RAT-731 | **R4 STATES ONE AMOUNT OR REFUSES BY THE NAME OF THE RULE THAT IS IN THE WAY — IT NEVER BLAMES THE CONNECTION SIZE FOR A PERCENTAGE. (Lane judgment 2026-08-28, `DEF-498`; no PO ruling is claimed and no figure moves.)** `charges.amount_rule` has stored four rules since r6.6 — `flat` | `percent` | `percent_with_minimum` | `formula` — and `resolution.ChargeCode` carried a flat amount and a per-size table and **nothing else**, so all four inexpressible cases arrived identically (`flat_amount=None`, empty `amounts`) and were refused with ONE sentence: _"R4: charge code 'LATE' applies to this account and states no amount for connection size None on service None"_. **`LATE` is 1.5% of what is overdue with a `$5.00` floor** (`BR-BIL-001`'s _max($5, 1.5% × overdue)_) and is an account-level code, so `sizes.get(None or "")` is None for **every account that has ever existed** — the refusal was unconditional AND it named a fact that has nothing to do with the problem, sending a utility to look at a meter. **Measured: 23 charge codes on a provisioned tenant, 5 unpriceable — `LATE`, `TAP`, `IMPACT`, `DAMAGE`, `BULK-WATER` — each refusing the WHOLE bill by name. After: 0 of 23.** **THE RULE: the contract carries `amount_rule`, `percent`, `min_amount` and `max_amount`, and each refusal says what is actually true of that rule.** **⚠ CARRYING THE COLUMNS DOES NOT MAKE R4 PRICE A PERCENTAGE, AND IT MUST NOT.** A percentage is a fraction of a **BALANCE**, and a balance is a BILLING fact that R1–R7 is never handed — the chain resolves frozen RATING facts. Naming a basis here would be inventing one, and the plausible candidates (this bill's total? the account balance?) differ by real money on every account. So a percent code refuses **naming the percentage and the missing balance**, and is billed by the rule that knows the balance (`BR-BIL-001` for the late fee). `formula` refuses naming `BR-RAT-041`, which reserves it visible-but-disabled. **⚠ A CODE WITH NO RULE AND NO AMOUNT STATES BOTH READINGS AND CLAIMS NEITHER (`DEF-2340`):** a code the PO RULED unpriced (CR-010 A2, 2026-08-17, _"actual cost, entered per case"_ — `TAP`, `IMPACT`, `DAMAGE`, `BULK-WATER`) and a code nobody has priced are the SAME stored row, because `charges` has no column for the per-case posture. That is the collapse `apps/tenancy/service_size_classes.py` names as forbidden — _"the two readings must never collapse"_ — and until it has a column the honest sentence asserts neither. **⚠ WHY THE FIVE REFUSALS WENT AWAY WITHOUT ANY OF THEM BEING PRICED:** every one is `one_time` or `penalty`, so `BR-RAT-730`'s narrowing drops them BEFORE the amount is asked for. **The order of the two steps is part of the rule** — ask *what does it cost* first and `DEF-498`'s refusal stands back in front of `DEF-493`'s narrowing, which is the state where _fixing either alone turns a refusal into an overcharge_. | `DEF-498` (CLOSED) · `DEF-2340` (the missing column) · `BR-RAT-041` (`formula` reserved) · `BR-BIL-001` (the late fee) · CR-010 A2 (PO 2026-08-17, `per_case` ratified as unpriced) · design §A2 (_"no amount => REFUSE"_) | **CONFIRMED (2026-08-28)** — lane judgment, recorded rather than asked (hard-won law 13) | `apps/rating/resolution.py` `charge_code_amount` · `ChargeCodeAmountMissing` · `06-backend/tests/test_def_498_def_493_charge_code_recurrence.py` (TC1-TC6) |

So both sources stand, they agree to the cent by construction, and `tests/test_def455_def254_base_codes_and_tax_silence.py::test_def455_tc4` goes RED the moment they drift — making it the safe place to delete the plan's figure from, one commit after R4 gains a production caller. ⚠ **THE PER-SIZE ROWS GOT THEIR WRITER ON 2026-08-28 (`BR-RAT-690`, `DEF-2190` CLOSED).** This sentence used to read *"`charge_code_amounts` HAS NO WRITER AT ANY LAYER — a model, a migration and two readers, and `CHARGE_FIELDS` carries no `size_amounts` key, so the per-size base this rule works its example on cannot be seeded through the validated write path at all"*, and it was true for eight days. `CHARGE_FIELDS` now carries `size_amounts`, `_validate_size_amounts` refuses a size the tenant has not configured, and CHG-02 step 2 draws the table — so **this rule's own example is expressible: `BASE-W` at `$8.00` for a 5/8" and `$34.00` for a 2"**, proved through the chain (`resolve_charge_codes` answered `$8.00`/`$8.00` before and `$8.00`/`$34.00` after). ⚠ **SUPERSEDED 2026-09-07 BY PO RULING (`DEF-6440`) — THE SHIPPED SEED NOW PRICES PER SIZE.** This clause read *"THE SHIPPED SEED STILL PRICES FLAT AND THAT IS DELIBERATE: `sample_data.py`'s `BASE-W $8.00` is `DEF-455`'s own figure and moving it to a per-size table would change what PLN-03 and the demo bill show **for a reason no ruling asked for**. The table is EMPTY on a fresh tenant until a utility prices it"*. **A ruling has now asked for it**, PO verbatim: *"We need to add this data for water - and all other services - base charge amounts"*, said while looking at the empty table. The reasoning was sound and is quoted rather than deleted (hard-won law 1), and the ONE thing it was protecting is protected by construction instead: the ladder is anchored on each code's own current figure, so the smallest size still states `$8.00` and PLN-03, the demo bill and every ❄️ figure are unchanged to the cent — which they must be, because a code that disagrees with `plan_services.base_charge` REFUSES the account by this rule's own *"migration artefact"* clause. **Seeded now:** `5/8" $8.00 · 3/4" $10.00 · 1" $14.00 · 1½" $24.00 · 2" $34.00 · 3" $68.00 · 4" $104.00 · 5" $154.00 · 6" $204.00` on `BASE-W`, the same ladder at `$15.00` on `BASE-W-COM`, six electric bands on each of the three electric codes and four gas bands — **40 rows on an eleven-service tenant where there were 0 on every tenant ever created**. The ladder, its capacity basis and the one figure pinned to this register rather than to the formula are `apps/rating/base_size_amounts.py`; the amounts and the reasoning are the decision log of 2026-09-07. ⚠ **EIGHT SERVICES ARE STILL FLAT AT `$0.00` AND THAT IS NOT AN OVERSIGHT** — trash by PO ruling (`OQ-317` option A, it bills per cart) and the other seven because no base has ever been signed for them; the figure is put to the PO in the review-ledger row rather than invented, because raising one moves `core/sample_fixture.CAPACITY_BASE` too or the bill refuses. ⚠ **AND THE RULING REACHED BOTH PRICING PATHS ON 2026-08-31, ELEVEN DAYS LATE (`DEF-2823` CLOSED, version `2.106.3`).** Until that day NEITHER path read the charge code: the live path read `plan_services.base_charge` (`BR-RAT-012`, superseded) and the ruled chain's **R3** read `tariff_versions.base_charge` — the column this very rule RETIRES — so R3 refused **47 of 47** accounts of the seeded utility while the live path billed all 47 correctly at `$8.00`. Every schedule version a real utility creates stores NULL there, because `RateScheduleWizard` dropped the control in this same ruling. **What landed, and it is this rule's own consequence clause executed literally:** R3 is now a MEMBERSHIP test — `resolution.resolve_served_size`, *does this schedule serve this size* — and R4 states the AMOUNT from the `is_base` code, per size where the code prices per size. The live path asks the same question through `rating.selectors.base_charge_from_charge_code`, a SECOND narrowing rather than a call into the chain, so the pricing shadow's R4 comparison can still fail. **The traversal, in the PO's own words of 2026-08-31:** *"We already agreed that it will be in charge code, per utility services , you ahve to take it from there, and plan will bind it , so plan -> charges code -> base scharges"* — the account resolves to a plan, the plan lists its SERVICES, and each service's base is the `is_base` code narrowed by customer class and connection size. **`plan_charge_rules` is NOT that binding**: `selectors.billable_charge_codes` excludes `is_base` rows by name (*"the base has its own route"*), `PlanWizard.servicesMissingBaseCharge` finds a service's base code by `is_base` + `service`, and the seeded fixture holds **9 binding rows of which 0 are `is_base`** while all 47 accounts resolve a base. **`plan_charge_rules.amount_override` is not consulted either** — PO 2026-08-19 ruling 1, *"no plan cannot override prices"*. **NO BILL MOVED, measured account by account:** all 47 ready accounts state the same figure on the plan and on the code, `10-scripts/def2799_live_base_charges.py` diffs empty across the change, `$390.00` across the run, and `golden-verify` reads `33 PASS / 0 FAIL` before and after — which is run but is NOT the evidence, because B5 hands the base to the engine directly and cannot see a sourcing fault. **A plan or schedule figure that DISAGREES with the code now REFUSES the bill by name**, which is this rule's *"migration artefact"* sentence applied to the plan as well as to the schedule. ⚠ **AMENDED-AS-CONFIRMED 2026-09-10 — THE PO NAMED THE WHOLE CHAIN, AND THE RATE SCHEDULE IS THE MIDDLE STEP.** PO verbatim: _"Charge Code -> base charge -> rate schedule -> plans - that is the path"_, and, on what a plan's charge step may do: _"Price -> charge code -> add 1 by 1 into plan - as per utility requirements. all Rules and price is defined in Charge code itself"_. **This CONFIRMS the rule rather than moving it** — the arithmetic and the source are unchanged — and it settles the one thing the rule left implicit: the base does not reach a plan directly, it reaches it THROUGH THE ATTACHED RATE SCHEDULE, which is what fixes the service and the size ladder that narrow WHICH charge-code figure applies. The schedule is the ROUTE and never a second place to type a figure — `tariff_versions.base_charge` stays retired, exactly as this rule already says. It is the same shape as the PO's minimum ruling of the same day (_"set it in rates and use it in plans"_): **one place to type, every other surface reads through.** ⚠ **AND IT SETTLES `then set the price` AGAINST `a plan composes; it does not price` — the PLAN WINS NOTHING.** The plan's charge step searches, picks and adds codes; the amount column is the charge code's own figure and is READ-ONLY there. `plan_services.base_charge` is therefore display-and-fallback only, and its DROP stays reserved under `BR-RAT-120`'s rollback clause, which this ruling did not address: emptying `SBH_BILLING_RESOLUTION_STEPS` turns the charge-code gate off entirely (`test_def2923_base_code_gate_follows_the_switch::TC3`), so a rolled-back deployment has no base source at all once the column is gone — `DEF-154`'s exact shape. |
| BR-RAT-094 | **A PLAN ATTACHES PRICES; IT NEVER SETS ONE — AND THE TWO OVERRIDE FIELDS GET DIFFERENT ANSWERS. (PO ruling 2026-08-21, option B — closes DEF-411.)** PO verbatim: _"(B) Keep the one that was signed off for its stated purpose, put a gate on it, and delete the tax one. Nobody has ever defended letting a plan skip a state tax."_ CR-013 part 5 forbids a plan inventing a price, and **two stored columns let it anyway**: `plan_charge_rules.amount_override` and `plan_tax_rules.rate_override`. They are not symmetrical, so they do not get the same treatment. **(1) `amount_override` SURVIVES, GATED TO `<= 0`.** BR-RAT-043 defends it for one stated purpose — *plan discounts = negative-amount charge rules on the plan* — so the discount half is ratified law and the raise half was never defended. **Worked, ratified:** Silver Creek's `CONN` connection fee is **`$85.00`** in the catalogue; the Small Commercial plan may write **`-$10.00`** and bill **`$75.00`**, and may NOT write `$95.00` — that write is now refused with `rating.above_maximum`, `maximum: 0`. **Zero is accepted**, because a zeroed charge is a 100% discount and not a price. **(2) `rate_override` IS REFUSED OUTRIGHT.** It is off the accepted field set for a plan tax rule and the read path serves `null`, so no screen can show one and no write can carry one. The case the PO named is real in our own suite: a `Senior` plan carried `rate_override: "0.00"` against the **6.25%** TX state utility tax — a plan zeroing a state tax. ⚠ **THIS AMENDED TWO TESTS THAT ASSERTED THE OLD BEHAVIOUR**, `US-RAT-008 TC4` (the `$85 → $95` raise) and `US-RAT-009 TC4` (the Senior zero). Both now assert the refusal in the same test rather than being deleted, so the superseded behaviour stays legible. ⚠ **THE COLUMNS ARE NOT DROPPED.** `rate_override` still exists on `plan_tax_rules` with every existing row intact; this rule closes the write path and the read path, which is what a ruling can do without a migration. | BR-RAT-095 | **THE CHARGE-TYPE SET IS FIVE, AND THE NAMING DOES NOT CHANGE (PO ruling 2026-08-21 — closes OQ-287).** The question had two halves and the PO gave them OPPOSITE answers. **(1) THE NAMING: NO CHANGE.** PO verbatim: _"No changes here"_. `refundable` is NOT renamed to *Deposit*; `penalty` is NOT renamed to *Event-driven*. **No enum value moves, no data migration runs, and none of the 23 seeded charge codes is touched** — which is why this half is free. **(2) THE FIFTH TYPE: ADD IT.** PO verbatim: _"add adjustment"_. `adjustment` is CREDITS AND CORRECTIONS, and it is the one genuinely new capability in CR-013 part 2's list of five. **Worked, ratified:** Mrs Alvarez's meter over-read in July and she was billed **`$74.60`** on 3,200 gallons she never used. Putting **`-$74.60`** back had no home: filed as a negative `penalty` it puts a customer credit in the LATE-FEE bucket and the late-fee report is wrong by that amount; filed as a negative `one_time` it lands in connection fees; not filed at all, it becomes a cheque run for a customer who is staying. Same for a `$50.00` goodwill credit after a main break and a `-$120.00` rebilling correction. **`adjustment` is the only type whose amount is routinely NEGATIVE**, and hard rule 4 is unchanged by it: an issued invoice is never edited, so an adjustment is a NEW LINE on the next bill. | PO ruling 2026-08-21 (decision log) · `OQ-287` (CLOSED) · CR-013 part 2 · hard rule 4 | **CONFIRMED (2026-08-21)** | `CHARGE_TYPES` (server + client) · CHG-02 step 1 · the charge-codes landing filter · `ChargeCodeWizard.test.tsx::all FIVE types render` |

| BR-RAT-096 | **A PLAN BINDS TAXES FROM THE CATALOGUE; THE JURISDICTION STILL DECIDES WHETHER ONE REACHES A GIVEN PREMISES. BOTH GATES, NOT ONE REPLACING THE OTHER. The plan says WHICH of the tenant's tax codes it carries; the jurisdiction says whether that code applies here. **A tax must pass BOTH.** ⚠ **THE RISK THIS CREATES, STATED RATHER THAN DISCOVERED:** two accounts in the same jurisdiction on different plans can now be taxed differently, because one plan bound a tax and the other did not. That is the under-taxation CR-013 part 4 warned of, and it is now a configuration a utility can make. An override nobody can find later is indistinguishable from a gate that never fired. **Worked, at Silver Creek:** a rule `Meter size ≤ 1\"` on Residential Standard refuses **233 of 1,247 accounts**; each refusal is now readable and each override leaves a row an auditor can read. ⚠ **THE OVERRIDE IS PER ASSIGNMENT, NEVER PER RULE.** Overriding for one customer does not weaken the rule for the next — a 'remember this' would silently convert a gate back into the advisory BR-RAT-043 used to describe. | PO ruling 2026-08-21 (decision log) · `OQ-305` (CLOSED) · `OQ-304` (the gate this doors) · `BR-RAT-043` (enforcement superseded) · `BR-RAT-077` (the audited-self-approval precedent) · hard rule 7 | **CONFIRMED (2026-08-21)** | PLN-04 · PLN-03 step 7 · `DEF-404` · `audit_log` |

Neither was withdrawn, so `PLN-04` has been READING the figure and applying nothing, and a customer moving to a plan with a higher deposit was never asked for the difference. **THE RULING TAKES THE MIDDLE AND IT IS NOT A COMPROMISE — IT IS THE ONLY OPTION THAT KEEPS BOTH RULES' PURPOSES.** BR-CUS-042's purpose is that the deposit should match the plan; BR-CUS-062's is that a plan change must not generate a surprise demand. **Showing the figure and offering it as a NEXT-BILL LINE serves both**: the deposit catches up, and it arrives on a document the customer already expects rather than as a separate demand for money. **IT IS AN OPTION, NOT AN AUTOMATIC POSTING** — the PO's own words are *"ask them to add it"*, so staff decide per change and the decision is recorded. Declining is a legitimate outcome and leaves the deposit as it stands, which is BR-CUS-062's behaviour preserved as the fallback. **A NEGATIVE DIFFERENCE IS NOT A REFUND HERE**: a customer moving to a cheaper plan is over-deposited, and BR-CUS-025 already rules that a deposit settles at move-out — this ruling does not create an early refund path and none should be inferred. ⚠ **`0.00` AND *UNAVAILABLE* MUST STAY DIFFERENT ON SCREEN** (the reason `deposit_differential` is served nullable): a `0.00` says the two plans want the same deposit; a blank says nobody could compute it. | PO ruling 2026-08-21 (decision log) · `OQ-179` (CLOSED) · `BR-CUS-042` (purpose served, mechanism amended) · `BR-CUS-062` (SUPERSEDED as to *no recompute*; its no-surprise-demand purpose is what the next-bill option protects) · `BR-CUS-025` | **CONFIRMED (2026-08-21)** | PLN-04 · `plan_deposit_rules` · the next bill run |
| BR-RAT-101 | **THE INSIDE/OUTSIDE-CITY FLAG LIVES ON THE PREMISES, AND BILLING RESOLVES IT ACCOUNT → UNIT → PREMISES. (PO ruling 2026-08-21 — closes the storage half of `DEF-466`.)** PO verbatim: _"On premises we will add this flag and during billing we will consider Account -> Unit -> Premises to consider it"_. **WHY THIS WAS OPEN AT ALL:** `price_usage` has multiplied every base line and every usage line by `jurisdiction_factor` since it was written, and `gather` has never set it — so **every bill this product has ever issued priced at exactly `1.0`**. It was invisible precisely because it is the identity: the multiplication runs on every line and cannot fail. **WHAT THE RULING SETTLES:** the flag is a column on `premises` — metering's table (B2) — and the bill run reaches it along the path it already walks for everything else, `accounts.unit_id → units.premises_id`. That matches design §A1's own words: *"the premise's jurisdiction flag (inside / outside city) and the account's customer class select which rows fire"* — **the premises selects, the account's class selects, and the two are different objects.** It also matches `PremisesFacts.inside_city_limits` in `resolution.py`, which has always expected exactly this and had no column behind it. ⚠ **`None` IS A THIRD STATE AND MUST STAY ONE.** `inside_city_limits` is nullable: `True` inside, `False` outside, **`None` = nobody recorded it**. `resolution.money_factor` already refuses on `None` rather than assuming inside — and that is the safe direction, because assuming *inside* under-charges a customer the utility may be entitled to surcharge, while assuming *outside* over-charges one who never agreed to it. A default of either would be a silent pricing decision on data nobody entered. ⚠ **THIS RULING DOES NOT SAY WHAT THE FACTOR IS, OR WHERE IT LIVES — `OQ-308`.** A flag is a selector; the multiplier and its per-service exemptions are money. `Catalogue.outside_city_factor` is a single tenant scalar and design §A1's *"which rows fire"* reads as per-row instead; those are different products. Nothing is built on the money half until it is ruled. ⚠ **AMENDED 2026-08-21 (DEF-438 cutover step-5 lane) — THE FLAG IS NOW READ ON THE LIVE BILL PATH, AND IT STILL HAS NO WRITER (`DEF-517`).** `gather._premises_geography` carries `inside_city_limits` to `_jurisdiction_factor_for`, which applies `BR-RAT-102`'s two gates, so the flag decides MONEY from this commit and not only a tax. **And nothing in the product can set it:** `create_premises`/`update_premises` do not accept the field, no serializer carries it, PRM-01/PRM-02 draw no control and the seeder sets it on nothing — grep finds the model, the migration, four READERS and no writer. Every premises of every tenant is `None`. **That refuses no bill today** only because `BR-RAT-102`'s first gate is shut (no plan states a factor, `DEF-515`), which is the whole reason the plan gate is FIRST. ⚠ **AMENDED 2026-08-27 (CR-039 wave C task 10) — THE FLAG NOW HAS A WRITER AND THE PARAGRAPH ABOVE IS HISTORY.** `metering.services._validate_premises` accepts `inside_city_limits` on create and on PATCH as the SAME three states this rule names, `metering.selectors.premises_detail_dict` serves it (`DEF-507`, 2026-08-26) and `JurisdictionField` draws it on the S16 guided add and the PRM-01 edit modal. Three refusals are deliberate and each is a money argument: `1` and `0` are REFUSED rather than coerced, because Python makes `1 == True` and a lenient parser would price a spreadsheet cell as *inside the city*; a word it cannot read is an error rather than a guess, which is the posture the CSV importer's `PRM.VALUES_PARSE` already takes on this one column; and an ABSENT key on a PATCH leaves the answer alone, so a clerk correcting a street name cannot un-answer a pricing question. An explicit `null` DOES clear it back to unrecorded, which fails safe: the bill refuses by name rather than pricing on a value nobody stands behind. ⚠ **WHAT THE RULING STILL DOES NOT SAY, AND THIS LANE DID NOT DECIDE — `OQ-423`.** Changing the flag changes what the address is charged. The product applies it FORWARD ONLY and touches no issued invoice (CLAUDE.md hard rule 4); whether a correction owes an adjustment on bills already issued, and what happens to DRAFT bills in an open run, is on the PO's agenda. Worked so the stake is visible: `161 Silver Creek Rd`, June 2026, 18 kgal — water usage `$109.00` inside the city and `$163.50` outside, base `$8.00` and `$12.00`, **`$58.50` on one bill**. **What the ruling did not say and the lane decided (decision log 2026-08-21):** a `None` refusal is a reason-coded `GatherProblem`, so it blocks ONE ACCOUNT and the run continues (`BR-BIL-055`), landing on the S31.2 exception tab under `premises_jurisdiction_unstated` with the premises named by its address and `retryable=False`. | PO ruling 2026-08-21 (decision log) · `DEF-466` (storage half closed, factor half open) · `OQ-308` (new) · design §A1 · `resolution.PremisesFacts` · `resolution.money_factor` · B2 (premises is metering's) | **CONFIRMED (2026-08-21)** | `premises.inside_city_limits` · `gather._premises_for` · R5 |
| BR-RAT-102 | **THE PLAN DECIDES WHETHER AN OUTSIDE-CITY SURCHARGE APPLIES AND HOW MUCH. (PO ruling 2026-08-21 — closes `OQ-308` and the money half of `DEF-466`.)** PO verbatim: _"We can have it on plan itself - Plan will decide whether to apply and then how much"_. **This was a THIRD option and it is better than the two I offered.** I put up *one tenant scalar* and *per rate schedule*; the PO put it where the product already binds everything — which is BR-RAT-096's own sentence, *"plan all steps are bascailly binding everything together"*, applied to money. A tenant scalar cannot express two plans with different surcharges; a per-schedule factor makes the surcharge a property of the PRICING MATH, which it is not — it is a property of the DEAL, and a deal is a plan. **THE SHAPE, and it is two gates, not one:** the **PLAN** states the factor (absent or `1` = this plan does not surcharge); the **PREMISES** states inside or outside (BR-RAT-101); a line is surcharged only when the plan states a factor AND the premises is outside. **Worked:** Silver Creek's residential water at `$59.00` on a plan stating `1.15`, at an outside-city premises, bills **`$67.85`**; the same premises on a plan stating nothing bills `$59.00`. ⚠ **AND `charges.outside_city_applies` IS NOT SUPERSEDED — IT IS THE OTHER HALF.** That column EXISTS and R4 already honours it. The plan says WHAT the factor is; the charge code says whether THAT CHARGE is subject to it. Same two-gate shape as BR-RAT-096 (the plan binds a tax, the jurisdiction scopes it), and it is why this ruling does not leave the product with two answers to one question — they are answers to two questions. The per-SERVICE exemption R5 reads (`outside_city_exempt_services`) belongs on the plan's own service line, because the plan is what lists its services. ⚠ **R5 REFUSES RATHER THAN DEFAULTING, AND THAT SURVIVES:** a plan stating a factor over a premises whose flag is `None` **stops the bill** — `money_factor` raises `JurisdictionUnstated`, because the factor changes every line and a guess is a wrong bill rather than a partial one. A plan stating NO factor never consults the premises at all, so a utility that does not price by jurisdiction can never be refused by this step. ⚠ **AMENDED 2026-08-21 (DEF-438 cutover step-5 lane) — THE READ PATH IS BUILT ON THE LIVE BILL RUN, AND THE WRITE PATH DOES NOT EXIST (`DEF-515`).** `rating.selectors.plan_jurisdiction_pricing` serves the two stored facts, `gather._jurisdiction_factor_for` applies the two gates per service and sets `TariffConfig.jurisdiction_factor`, and `price_usage` has always multiplied the base and usage lines by it — so `DEF-466`'s dead identity is dead no longer. **The worked figure is now reproduced from `core/sample_fixture.py` rather than quoted:** an 11 kgal household is `5 × $4.00 + 6 × $6.50 = $59.00` usage over a `$8.00` base; at `1.15` outside the city that is **usage `$67.85`, base `$9.20`, bill `$67.00 → $77.05`** (`tests/test_def_484_size_and_jurisdiction.py`). **BUT NOTHING WRITES `rate_plans.outside_city_factor` OR `plan_services.outside_city_exempt` AT ANY LAYER** — no `PLAN_FIELDS` entry, no serializer, no wizard control, no seeder payload — so on every real tenant the factor is NULL, the premises is never consulted and not one figure moves. The tests write both columns through the ORM, which is precisely the untested-sourcing shape `DEF-466` was filed for, and is why `DEF-515` is a row and not a comment. **One clarification the ruling implies and does not state:** a stored `1` and an absent factor are folded into ONE shape (`None`) by the selector rather than at each call site, so a utility that typed `1` cannot refuse its own run over premises flags nobody has set. ⚠ **AMENDED 2026-08-27 (CR-039 wave C task 11) — THE FACTOR NOW HAS A WRITE PATH AND THE SEEDED ESTATE EXERCISES IT.** `PLAN_FIELDS` carries `outside_city_factor` and `PLAN_SERVICE_FIELDS` carries `outside_city_exempt`, so `create_plan`/`update_plan` write both and the sample seeder posts them through the validated write path rather than reaching past it to the ORM (🔒 sensitive path). **What the demo tenant now holds, every figure cited:** the sample RESIDENTIAL plan states **`1.5`** — r10's own words on that very schedule, *"Premises outside city limits multiply base and variable by 1.5 — set on the premise record"* — with `sewer` exempt (§A4 S2: *"BASE-S $8.00 (flat everywhere — the multiplier does NOT touch sewer)"*); the sample COMMERCIAL plan states **nothing**, deliberately, so the first gate's own promise is demonstrable; and **2 of the 44** seeded premises are outside the city, DERIVED from r10's `1,186 of 1,247` (61/1,247 = 4.89% of 44 = 2.15). All 44 are STATED, because 42 unrecorded addresses under a surcharging plan would be 42 exception rows. **Worked, on a real issued bill:** `161 Silver Creek Rd`, June 2026, 18 kgal — water usage `$109.00 → $163.50`, water base `$8.00 → $12.00` (`tests/test_cr039_c11_seeded_surcharge.py`). ⚠ **AMENDED 2026-08-28 (lane RATING-WRITE-PATHS) — `DEF-515` IS CLOSED: THE SCREEN CAN NOW ASK FOR IT.** PLN-03 **step 2** — the step that binds services, because this rule puts the per-SERVICE exemption *"on the plan's own service line, because the plan is what lists its services"* and splitting the two would let a utility state a factor and never meet the exemptions — draws the plan's factor and a tick per bound service. **Worked on the seeded estate, every figure the fixture's own:** `161 Silver Creek Rd`, June 2026, 18 kgal — water base `$8.00 → $12.00` and water usage `$109.00 → $163.50`, a water subtotal of `$117.00 → $175.50`, **`$58.50` on one bill on one service** that no human could ask for through any screen until this commit. **TWO PAYLOAD RULES THE SCREEN MUST NOT COLLAPSE, both pinned by tests:** a blank factor posts `null` and the key is PRESENT — omitting it would leave the stored factor in place and make clearing the field do nothing, so a surcharge could be switched on and never off; and `outside_city_exempt` is sent on EVERY service row including the false one, because the server replaces the whole service set and an omitted key on a just-unticked row would silently keep the stored `true`. The six-place stored factor is trimmed for the input (`1.500000` → `1.5`) and NEVER re-rounded — a value with six significant places survives intact. And `OQ-424`: sewer is exempt and its base is `$0.00`, so the exemption reaches nothing while BR-RAT-015 ❄️ takes 40% of a water usage charge the multiplier has already moved — `$43.60 → $65.40` on the same bill. Two signed sentences meeting for the first time; the PO's to settle. | PO ruling 2026-08-21 (decision log) · `OQ-308` (CLOSED) · `DEF-466` (money half closed) · `BR-RAT-101` (the flag) · `BR-RAT-096` (the binding principle) · `charges.outside_city_applies` (the other half, already built) · `resolution.money_factor` | **CONFIRMED (2026-08-21)** | `rate_plans.outside_city_factor` · `plan_services.outside_city_exempt` · R5 · PLN-03 |
| BR-RAT-103 | **A TAX HAS FOUR STATUSES, THREE OF THEM DERIVED — AND IT PRICES A BILL ONLY INSIDE ITS EFFECTIVE WINDOW. (CR-013 part 4, 2026-08-18, built 2026-08-21 — closes `DEF-387` and `DEF-467`.)** Part 4's landing column is verbatim *"Status (Active/Draft/Scheduled/Expired)"* and step 4 offers *"Draft / Save & schedule (future statutory date, shows as Scheduled in the rate calendar) / Activate"*. **THE THREE DERIVED ONES COME FROM DATES ALREADY STORED** — `effective_to` in the past is *expired*, `effective_from` in the future is *scheduled*, neither is *active* — computed in `selectors.tax_status`, never stored, which is what `selectors.schedule_state` already does for a rate schedule; four objects with four status vocabularies must at least agree on where a status comes from. `inactive` is a FIFTH value the product already had (IS-1 deactivate-first) and is kept, exactly as `schedule_state` keeps `archived` beyond US-RAT-004's ratified set. ⚠ **THE FOURTH CANNOT BE DERIVED AND IS STORED AS `taxes.is_draft`.** *"Nobody has put this into force yet"* is not a date: the five ruled Texas rates are seeded with NO `effective_from` and are pricing real bills, so reading *undated* as *draft* — which `DEF-387`'s own suggested fix proposed — would print **Draft** over the State Utility Tax while it charges 6.25 %. ⚠ **THE FLAG DEFAULTS TO `False` AND THAT IS THE DESIGN, NOT A CONVENIENCE.** The first implementation used a nullable `activated_at` backfilled on migration; the fixture seeder, the sample-data seeder and every test create taxes with `Tax.objects.create(...)`, so each would have minted a silent DRAFT THAT NEVER PRICES and **the ruled Texas rates would have stopped charging on the next tenant provisioned**, with nothing red. Hard-won law 3: the unsafe option must be the one you type. ⚠ **AND THE WINDOW IS NOW READ, WHICH IT NEVER WAS (`DEF-467`).** `plan_tax_rules` resolved a tax by jurisdiction and kind with **no date predicate at all** while its docstring claimed *"taxes carries no effective-dating columns, so there is nothing to filter on"* — true when written, false since r6.5. **Worked:** a 6.25 % rate stored with `effective_from` 2027-01-01 charged **`$6.25`** on today's $100.00 bill and now charges **`$0.00`**; an expired rate charged for ever and now stops. A NULL bound is *no bound stated*, so every existing (undated) row is in force exactly as before and **golden stays 33/33** — read that as the DOCUMENT check it is (DEF-2846): the harness recomputes B4/B5's own figures and never runs the engine, so the engine-verified evidence for this rule is `06-backend/tests/golden/`, 14 of the 61 signed GTs. The outcome of a tax outside its window is NO LINE AT ALL, never a zero-valued one — DEF-273's ruled direction. | CR-013 part 4 (`11-change-requests.md`) · `DEF-387` · `DEF-467` · `selectors.schedule_state` (the derivation precedent) · hard-won law 1 (the stale docstring) · hard-won law 3 | **CONFIRMED (2026-08-21)** | `taxes.is_draft` · `selectors.tax_status` · `selectors.tax_in_force` · `test_cr013_p4_tax_model.py::test_def387_*` · `::test_def467_*` |
| BR-RAT-104 | **AN IN-FORCE TAX'S RATE HISTORY IS NOT EDITABLE IN PLACE; A TYPO IS FIXED BY A DOCUMENTED CORRECTION ROW OVER THE SAME WINDOW. (CR-013 part 4, 2026-08-18, built 2026-08-21 — closes `DEF-384`.)** Part 4, shared rules, verbatim: *"in-place edits of an active version are refused outright, even for Owner/Admin, because rate history is an audit artefact; a historic typo is fixed by a documented correction version with a reason."* ⚠ **THIS WAS REFUSED ON THE CLIENT AND NOWHERE ELSE.** `TaxWizard` has stopped the edit since it was built, correctly — and `PATCH /taxes/{id}` accepted a new rate on a live tax from anything that could reach the API. A client-side refusal is not a refusal. **A CORRECTION IS NOT A RATE CHANGE, AND THE DIFFERENCE IS THE WINDOW:** a rate CHANGE is already a new row with a NEW window (BR-RAT-037); a CORRECTION restates the SAME window, because the figure recorded for that period was wrong. So `correct_tax` mints a new row carrying the corrected values, **copies the corrected row's window verbatim rather than taking it from the payload**, links the two (`corrects` / `superseded_by`), and the corrected row **stops pricing while keeping its rate, its dates and its audit trail for ever** — which is what makes part 4's *"every rate with statutory date, citation, and amount collected under each"* readable. **Worked:** a rate typed `0.625000` (62.5 %) instead of `0.062500` charges **`$62.50`** on a $100.00 bill where it should charge **`$6.25`**; after the correction the next run charges `$6.25` and the 62.5 % row stays visible as the thing that happened. ⚠ **WHAT IS REFUSED IS RATE HISTORY, NOT EVERY FIELD** — `services.TAX_RATE_HISTORY_FIELDS` is the list, and filing detail (`remit_to`, `filing_form`, the reminder recipient, the payment method) stays editable in place, because part 4's refusal is REASONED and a bookkeeper's email address is not rate history. **That boundary is a lane JUDGMENT, recorded in the decision log 2026-08-21, not a line quoted from the CR.** A DRAFT is freely editable (it has never priced anything); a SUPERSEDED row is frozen entirely. ⚠ **AND BILLS ALREADY ISSUED ARE NOT TOUCHED** — hard rule 4, and part 4's own *"bills store the version id, base amount and exemptions applied so any past remittance report recomputes identically"*. Whether a correction MUST also restate them is **`OQ-310`**, unruled, and `correct_tax` names it in its own response so an operator who has just fixed a wrong rate is not left believing the money is fixed.  ⚠ **AND IT REACHED THE RATE SCHEDULE ON 2026-09-17, SEVENTEEN DAYS LATE (`DEF-RAT-5709`).** This rule's TITLE names a tax, and the sentence it quotes from part 4's **shared** rules names no object at all — *"in-place edits of an ACTIVE VERSION are refused outright"*. The 2026-08-21 build satisfied it on ONE of the two objects in this module that have rate history, and nothing counts a shared rule's surfaces, so a rule that was half built read as a rule that was built. The PO found the other half in the beta: *"if user make an error while creating the rate schedule and they want to rectify that error using edit - how are they going to do it; it is not a new rate or it is not a change in rate - it is just an error handling"*. `services.correct_rate_schedule_version` is the tax path's shape verbatim — the window copied from the row being corrected and never read from the payload, `corrects`/`superseded_by` linking the two, the corrected row keeping its rate, its dates and its trail for ever. **One thing is STRICTER here:** a date in a correction payload is REFUSED BY NAME rather than silently overridden, because a caller that sent one believed it would be used. **And one line does the work that `Tax.superseded_by` does by itself:** a tariff version has a WINDOW, so after a correction two activated rows contain the same day and `selectors.in_force_version` carries `.filter(superseded_by__isnull=True)` — without it the order-by picks by `effective_from`, which is identical on both, and which row prices becomes a coin toss. `activated_versions` deliberately does NOT filter: a superseded row is still the history BR-RAT-017 reads. **Hard rule 4 is untouched and is STATED ON SCREEN** — bills already issued keep their figure, and the count is served from `invoice_lines.tariff_version_id` (measured before it was trusted: 456/456 usage and 184/184 base lines carry it, 0 orphans). Whether a correction MUST also restate those bills is still `OQ-310`, unruled | CR-013 part 4 · `DEF-384` · `BR-RAT-037` (a change is a new row) · CLAUDE.md hard rule 4 · `OQ-310` (open) · decision log 2026-08-21 (the field boundary) | **CONFIRMED (2026-08-21)** | `taxes.corrects` · `taxes.superseded_by` · `services.correct_tax` · `POST /taxes/{id}/corrections` · `test_cr013_p4_tax_model.py::test_def384_*` |
| BR-RAT-105 | **AN EXEMPTION IS A CATEGORY WITH A CERTIFICATE AND AN EXPIRY, HELD BY ONE ACCOUNT AGAINST ONE AUTHORITY'S TAX — AND AN EXPIRED CERTIFICATE REINSTATES THE TAX SILENTLY. (CR-013 part 4, 2026-08-18, built 2026-08-21 — closes `DEF-383`.)** Part 4 step 2 asks for *"exemption categories with certificate requirements"* and the detail tab for *"Exemptions — categories, accounts holding each, certificate expiry"*. ⚠ **THIS IS NOT `taxes.applies_to_customer_classes`, AND THE MODEL DOCSTRING HAS WARNED ABOUT THE CONFLATION SINCE `DEF-273`.** That array answers *"who does this rate reach at all"* — r10's *"Comm, Industrial only"* — is a property of the RATE, is the same for every account in the class, and never expires. An EXEMPTION is a property of ONE ACCOUNT: a church holds a certificate, it has a number and an expiry, and when it lapses the tax comes back. ⚠ **NOR IS IT `accounts.tax_exempt`, WHICH IS BLANKET** — one boolean, one expiry, every authority at once. Part 4's object is PER TAX and PER CATEGORY. **Worked, on a $100.00 electric bill under a 6.25 % state tax and a 1.50 % city fee:** a church holding a RELIGIOUS certificate against the state tax pays **`$1.50`**, not `$0.00` and not `$7.75` — a city is not a state and its statute is its own. Collapsing the two would have widened every exemption a utility records to every authority it remits to. ⚠ **AND THE EXPIRY RULE IS THE OPPOSITE OF THIS PRODUCT'S USUAL POSTURE, DELIBERATELY.** Part 4, verbatim: *"Expired exemption certificates reinstate the tax on the next run with no exception raised — it's expected behaviour."* Everywhere else a bill run STOPS and names what changed underneath it; here it must not, because an expiry is a date passing rather than a fault, and a utility with 1–2 staff cannot triage an exception queue that refills every January. **Worked:** the same church pays `$0.00` while live and **`$6.25`** on the first run after expiry, with no exception, no hold, and the reinstatement visible because the line is simply back. `expires_on` NULL is **permanent, not unknown** (a municipality does not renew being a municipality). A certificate is REVOKED, never deleted — an authority that disallows a claim needs to see when the utility stopped honouring it. ⚠ **AN ABSENT `account_id` MEANS THE TAX APPLIES**, which is the opposite direction to DEF-273's three fail-closed gates: those are restrictions stated on the TAX, this is a claim made by a CUSTOMER, and a claim nobody has made is not an exemption. | CR-013 part 4 · `DEF-383` · `DEF-273` (the conflation it warns about) · `BR-RAT-006` (exemption is account-level) · `apps/billing/gather._tax_exempt_on` (the blanket flag, unchanged) | **CONFIRMED (2026-08-21)** | `tax_exemption_categories` · `tax_exemption_certificates` · `selectors.account_holds_exemption` · `test_cr013_p4_tax_model.py::test_def383_*` |
| BR-RAT-106 | **DISCOUNT TREATMENT IS STATUTORY AND STATED PER TAX, NEVER GLOBALLY. (CR-013 part 4, 2026-08-18, built 2026-08-21 — closes `DEF-382`.)** Part 4 step 2, verbatim: *"discount treatment — before or after discounts, stated per tax because it's statutory"*, and part 4's own commentary adds *"Any global before/after setting is wrong."* ⚠ **IT WAS NOTICED TWICE AND NEVER BECAME A ROW ANYBODY TRACKED** — `apps/rating/discounts.py` and `discount_services.py` both name `taxes.discount_treatment` as EP-RAT-13's unbuilt column, in docstrings. Hard-won law 1: a comment is a note, never a plan. `before` means the discount lands BEFORE the tax, so the authority levies on the REDUCED subtotal; `after` means the authority levies on the full tariff amount and the discount never reaches the tax base. The word names where the DISCOUNT goes, which is how a statute is written. **Worked, and it is the pair `resolution.py` already carried:** a `$1.00` PAPERLESS credit on a Bexar County bill leaves that authority's 2 % line at **`$3.78`** under `before` and **`$3.80`** under `after` — two cents, per bill, per authority, in the direction a regulator audits. ⚠ **THE DEFAULT CLAUSE OF THIS RULE IS SUPERSEDED BY `BR-RAT-650` (2026-08-28, `DEF-652`) — THE REST OF IT IS UNCHANGED AND STILL CONFIRMED.** It read: *"`default="before"` is the single behaviour the engine has always had, so no existing figure moves; what changes is that it is now a statement a utility makes per authority rather than an assumption nobody could see or contradict."* That was correct on 2026-08-21, when the column was new and the question it stood in for was unanswered. The PO answered it on 2026-08-27 and **the stored answer is now `after`** — see `BR-RAT-650`. **Everything else here holds:** the treatment is still statutory, still PER TAX, still bounded by `ck_taxes_discount_treatment`, still read in exactly one place (`selectors.taxable_subtotal_for`), and `before` is still fully expressible for an authority that levies on the discounted amount. Bounded in the DB (`ck_taxes_discount_treatment`) as well as in `_validate_tax`. The reader is `selectors.taxable_subtotal_for`, which PICKS A BASE and rounds nothing — B4 ❄️ decides what a tax line rounds to and it decides it in one place. | CR-013 part 4 · `DEF-382` · `discount_pricing.DiscountOutcome` (which returns BOTH subtotals for exactly this) · hard-won law 1 · B4 ❄️ | **CONFIRMED (2026-08-21) · DEFAULT CLAUSE SUPERSEDED 2026-08-28 by BR-RAT-650** | `taxes.discount_treatment` · `selectors.taxable_subtotal_for` · `test_cr013_p4_tax_model.py::test_def382_*` |
| BR-RAT-107 | **THE MATCHED PREMISE COUNT IS COMPUTED BY THE ENGINE'S OWN PREDICATE, NOT BY A QUERY THAT AGREES WITH IT. (CR-013 part 4, 2026-08-18, built 2026-08-21 — closes `DEF-380`.)** Part 4, TAX-02 step 1: *"Shows the matched premise count so a wrong boundary is visible before rates are entered"*, and step 4: *"read-back with matched premise count and sample line"*. Nothing served that count: `premises_in_scope` answered ONE premises at a time and was reachable only from the bill run. ⚠ **THE COUNT RUNS `premises_in_scope` PER PREMISES — the same function `plan_tax_rules` calls to decide whether a real bill attracts the tax.** A count computed from a WHERE clause would be a SECOND implementation of one rule, and a safeguard that can disagree with the thing it safeguards is worse than no safeguard: it would say *61 premises* on a screen while the bill run charged 1,247. At this product's scale — under 3,000 connections — correctness is worth more than the WHERE. **Worked, on Silver Creek:** scope `city_limits` = *San Antonio* over 1,247 premises answers `count 1,186` of `total 1,247`; the 61 outside-city premises r10 exempts are the difference, and that is the figure the safeguard exists to put in front of somebody before they type a rate. ⚠ **`resolvable` TRAVELS WITH THE COUNT.** Three of the five mechanisms — `county`, `taxing_district`, `premise_list` — have no fact to resolve against, so the honest count is **0 of 1,247**: a TRUE statement about the product and a MISLEADING one about the utility's boundary. A bare `0` would read as *"your boundary matches nobody, fix your boundary"*. The premises facts cross a documented seam (`rating.seams.premises_geography_rows`) because `premises` is METERING's table (B2 §2), and the sample line carries the premise number and address only — never an owner: this read exists to prove a BOUNDARY, and a boundary needs a place, not a person. | CR-013 part 4 · `DEF-380` · `BR-RAT-101` (the `inside_city_limits` flag the count now reads) · `DEF-273` (the five mechanisms) · B2 §2 (premises is metering's) | **CONFIRMED (2026-08-21)** | `selectors.matched_premises` · `GET /taxes/matched-premises` · `seams.premises_geography_rows` · `test_cr013_p4_tax_model.py::test_def380_*` |
| BR-RAT-108 | **A CHARGE CODE PRICES ONE CUSTOMER CLASS, AND THE BASE IS DERIVED FROM THREE DIMENSIONS — NOT TWO. (PO ruling 2026-08-21 — closes `DEF-467`. **Numbered BR-RAT-103 when ruled; renumbered 108 on merge — CR-013 part 4's tax lane had taken 103–107 in parallel.**)** The PO asked how one base is chosen from many: _"based on Asset and Account type you will have select 1 base charge out of so many charges … then you need to derive which base rate to select"_, then, asked directly: _"Yes"_. **THE DERIVATION, STATED:** a base charge is narrowed by **SERVICE** (`charges.service_id`) → **ACCOUNT TYPE** (`charges.applies_to_customer_classes`, new) → **METER SIZE** (`charge_code_amounts.size_class`). Exactly one survivor is the base; **none** falls back to the schedule's size row with `base_source` recording it; **more than one REFUSES and names them all** (BR-RAT-088 read path, `DEF-468`). **WHY THE COLUMN WAS NEEDED, worked on real data:** Silver Creek's water base is **`$8.00` residential** and **`$15.00` commercial** — one service, two amounts, and a charge code is per TENANT where the plan column it replaces was per PLAN. Without this the two could not both exist, and the only migration available was two codes distinguished by NAME, which BR-RAT-088's own docstring warns against because name-matching fails silently. **The PO's point about capability was already served:** varying the base by meter size *within* one class is `charge_code_amounts`, which exists — so `BASE-W` residential can state `5/8" $8.00 · 1" $14.00 · 2" $34.00` and a commercial code its own. ⚠ **NULL OR `[]` MEANS EVERY CLASS**, which is what every existing row means, so the column is additive and no bill moves. An **INCLUSION** list per DEF-273 — a class nobody listed is simply not in it, where an exclusion list silently WIDENS every time a tenant adds a class. ⚠ **AN UNKNOWN CLASS IS REFUSED, NEVER DROPPED**, and the stakes are higher here than on a tax: a silently-vanished `"residental"` leaves a base the utility believes is restricted applying to EVERYBODY, on every bill. ⚠ **AND THIS DOES NOT MAKE TWO MATCHING BASE CODES LEGAL — IT MAKES THEM SEPARABLE.** Before it, two base codes for one service could only be ambiguity; now it is how a utility prices two classes. | PO ruling 2026-08-21 (decision log) · `DEF-467` (CLOSED) · `DEF-468` (the refusal this makes meaningful) · `BR-RAT-088` · `DEF-273` (the inclusion-list reason) · `resolution.ChargeCode.customer_classes` | **CONFIRMED (2026-08-21)** | `charges.applies_to_customer_classes` · `_customer_class_list` (shared with taxes) · R4 · CHG-02 |
| BR-RAT-109 | ⚖️ **SUPERSEDED 2026-10-03 by PO ruling (1) → `BR-RAT-1061`: approval is the last act and there is no Activate, so no door remains through which a never-submitted draft goes live.** ~~**ACTIVATING A NEVER-SUBMITTED DISCOUNT IS ALLOWED, WARNED, AND RECORDED — NOT REFUSED.~~ (PO ruling 2026-08-21 — closes `DEF-479`. **Numbered BR-RAT-104 when ruled; renumbered 109 on merge.**)** PO verbatim: _"We will show message and not refuse it"_. **THE PROBLEM:** BR-RAT-077 computes the second-approval requirement **at SUBMIT** and stores it with the threshold that decided it. `activate_discount_version` accepts a `DRAFT` version as well as a `SCHEDULED` one — so a version that never passed through submit carries **no requirement at all**, and one admin can take it live alone. **The case that slips through most cleanly is a discount with NO budget cap**, which BR-RAT-077 says *always* requires the second approval — unbounded is the largest commitment there is. **THE RULING IS WARN-AND-ALLOW, and it is the third time the PO has chosen that shape**: plan-change backdating (2026-08-11, option B) and self-approval (2026-08-19, *"yes allow it"*). The reason is the same each time — **Silver Creek has 1–2 staff**, and a refusal that requires a second person is a refusal that stops the business. ⚠ **THE WARNING MUST SAY WHAT WAS SKIPPED, NOT THAT SOMETHING WAS.** It cannot say *"this needs two approvals"*, because nothing computed that — skipping submit is precisely why there is no figure. It says: this was never submitted, so **no second-approval check was performed**; and where the discount states no budget cap it adds that BR-RAT-077 would have required one. **A warning naming a figure it does not have would be worse than silence.** ⚠ **AND WARN-AND-ALLOW ONLY WORKS IF THE FACT IS RECORDED** — which is BR-RAT-077's own posture for self-approval, *"with the fact recorded on the row and in the audit trail, so an auditor sees it rather than inferring it"*. The activation writes `activated_without_submission` into its `audit_log` entry, so the absence of a check is a fact an auditor reads rather than one they must reconstruct from a null. | PO ruling 2026-08-21 (decision log) · `DEF-479` (CLOSED) · `BR-RAT-077` (the requirement it skips, and the self-approval precedent) · decision log 2026-08-11 (backdating, option B) · hard rule 7 | **CONFIRMED (2026-08-21)** | `activate_discount_version` · `DefinitionDetailPage` · `audit_log` |
| BR-RAT-110 | ⚖️ **COMPLETED 2026-10-03 by PO ruling (2) → `BR-RAT-1062`; `OQ-322` CLOSED. Both gates now apply at ENROLMENT too, and unbinding is ruled: it ends the binding and its enrolments the day before (the *"UNBINDING IS NOT RULED"* clause below is history).** **A PLAN BINDS DISCOUNTS FROM THE CATALOGUE; ENROLMENT STILL DECIDES WHO ACTUALLY GETS ONE. BOTH GATES, NOT ONE REPLACING THE OTHER. (Closes `OQ-300`, closed by the PO 2026-08-21 on option (A); built 2026-08-21. **Numbered 110 against the tip of `claude/plans-and-rates-83m9zx`; two sibling lanes were minting rules the same hour, so renumber on merge if 110 is taken.**)** This is `BR-RAT-096` applied to step 6, and it is the PO's own sentence that licenses it: _"plan all steps are bascailly binding everything together"_ — step 3 binds rate schedules, step 4 binds charge codes, step 5 binds taxes, **step 6 binds discounts**, and on step 6 specifically the PO said _"next step - all Seelct and add - this UI does not go well"_, i.e. make it the same search-and-add. **THE SHAPE, and it is the shape `plan_tax_rules` already has:** `discounts: [{discount, available_from, available_to}]` joins `PLAN_FIELDS`, `_apply_plan_children` replaces the set, and `plan_detail` serves it back — identical to `charge_rules`, `tax_rules` and `deposit_rules`, because a second shape for the same idea is how two screens start disagreeing. ⚠ **WHAT IS NOT REVERSED, AND IT IS THE WHOLE SAFETY OF THE TABLE:** `BR-RAT-069` stands — *"plan attachment makes a discount available, not granted"*. A bill run reads `discount_enrolments` and **never** `plan_discount_rules`. **Worked, on our own seeded data:** Residential Standard carries **842** accounts and binds `SENIOR` at **15%**; binding it changes **0** bills, because 0 of those 842 accounts is enrolled. Read the availability row as the grant instead and all **842** are discounted — which is the failure `BR-RAT-069` exists to name, and it would show up as a **15%** revenue drop nobody authorised. ⚠ **AND `plan_discount_rules` STILL HAS NO VALUE COLUMN** (PO 2026-08-19, _"no plan cannot override prices"_). The binding chooses WHICH discounts a plan offers and never at what value; r10 draws a per-plan discount-value override and it is not built, deliberately. Availability DATES are the only qualifiers the row carries, and they may narrow the discount's own window, never widen it. ⚠ **UNBINDING IS NOT RULED — see `OQ-322`**: dropping a discount that accounts are enrolled on refuses with `NotImplementedError("OQ-322")` rather than guessing whether the enrolments end. | PO ruling 2026-08-21 (`OQ-300` closure) · CR-013 part 5 step 6 · `BR-RAT-096` · `BR-RAT-069` | **CONFIRMED (2026-08-21)** | `test_wave_g_plan_binding.py::BR-RAT-110` · `PlanWizard.test.tsx::step 6 binds` |
| BR-RAT-111 | ⚠ **(2026-10-03) every `OQ-324` clause in this row is HISTORY — it is CLOSED (B): a plan version has a window (`BR-RAT-1066`), so a plan correction restates the version's number AND its window.** **A PLAN VERSION IS A FILING RECORD, NOT A COUNTER — WHO CHANGED IT, WHEN, WHY, AND WHAT CHANGED, KEPT FOR EVERY VERSION. (CR-013 part 5, 2026-08-18; built 2026-08-21. **Numbered 111 against the tip; renumber on merge if taken.**)** Part 5, verbatim: the plan is *"the contract boundary: the plan version is what makes a bill explicable months later"*, and PLN-02 asks for *"Versions with component-level diff and the re-priced account count"*. `rate_plans.version` has been an INTEGER since W1 and `BR-RAT-037` has bumped it since W1 — so the product could say a plan was on v3 and could not say what v1 or v2 held, who filed them or why. The reason lived in `rate_change_log`, keyed by `entity_id` with a `before`/`after` blob, which is a compliance trail and not a version list: nothing on the plan could reach it and PLN-02's Versions tab printed one row for the current version with the CHANGE column stating its own absence. **THE SHAPE IS COPIED FROM THE RATE SCHEDULE'S, DELIBERATELY** (`tariff_versions`, migration 0019 — `version_label` free text and never unique, `submitted_by` a cross-module FK to `identity.User` served as `{id, name}` through `seams.user_display_names`, `change_reason` the authored words on the row a regulator reads). A second shape for the same idea is how two screens start disagreeing about what a version is. **Worked:** Residential Standard is renamed and its water base moves from `$8.00` to `$9.50`; v2 is written with the actor, the timestamp, the typed reason and a snapshot, and PLN-02 lists **2** rows descending with **1** changed component named (`services.water.base_charge 8.000000 → 9.500000`) — where before it listed **1** row and printed *"not stored"*. ⚠ **THE RECORD IS INERT AND THAT IS THE RULE, NOT AN OMISSION.** No pricing path reads `plan_versions`: `resolution` and `billing/gather` are untouched, because *which* version prices an account already assigned is `OQ-323` and is unruled. ⚠ **AND A VERSION HAS NO EFFECTIVE DATE** — part 5 step 8's *Draft / Submit / Activate* would make one pend, and that is `OQ-324`; a column added before the ruling would make the unruled reading storable. ⚠ **APPEND-ONLY** (hard rule 4's spirit): a version row is never updated or deleted, corrections are new versions. | CR-013 part 5 (`11-change-requests.md`) · `BR-RAT-037` · `BR-RAT-082/083` (submitter is not approver) · `DEF-367`/`DEF-369`/`DEF-280` on the schedule side | **CONFIRMED (2026-08-21)** | `test_wave_g_plan_versions.py::BR-RAT-111` · `PlanDetail.test.tsx::versions` |
| BR-RAT-112 | **ELIGIBILITY STAYS ADVISORY. THE PRODUCT DOES NOT REFUSE TO PUT AN ACCOUNT ON A PLAN, AND THAT IS A DELIBERATE PRODUCT PRINCIPLE RATHER THAN AN UNBUILT FEATURE. (PO ruling 2026-08-21, closing `CR-022`; CONFIRMS `BR-RAT-043` rather than reversing it.)** PO verbatim: _"dont refuse, it is okay many times utilities are not in position to make all changes due to human factor hence we have to keep flexibility"_. r10 draws the opposite — *"Accounts outside the bounds cannot be moved onto the plan"* — and **r10 is overruled here**, which is one of the few places it is; the ruling is recorded rather than the drawing. ⚠ **THE REASONING IS THE PART THAT GENERALISES, so it is written down and not just the answer.** A 1–2 staff utility often cannot make its own data right on demand: the customer class is wrong in the legacy system, the meter size was never recorded, the income-program flag lives in a spreadsheet. A product that hard-blocks on those facts does not produce correct data — it produces a member of staff who cannot bill a customer today and who will find a way around the block by tomorrow. **Advisory keeps the truth visible without making the operator dishonest.** ⚠ **SO THE THREE THINGS `CR-022` SAID WOULD HAVE TO EXIST DO NOT.** No predicate grammar, no evaluator (`DEF-404` is not a blocker for this and can be closed as *won't-build for this purpose*), and no *"1,014 accounts satisfy these rules · 233 would not"* count, because nothing gates on it. The wizard's step 7 keeps r10's GEOMETRY with its controls disabled and their reason named — which is what it already ships. ⚠ **AND THE RETROACTIVE QUESTION NEVER ARISES**, which was the expensive half: no account already on a plan can be thrown off it by a rule change, and r10's *"re-run at every meter exchange"* has nothing to re-run. **This ruling is the same principle as `BR-MET-100`** (a units row naming an unknown plan LOADS, flagged): where the product could refuse or could record-and-show, this PO rules record-and-show, twice on one day. | CONFIRMED |
| BR-RAT-113 | **STAFF PICK TAXES AND FEES AND ATTACH THEM TO A PLAN. PLN-03 STEP 5 IS NO LONGER READ-ONLY. (PO ruling 2026-08-21, closing `CR-021`; SUPERSEDES `CR-013` part 5 step 5's *"taxes read-only with the jurisdiction that brings each"*.)** PO verbatim: _"staff need to pick up and attach taxes and fees on the plan"_. So step 5 becomes what steps 4 and 6 already are — catalogue on the left, *applied to this plan* on the right — and the right-hand pane is now **chosen**, not derived. ⚠ **THIS IS THE CAPABILITY READING, NOT THE LAYOUT ONE.** `CR-021` step 3 recorded two readings and said the layout one had already been built; the PO's words name the other. `DEF-444` (the tenant had zero tax jurisdictions, so the screen was empty whatever its design said) is **fixed**, so this ruling is about a populated screen and not about an empty one. ⚠ **WHAT IT DOES *NOT* SAY, AND THE ONE THING STILL TO RULE: ATTACHING IS ADDITIVE.** The ruling is *"pick up and attach"*. It does not say a plan may **omit** a tax its jurisdiction levies, and `BR-RAT-094` (PO 2026-08-21, nineteen hours earlier) refused the two doors a plan had to change a tax at all, on the PO's own words: _"Nobody has ever defended letting a plan skip a state tax."_ **So this rule is implemented as ADD-ONLY**: a plan may attach taxes and fees beyond what its jurisdiction brings, and a jurisdiction-mandated tax stays on the bill whether or not anyone ticked it. Whether staff may also DETACH a mandated tax is a separate question, raised with a worked figure rather than assumed in either direction. ⚠ **`BR-RAT-025 D8` is narrowed, not reversed:** *"tax rules via plan + account-level exemption"* stays true — the EXEMPTION remains an account-level fact (`BR-RAT-060`), and what changes is that the plan's tax set is now partly authored rather than wholly derived. | **CONFIRMED (2026-08-21)** · ⚠ **BUILT 2026-08-22 (W7, v2.30.0).** `plan_tax_rules` holds TWO row shapes (migration `0022_br_rat_113_plan_tax_attachments`): `jurisdiction_id` = the BR-RAT-096 binding, `tax_id` = one explicitly attached catalogue tax — exactly one per row (`ck_plan_tax_rules_one_source`), plan × tax UNIQUE. The jurisdiction's own taxes stay DERIVED at pricing time, never materialised — which makes ADD-ONLY structural: a mandated tax is not a row, so no deletion can omit it. `selectors.plan_tax_rules` prices attachments through the SAME four gates and DE-DUPLICATES (the same tax via both paths bills once: bind Texas 6.25% AND attach its row → $6.25 on $100.00, never $12.50). `POST /plans/{id}/taxes` / `DELETE /plans/{id}/taxes/{tax_id}` delegate to `update_plan`, so a published plan takes BR-RAT-037's reason + version bump; a detach attempt on a mandated tax refuses BY NAME citing BR-RAT-094 (`rating.plans.mandated_tax_not_detachable`) — the unruled detach question stays unruled. PLN-03 step 5 attaches at the tax grain beside the authority-grain bind; derived taxes are marked *Brought by jurisdiction* with no remove control. Tests: `tests/test_br_rat_113_attach_taxes.py` (11 TCs, refusal proved RED first); golden exit 0 before and after. 🔒 HUMAN-REVIEW-PENDING (ledger row 2026-08-22). |
| BR-RAT-114 | ⚠ **AMENDED 2026-10-12 (PO, one-time bill review — decision log of that date; `DEF-BIL-10067`).** **(1) Each service is cut ONLY at ITS OWN change dates** — PO: *"why 3 it is only 2 cuts … since ther eis only 1 change in that period for given utility service"*; a service with no change in the period is not cut. **(2) One BASE row per service**, two only when the base price itself changes in the period (Q-a). **(3) Usage is split by days per service at its change**, one row per part; a reading dated on the change day wins (unchanged). **(4) By rate type:** tiered — each part gets its share of the tier limits by days (Q-b) · time-of-use — each window's kWh split by days at that part's window prices (Q-c) · demand — kWh split by days, the month's peak kW charged once at the rate in force at the end of the period (Q-d) · seasonal — a season change cuts like a rate change (Q-e). Worked: water and sewer change 15 Sep, storm water 20 Sep, trash none; reading 2.9 → 4.6 = **1.7 kgal**, water 1–14 Sep **0.79 kgal** + 15–30 Sep **0.91 kgal**; trash one row; storm water cut at 20 Sep. The sentence below *"one line per sub-period per charge"* is NARROWED by (1)–(2). **AN ORDINARY BILL SPLITS AT A MID-PERIOD RATE OR PLAN CHANGE — one bill, priced per sub-period, each sub-period at the version in force.** PO verbatim (2026-08-22): _"We need this - Water at $2.00/kgal until the 15th, then $2.20, 10 kgal spread evenly: split = $21.00"_. **The confirmed figure is the contract:** 10 kgal spread evenly across a period whose change lands at its midpoint prices 5 kgal @ $2.00 + 5 kgal @ $2.20 = **$21.00** — never the single-version $20.00 or $22.00. Consumption apportions to sub-periods **evenly by day** unless an actual read dates the boundary, in which case the read wins (hard rule 3 — consumption always with its explicit basis). This brings the ordinary run into agreement with the back-bill path, which has priced per sub-period since it shipped (DEF-503's own evidence). The bill draws one line per sub-period per charge, each naming its rate and dates, because a split a customer cannot see is a number they cannot check. | PO ruling 2026-08-22 · DEF-503 · DEF-504 · decision log 2026-08-22 | **CONFIRMED (2026-08-22)** · ⚠ **AMENDMENT (1)–(4) BUILT 2026-10-12** (`DEF-BIL-10067`, lane OTB-FIX — `midperiod.split_plan` cuts each service at its own tariff versions; one base row per service unless the base price changed; Q-b..Q-e; the last part takes the remainder so the parts add back to the meter; 🔒 HUMAN-REVIEW-PENDING, review-ledger row of that date; worked figures `06-backend/tests/test_def_bil_10067_one_time_per_service_split.py`, 10 of 19 RED at `c290f23c3` first; the catch-up path `backbill.groups_split_by_days` is NOT changed and still cuts every service and repeats the base per part) · BUILT 2026-08-22 (`apps/billing/midperiod.py` + the run/single wiring — 🔒 HUMAN-REVIEW-PENDING; the $21.00 pin is `06-backend/tests/test_br_rat_114_midperiod_split.py` tc1, proved RED first at 22.00; golden 33/33 before and after) | DEF-503 · DEF-504 · golden-style pin: $21.00 |
| BR-RAT-115 | **ANY TAX MAY BE DETACHED FROM A PLAN — THE FLOOR IS THAT AT LEAST ONE TAX IS LEFT STANDING, WHATEVER ROUTE THE DEPARTING ONE ARRIVED BY. (AMENDED 2026-08-22, the PO's second pass — verbatim: _"yes lets go with B"_.)** ⚠ **The first build scoped the floor to MANDATED detaches only**, reasoning that detaching an explicit attachment had already shipped under BR-RAT-113 and should not be reversed by inference. The worked case was then put to the PO — a plan binding NO authority and carrying ONE hand-attached tax, the 2.00% county rate at **$2.00** on a $100.00 bill, where detaching it would leave the plan taxing **nothing** — and the PO ruled the WIDER floor. **So the gate is on the OUTCOME, never on the route:** would this detach leave the plan with zero taxes? Then it refuses, by name, with the tax named. ⚠ **A REDUNDANT DETACH IS NOT A DEPARTURE AND MUST NOT TRIP THE FLOOR** — removing the explicit attachment of a tax the plan's own jurisdiction still mandates changes no money at all (**$6.25 either side**), so the floor is computed over what the plan will tax AFTER the detach and never by subtracting the departing tax a second time. That subtraction was the first fix's bug and `tc6` is what caught it. The original text follows. | **AMENDED 2026-08-22 (PO)** · supersedes-refines the mandated-only reading | **CONFIRMED (2026-08-22, amended same day)** | `tests/test_br_rat_113_attach_taxes.py` tc4 · tc4b · **tc4c** · tc6 |
| BR-RAT-115 (superseded first reading, kept for the chain) | **A MANDATED TAX MAY BE DETACHED FROM A PLAN — THE FLOOR IS AT LEAST ONE TAX LEFT STANDING.** PO verbatim (2026-08-22): _"we ask madetory atleast 1 tax shuld be there"_. SUPERSEDES-REFINES the BR-RAT-113-era absolute refusal (`rating.plans.mandated_tax_not_detachable`): staff may detach any tax — jurisdiction-mandated included — with the standard reason + version bump (BR-RAT-037), UNLESS the detach would leave the plan carrying **zero** taxes, which refuses by name with the count. Worked: a plan carrying only Texas state 6.25% ($6.25 on $100.00) refuses the detach; the same plan also carrying a city fee allows either to go. The jurisdiction BINDING is untouched — this governs the plan's tax set, not the authority's existence. | PO ruling 2026-08-22 · supersedes-refines the BR-RAT-113 refusal · CR-021 | **CONFIRMED (2026-08-22)** | `tests/test_br_rat_113_attach_taxes.py` (floor cases) |
| BR-RAT-116 | **A SERVICE-SCOPED TAX'S BASE IS THE SCOPED SERVICES' UTILITY SERVICE CHARGE PLUS BASE FEES — a whole-bill discount does not shrink it (closes OQ-331).** PO verbatim (2026-08-22): _"tax is always on Utility service charge + base fees"_. On the OQ-331 worked bill — $59.00 electric + $40.00 water, SENIOR −10% whole-bill (−$9.90), 1.5% electric-only franchise fee — the fee line is **$0.89** (1.5% × $59.00), never $0.74 (whole reduction off electric) or $0.80 (pro-rata). `DiscountTaxBaseUnruled` retires; the refused bill prices. **The boundary is stated because it matters:** BR-RAT-106's whole-bill before/after treatment carries its own finance signature and is NOT altered by this rule — a whole-bill tax under `before` still taxes the discounted subtotal. | PO ruling 2026-08-22 · closes OQ-331 · BR-RAT-106 untouched | **CONFIRMED (2026-08-22)** | `compute_tax` scoped-base cases: $0.89 |
| BR-RAT-890 | **A RATE PLAN CANNOT BE PUBLISHED WITHOUT A START DATE, AND A PUBLISHED PLAN CANNOT HAVE ITS DATE CLEARED.** PO ruling 2026-08-28, closing `DEF-494`. The question put was *"a rate plan has been published, but nobody ever typed a start date for it — should it be charging customers?"* and the PO took neither obvious answer: **require a date to publish, so the state stops existing**; any published plan without one gets a date. **Why it mattered:** `selectors.plan_ref` — what the bill run reads — filters on `status='published'` and consults **no date at all**, so an undated published plan bills FOREVER; `resolution.PlanVersion.in_force_on` requires a stated date, so the SAME plan is in force NEVER and R1 refuses the account. Both readings are defensible and `BR-RAT-007` chooses neither. **Measured on the seeded tenant through the real bill run and the real shadow report, three runs each: 47 of 47 accounts refused at R1 before, 0 after, and the comparison now reaches R3.** R1 gates every later step, so this single ambiguity made R2–R7 unmeasurable. ⚠ **BOTH HALVES ARE REQUIRED OR THE GATE IS DECORATIVE:** `publish_plan` refuses an undated plan, and `update_plan` refuses setting the date back to NULL on a PUBLISHED plan — without the second, the state the PO ruled out of existence returns one request later, on a plan already billing customers. **Clearing the date on a DRAFT stays lawful** — a draft prices nothing, and `BR-RAT-031`'s save-and-come-back is what a draft is for. | PO ruling 2026-08-28 · decision log 2026-08-28 · `DEF-494` · `BR-RAT-007` · `BR-RAT-030` · `BR-RAT-031` | **CONFIRMED (2026-08-28)** · BUILT (🔒 rating math + migration ordering — `rating/0024`, HUMAN-REVIEW-PENDING) | `services.publish_plan` · `services.update_plan` · `migrations/0024_def494_publish_needs_a_start_date.py` |
