| BR-RAT-117 | **A CHARGE CODE MARKED `recurring` AND `auto_apply` LANDS ON EVERY ORDINARY BILL WITHOUT THE PLAN NAMING IT.** PO verbatim (2026-08-22): _"ruccuring and Autom apply land on bill"_ — closing engine-cutover step 1. **The gate is BOTH flags together.** A code that is recurring but not auto-apply, or auto-apply but one-time/refundable/penalty, is NOT auto-applied — those wait for their own trigger, which is what `charge_type` is for. **The narrowing is R4's and does not change:** `resolve_charge_codes` filters an auto-applied code by service, by `applies_to_customer_classes` (BR-RAT-108's inclusion list, NULL/[] = every class) and by connection size, so a water fee never reaches an electric-only account and a residential-scoped one never reaches a commercial account. **BILLED ONCE, ALWAYS:** a code that is auto-applied AND named by a plan produces ONE line — the selection is a union keyed on the code, never two passes; the $24.00-for-one-$12.00-fee failure BR-RAT-088 was written from is pinned by a test here too. **Worked:** a $12.00/month recurring auto-apply water service fee across 1,247 water connections is **$14,964.00** a month that the product bills as **$0.00** today unless every plan names the code — the silence is the defect this rule closes. ⚠ **THIS DOES NOT MOVE THE BASE.** `is_base` still owns the base charge (BR-RAT-088) and an auto-applied non-base code is an ADDITIONAL line, never a second base — `resolve_base_charge`'s two-base refusal (DEF-468) stands untouched. ⚠ **AMENDED 2026-09-19 (PO ruling, `DEF-RAT-5784`): A PLAN NAMING A CHARGE CODE MAKES IT *AVAILABLE*; THE CODE'S OWN TRIGGER STILL DECIDES WHETHER IT BILLS. NOTHING IN THIS RULE IS REVERSED — AN OVER-IMPLEMENTATION OF IT IS WITHDRAWN.** PO verbatim: _"this is correct understanding - option a is ocrect - A — the charge code decides / Naming a charge in a plan makes it available. Whether it bills monthly is the charge code's own setting"_, and, the same day and on the same subject, _"plan is only binding - even plan should not talk about rules here"_ (`DEF-RAT-5779`). **WHAT WAS ACTUALLY BUILT AND IS NOW CORRECTED.** `DEF-561` (2026-08-22) found that NEITHER route reached a bill — `plan_charge_rules` had no billing reader at all and `gather` left `fees` empty — and built `selectors.billable_charge_codes` as a union of two routes. **Route 2 applied no `charge_type` test and no `auto_apply` test**, so anything a plan named rode every cycle whatever its declared trigger. **Read literally, this rule's own words authorise ROUTE 1 ONLY** — _"a charge code marked `recurring` AND `auto_apply` lands on every ordinary bill WITHOUT THE PLAN NAMING IT"_ — and say nothing that exempts a plan-named code from its own trigger; `DEF-561` went further than the words it cited. **MEASURED ON `brazosbend`, 2026-09-19:** 9 `plan_charge_rules` attachments, 5 distinct codes, **every one `penalty` or `one_time`** — `TAMPER` `$250.00`, `NSF` `$30.00`, `CONN` `$45.00`, `DISC` `$45.00`, `LATE` (no amount) — over 10 units on 3 plans: **`$955.00` a month, `$11,460.00` a year, and a `$250.00` tamper fee billed twelve times a year without anyone tampering.** **AND NOTHING CORRECT DEPENDED ON IT:** that tenant holds **zero** `recurring`+`auto_apply` codes, so route 1 selected nothing and **every fee line the product could produce was one of those nine**; the three `Sample —` plans carrying 78 of its 88 units name no charge code at all. After the fix all three plans bill **`$0.00`** of fees. **THE TRIGGER IS `BR-RAT-730`'s, UNCHANGED:** `charge_type == 'recurring'` AND `auto_apply`, applied to the WHOLE union rather than to route 1 alone. **`is_base` IS STILL EXCLUDED BY NAME HERE** (BR-RAT-088 — the base has its own route), which is a deliberate and pinned divergence from R4's version of the predicate, where a base code rides regardless. ⚠ **AND THE GATE MAKES ROUTE 2 A SUBSET OF ROUTE 1, WHICH IS STATED RATHER THAN HIDDEN:** `auto_apply` IS _"rides without the plan naming it"_, so the plan binding now selects nothing route 1 has not already selected. Whether a utility should be able to put a monthly fee on ONE plan — a `recurring` code, not auto-applied, named by that plan — is a real capability this closes off and is **`OQ-RAT-12463`, OPEN**; it is not decided here, because the product has no screen that expresses it and `DEF-RAT-5779`'s step-4 _On every bill_ column (derived from exactly `auto_apply AND recurring`) would print **No** beside a code that billed monthly. | PO ruling 2026-08-22 (engine cutover step 1) · **PO ruling 2026-09-19 (`DEF-RAT-5784`)** · `DEF-RAT-5779` · `BR-RAT-730` · `OQ-RAT-12463` · `OQ-RAT-12464` · `DEF-561` · decision log 2026-08-22 · BR-RAT-088 · BR-RAT-108 · R4 | **CONFIRMED (2026-08-22)** · TO BUILD (🔒 rating/billing math — machine gates + HUMAN-REVIEW-PENDING) | R4 `resolve_charge_codes` · `apps/billing/gather.py` fee-line reader · pin: one line, $12.00, not two |
| BR-RAT-118 | **THE PRODUCT'S SERVICE VOCABULARY WINS OVER r10's, IN BOTH HALVES — THE NAME IS `Trash` AND THE BADGES ARE `St`/`So`/`Th`/`Ba` (PO ruling 2026-08-24, closing DEF-636).** PO verbatim, on the name: ***"We call trash"***. **This is the FIRST ruling where the prototype loses**, and it does not weaken the 2026-08-16 reference default (*"for this session we will be refering to r10 - as default"*) — that default settles LAYOUT, COPY, FLOWS and STATES, and a service's own name is none of those. It is the product's word for one of its ten services, and CLAUDE.md's seed list has said `Trash` since the platform seed was written. **The badge half follows from the same sentence and needs no second ruling, because the two halves have the same subject and the same answer: where B19's drawing and the shipped product name the same service differently, the product's name is the product's name.** **Why r10 differs at all, which is the part worth keeping:** four services — storm water, solar, thermal/heat, battery — were added by **OQ-273 AFTER r10 was drawn**, and their obvious initials were already taken (`S`=Sewer, `T`=Trash, `B`=Broadband). r10 solved that collision by inventing new single letters `D`/`O`/`H`/`Y`; the product solved it by going to two characters, `St`/`So`/`Th`/`Ba`. Both are valid answers to a collision r10 could not have known about. The product's is the one already on screen. **Worked, and it is why this was a defect rather than a preference:** the CR-030 W2 Accounts list already renders `St` in its service-chip column. Building REQ-03.2 as B19 §3 draws it would have put `D` on the request form and `St` on the accounts list **for the same service, in the same product, two clicks apart**. ✅ **NOTHING SHIPS DIVERGENT AND NOTHING NEEDED CHANGING IN CODE.** `serviceRequestShared.tsx` already imports `serviceBadge()` rather than re-declaring letters, and every card renders `rating.service.<code>` from the one catalogue — `rating.service.trash` = `Trash` in en.json and translated in the other ten. The two places carrying r10's words were both DOCUMENTS (B19 §3's table and §8.6's open item), now stamped. ⚠ **THE GENERAL FORM, so the next divergence does not need the PO again:** where r10 and the shipped product NAME the same thing differently, the product wins and the divergence is stamped in B19. Where r10 and the product LAY OUT or SEQUENCE the same thing differently, r10 still wins (2026-08-16). A name is not a layout. | PO 2026-08-24 (DEF-636) · BR-RAT-024 (the ten services) · OQ-273 (the four added after r10) · CLAUDE.md service seed order · B19 §3 · §8.6 | CONFIRMED | W4 · shipped |
| BR-RAT-650 | **A TAX LEVIES ON THE FULL SERVICE VALUE UNLESS ITS OWN AUTHORITY SAYS OTHERWISE — `taxes.discount_treatment` DEFAULTS TO `after` (PO ruling 2026-08-27, closing `DEF-652`).** PO verbatim: *"Does a discount come off before or after tax? Tax the full amount before discount — The utility remits tax on the full service value; the discount is the utility's cost, not the tax authority's. - what does the tax law in the US say - lets go with it"*, confirming an earlier *"this is correct - A $62.14 bill with a 25% senior discount of $15.54 — is the 6.85% tax charged on $62.14 ($4.26)"*. ⚠ **READ THE NAMES CAREFULLY: they describe when the DISCOUNT is applied, not when the tax is.** `after` puts the discount after the tax, so the authority levies on the FULL amount — which is what the ruling asks for. **Worked, and this is the figure that moved:** a **$62.14** bill with a 25% senior discount of **$15.54** was taxed at 6.85% on the reduced **$46.60** and charged **$3.19**; it is now taxed on the full **$62.14** and charged **$4.26** — **$1.07 more per discounted bill per cycle**, on every discounted account of every tenant. **THE COLUMN IS NOT COLLAPSED AND `BR-RAT-106` IS NOT WEAKENED:** the treatment stays statutory and PER TAX, `before` stays lawful and storable, and TAX-02's *Discounts* select still offers both — an authority that levies on the discounted amount is one choice away. **EXISTING ROWS WERE MOVED** (migration `rating 0023`, per tenant under that tenant's own RLS context) because no stored `before` was ever a utility's answer: every row predates the ruling, and the control shipped pre-selected to `before`. ⚠ **NOTHING ALREADY ISSUED CHANGES** — hard rule 4 makes an issued invoice immutable, so the new figure applies from the next bill run and a re-pricing of past bills is a CORRECTION (new rows, a new run), never an edit. ⚠ **A NULL reaching `selectors.taxable_subtotal_for` still answers `before`, deliberately:** a NULL there is not a row's answer — the column is NOT NULL — it is a SEAM that published nothing, and a wiring gap must keep behaving as it always has rather than quietly acquiring a new money rule. **NO ❄️ FROZEN FIGURE MOVES:** B4 and B5 are silent on discounts, and `10-scripts/03-golden-verify.py` reads 33 PASS / 0 FAIL before and after — the new case is a separately counted part 3, marked ruling-derived rather than finance-signed. | PO ruling 2026-08-27 · `DEF-652` · SUPERSEDES the default clause of `BR-RAT-106` · hard rule 4 · B4 ❄️ (silent) | **CONFIRMED (2026-08-28)** | `rating.models.Tax.discount_treatment` · migration `rating 0023` · `tests/test_def_652_tax_on_the_full_service_value.py` · `03-golden-verify.py` part 3 |
| BR-RAT-690 | ⚠ **STORAGE HALF SUPERSEDED 2026-10-01 by `BR-RAT-1049` (`CR-156-BRC10`, PO *"logical kill"*): a save no longer deletes and re-inserts the code's rows — an unchanged row is untouched, a changed or removed one is CLOSED and marked killed, the new price is a NEW row. The validation half below (the three states — no row · NULL row · `0.00` row — and the allowed size set) STANDS.** **A CHARGE CODE'S AMOUNT MAY BE STATED PER CONNECTION SIZE, AND THE SIZE MUST BE ONE THE TENANT CONFIGURED (`DEF-2190`, 2026-08-28).** ⚠ **NO PO RULING IS CLAIMED. This is the WRITE PATH for a rule already CONFIRMED** — `BR-RAT-088` (PO 2026-08-20) moved the base amount onto the charge code and worked its own example on a base that differs by size: **a 5/8" water base at `$8.00` and a 2" at `$34.00`, on ONE code**. `charge_code_amounts` shipped with a model, a migration and four readers and **no writer at any layer**: `CHARGE_FIELDS` carried no `size_amounts` key, so `POST /charges` refused the field by name as unknown and nothing but a test could write the table. **Worked, on the live chain, before and after:** with one scalar of `$8.00`, `resolve_charge_codes` answers **`$8.00` for a 5/8" and `$8.00` for a 2"**; with the table, **`$8.00` and `$34.00`** — **`$26.00` a month per 2" connection**, and nothing could see it because with the table EMPTY a per-size read and a scalar read agree for every tenant. **THREE STATES, KEPT APART END TO END (`DEF-277`):** a size with **no row** is one nobody priced and R4 refuses by name; a row with a **NULL amount** says the utility **does not serve** that size and R4 refuses by name; a row reading **`0.0000`** is a PRICE and bills a `$0.00` line. A blank input posts as `null` and never as `0`. **THE SIZE IS CHOSEN, NEVER TYPED.** The allowed set is the tenant's own ACTIVE `service_size_classes` (`BR-TEN-031`/`170`) — the SAME rows `/lookups/service_size_classes` publishes to AST-05's connection-size picker and RAT-03's size matrix — **UNIONED with the sizes this code has already stored**, so a size Settings RETIRES does not make a code that already priced it unsaveable (`ChargeCodeAmount`'s own rule). ⚠ **AND THIS IS A DELIBERATE DIVERGENCE FROM `_validate_size_rows` ONE SCREEN ACROSS,** which checks nothing and says why: an existence check would refuse the retired-size case. The union honours that reason without its cost — a typed `W58` where the tenant configured `W-58` would otherwise store a row nothing ever looks up, and the bill would refuse on billing day for a base the utility believes it set. **ONE BASIS, REFUSED AT THE WRITE.** `resolve_charge_codes` already refuses a code stating BOTH a flat amount and a per-size table — *"CR-013 part 2 gives a code one basis; this chain refuses rather than choosing which figure the customer is billed"* — and it refuses ON BILLING DAY. The same law now fires at the save, which also closes a silent preference: `charge_code_facts` sets `flat_amount=None if amounts else …`, so a code holding both would quietly bill the TABLE and ignore the scalar somebody typed. **OFFERED ON A SERVICE SCOPE, NOT ON `is_base`** — `resolve_charge_codes` walks EVERY charge code and asks for a per-size amount whenever the code carries a table, so gating the input on `is_base` would leave a capability the engine already consumes unreachable for every other code; and it is the condition `stepTwoSections` has used since CHG-02 shipped, so WHO is asked has not changed. ⚠ **AND ON `amountFieldsFor(rule).amount`, WHICH IS ONLY `flat`:** a per-size table IS the flat amount stated once per size, and `charge_code_amount` refuses a percentage code (*"R1-R7 resolves rating facts and is handed no balance"*, `DEF-498`) before it ever looks at a size table — so per-size figures on one would be a configuration nothing can ever read. **NO BILL MOVES TODAY:** `BR-RAT-120` records that the chain still has no production caller (`gather._base_charge_for` reads the plan's column), so this makes a figure SETTABLE and changes no invoice. ⚠ **AMENDED 2026-09-01 (PO ruling, `CR-057`): THE *"NOTHING IS SWITCHED ON"* CLAUSE IN THIS RULE IS SUPERSEDED — R3 AND R4 NOW PRICE REAL BILLS.** `BILLING_RESOLUTION_STEPS` ships `"R3,R4"`, so `apps/billing/resolution_switch.py` is a production caller of the chain. Nothing else in this rule moves — what it says R4 or R3 WILL do is what they now DO. Measured before the flip: 3 utilities, 163 accounts, **163 unchanged, 0 moved, 0 refused**. Rollback is `SBH_BILLING_RESOLUTION_STEPS=` emptied, one line, no release. See `BR-RAT-120` and `BR-BIL-870` for the full amendment. | `DEF-2190` (CLOSED here) · **implements `BR-RAT-088`** (CONFIRMED 2026-08-20) · `DEF-277` (blank is not zero) · `DEF-431` (the read half) · `BR-RAT-089`/`092` (store 4, display 2) · `BR-TEN-031`/`170` · CR-013 part 2 ("one basis") · `BR-RAT-120` (nothing is switched on) | **BUILT 2026-08-28 · HUMAN-REVIEW-PENDING** (rating money path — row in `12-governance/09-human-review-ledger.md`) | `apps/rating/services.py` (`_validate_size_amounts`, `_write_size_amounts`, `CHARGE_FIELDS`) · `apps/rating/seams.py` (`configured_size_class_codes`) · `07-frontend/src/features/rating/ChargeCodeWizard.tsx` (`PerSizeAmounts`) · `06-backend/tests/test_br_rat_690_per_size_amounts.py` |
| BR-RAT-830 | **A SAMPLE-DATA REPLAY ADDS WHAT IS GENUINELY MISSING TO AN ALREADY-SEEDED PLAN — IDEMPOTENCY IS PER OBJECT, NEVER PER PARENT (`DEF-1250`).** `BR-TEN-063` says a replay must not double anything. Taxes, base charge codes, rate schedules, schedule versions and discounts each implement that keyed on their **own natural key**, so a replay adds an object that is genuinely new; **plans were keyed on the plan's `code`**, so an existing plan was skipped WHOLE — contents included — and a service the tenant switched on afterwards could never reach it. *"If the parent exists, add nothing"* is strictly **stronger** than the rule it implements: strong enough to also refuse what is genuinely absent, which is why it read as correct. **MEASURED on a booted tenant** (provision `water + sewer`, load, switch trash on at S8, load again): the replay DID add the trash schedule and the `BASE-T` charge code and `SCW-RES` still priced `water, sewer` — a trash schedule no plan pointed at. **THE REPLAY NOW RECONCILES THE SERVICE SET, under three guards, and each guard is a defect it would otherwise be:** **(1) SAMPLE PLANS ONLY** — `BR-TEN-068` promotes a sample plan to the operator's own the moment a real account is on it, and a fixture may not reprice a live tariff; **(2) ADDITIVE, NEVER A REWRITE** — `update_plan` REPLACES the service set, so the payload is *the plan's current rows restated verbatim* plus the missing ones, carrying every field (base, minimum, GL override, `outside_city_exempt`, tariff) so that no operator edit is silently reset (`BR-TEN-012`); a NULL base stays NULL, because a blank base means *this size is not served* and a `"0"` would convert a refusal into a `$0.00` line (`CR-013` part 1 / `DEF-277`); **(3) THROUGH `update_plan`** — adding a priced service to a PUBLISHED plan is a rate change, so `BR-RAT-037`'s version bump and required reason, `BR-RAT-111`'s filing record and the audit row all happen; a direct `PlanService.objects.create` would give a utility a plan whose filing history does not mention the day it started charging for trash. **A second replay writes nothing and does not bump the version**, which is `BR-TEN-063`'s own test. | `DEF-1250` · `BR-TEN-063` · `BR-TEN-068` · `BR-TEN-012` · `BR-RAT-037` / `BR-RAT-111` · `CR-013` part 1 / `DEF-277` · `BR-BIL-300` (the bill half, already built) | **CONFIRMED · BUILT 2026-08-28** — a lane's judgment recorded with its worked figure, not a PO ruling: it implements `BR-TEN-063` at the grain the rule is about and changes no signed figure | `apps/rating/sample_data.py` (`_current_plan_services`, `_reconcile_plan_services`) · `tests/test_def_1250_enable_after_seed.py` tc1–tc4 |
| BR-RAT-831 | **THE TENANT'S ENABLED SERVICES THAT NO PUBLISHED PLAN PRICES ARE REPORTED BY NAME (`DEF-1250` prevention (a)).** `BR-TEN-060`'s report-by-name, pointed at a configuration gap rather than at an unwired seam: the sample-data run states, once per tenant, which services this utility sells that nothing prices. **It does not overlap with `BR-RAT-830`** — the reconciliation repairs the SAMPLE plans for the services the sample estate is shaped for, and cannot help a utility whose plans are entirely their own, or one that never reloads the sample data. ⚠ **AMENDED 2026-09-01 (`DEF-2397`): this sentence named **gas** as the example, on the ground that *"the fixture has no gas shape"*, and that half is no longer true.** `apps/rating/sample_data` is service-shaped now — it asks `core.sample_fixture.estate_plan` the same question metering and assets ask, so a gas utility receives a gas schedule, two plans and its `[W] §1` `$6.00` base. The RULE is untouched and so is everything else this row says; only the example was stale, and it was stale because the fixture and the tariff were shaped by two different rules — which is the defect itself. Those tenants sell something no plan prices, and under `BR-BIL-300` / `OQ-670` (PO 2026-08-27, *"(b) Bill $0.00"*) every account whose unit takes such a service now bills a `$0.00` line for it — better than the silence `DEF-1060` was, and still not an answer. **A READ, never a write:** what an ENABLE should do to an already-published plan — add the service to every plan, or offer it — is a product decision and is filed as `DEF-2492`, not chosen here. | `DEF-1250` prevention (a) · `BR-TEN-060` · `BR-BIL-300` / `OQ-670` · `DEF-1060` | **CONFIRMED · BUILT 2026-08-28** (the read; the screen half is `DEF-2492`, open) | `apps/rating/sample_data.enabled_services_no_published_plan_prices` · `apps/rating/seams.enabled_service_codes` · `tests/test_def_1250_enable_after_seed.py` tc7 |
| BR-RAT-832 | **THE SEEDED TRASH BASE IS `$0.00` — `OQ-317` CLOSED 2026-08-27 ON OPTION A AND RETIRED THE WORKBOOK'S `$18.00` MONTHLY BASE (`DEF-2490`).** PO verbatim: *"option A is correct; where do you see the carts - they are not on accounts theya re on Units."* **Worked: a three-cart household is `3 x $15.00` = `$45.00`, not `$63.00`; a one-cart household is `$15.00`, not `$33.00`.** Across the demo tenant's 48 trash accounts the retirement is `$864.00` a month. ⚠ **IT IS A STORED ZERO, NOT AN OMISSION, AND THE DIFFERENCE IS A REFUSAL:** `CR-013` part 1 (PO 2026-08-19) makes a BLANK base mean *this size is not served* and `gather._base_charge_for` stops the bill on it (`DEF-277`), while a stored zero prints `$0.00` (PO 2026-08-19, *"print $0.00"*). Retiring a base is *trash has no base*, not *trash is not served*. **`core/sample_fixture.TRASH_FLAT` is kept as the RECORDED superseded `[W] §1` figure — a signed figure is superseded, never deleted — and is read by nothing;** `TRASH_BASE` is what the plan and `BASE-T` both read, and it cites `OQ-317` at the line the figure sits on, which is `DEF-491`'s own prevention and the thing that would have caught this row. | `OQ-317` CLOSED 2026-08-27 (option A) · `OQ-303` / `BR-RAT-090` (`$15.00` per cart) · `DEF-2490` · `DEF-491` · `CR-013` part 1 / `DEF-277` · billing workbook `[W] §1` (SUPERSEDED) | **CONFIRMED · BUILT 2026-08-28** — implementing a PO ruling already recorded, not a new figure | `core/sample_fixture.TRASH_BASE` · `apps/rating/sample_data.py` (`_plan_payloads`, `BASE_CHARGE_CODES`) · `tests/test_def_1250_enable_after_seed.py` tc6 |
| BR-RAT-960 | **A USAGE ALLOWANCE TAKES ITS UNIT FROM THE SERVICE IT IS ATTACHED TO, AND IS THEREFORE A ONE-SERVICE DISCOUNT (PO ruling 2026-08-31, closing `OQ-291`).** PO verbatim: _"a) Take the unit from the service it's attached to"_. `discount_versions.value` holds the NUMBER and no column holds a unit; the unit is now RESOLVED from the service the discount's scope names — its billed unit as the tenant holds it (`tenant_services.billed_uom`, falling back to the register's own `primary_uom`, which is what a NULL `billed_uom` MEANS and not "unconfigured"). **WORKED, AND THE TWO FIGURES ARE THE WHOLE RULING: the SAME stored row `value = 2` is worth `$14.40` on water and `$0.28` on electric.** On water priced `0-5 @ 3.20 / 5-15 @ 4.80 / 15+ @ 7.20` a customer reading `18.72 kgal` pays `$90.78`; the allowance resolves to **`kgal`**, deducts before the tier walk to `16.72 kgal`, and the bill is `$76.38` — **`$14.40`**, because the two kilogallons come off the TOP block at `7.20`. On `Res Electric · Tiered` (`0-500 @ 0.11 / 500-1,000 @ 0.14 / 1,000+ @ 0.19`) a customer reading `620 kWh` pays `500 × 0.11 + 120 × 0.14 = $71.80`; the same row resolves to **`kWh`**, deducts to `618 kWh`, and the bill is `$71.52` — **`$0.28`**. **A 51-fold difference decided entirely by which service the discount is attached to**, which is exactly the silent failure `OQ-291` described and this rule removes. ⚠ **THE RECOMMENDATION WE MADE WAS DECLINED AND THAT IS RECORDED RATHER THAN FOLDED AWAY.** The lane recommended option (B), a `value_uom` column, arguing CLAUDE.md hard rule 3 — _"consumption: always with an explicit unit; never assume"_ — and that deriving a unit is an inference rather than a stated fact. The PO chose the narrower, cheaper answer. **Hard rule 3 survives in the one way it still can: the inference is SHOWN, never silent.** The resolved unit is printed beside the figure wherever the allowance is displayed (`selectors.discount_value_display` serves `2.00 kgal`, not a bare `2.00`) and named in every refusal; and a service that states NO unit still REFUSES rather than being given one — an assumed unit is the thing hard rule 3 forbids, and the ruling supplies a source, not a default. ⚠ **THE SECOND HALF IS DECIDED BY IMPLICATION AND IS OURS TO WRITE DOWN, NOT TO RE-ASK (law 13).** A discount attached to the WHOLE BILL names no service, so there is nothing to take a unit from. **A usage allowance is a ONE-SERVICE discount.** Any other scope (`whole_bill`, `usage_only`, `base_only`, `named_codes`) **REFUSES AT SETUP** — in `_validate_version`, on the `applies_to` field, where a clerk is typing — and NEVER at billing time. The four non-`one_service` scopes fail for one identical reason (none names a single service); the ruling names `whole_bill` because that is the case it was shown. **A refusal a clerk meets while configuring is a correction; the same refusal met during a bill run is an outage.** ⚠ **THIS RULE MOVES NO BILL, AND THAT IS DELIBERATE.** `usage_allowance` stays OUT of `DISCOUNT_TYPES_PRICEABLE` — see `BR-RAT-962`, which states why the blocker changed identity rather than disappearing. | PO ruling 2026-08-31 (decision log, _FIVE PO RULINGS IN ONE REPLY_ §3) · `OQ-291` CLOSED · CR-013 part 3 (the five types) · CLAUDE.md hard rule 3 · `BR-RAT-038` (no silent conversion) · `BR-TEN-078` · `DEF-355` | **CONFIRMED (2026-08-31)** · BUILT (🔒 rating math — `resolution.py`, `discount_services.py`, `selectors.py`, all HUMAN-REVIEW-PENDING) | `tests/test_po_2026_08_31_discount_rulings.py` TC1–TC9 · `resolution.usage_allowance_units` · `resolution.allowance_deducted_quantity` · `discount_services._validate_version` GATE 3 · `selectors.discount_value_display` |
| BR-RAT-961 | **A SUBSIDY IS PRICED ON THE STANDARD RATE SCHEDULE WITH AN ORDINARY DISCOUNT LINE ON TOP; A DISCOUNT NEVER SWAPS A RATE SCHEDULE (PO ruling 2026-08-31, closing `OQ-292`).** PO verbatim: _"we will apply standard rate and also provdie discount - thats how it should be handled; sicne there is subsidy we dont change rate schedule"_. **THE RULING IS NONE OF THE THREE OPTIONS WE OFFERED** — record the target schedule on the discount, express it as a plan variant, or cut the type — and it dissolves the question instead of answering it: a subsidy is not a different rate table. **WORKED END TO END ON THE SEEDED ESTATE, three figures, no invention:** a `620 kWh` residential customer on `Res Electric · Tiered` (`0-500 @ 0.11 / 500-1,000 @ 0.14 / 1,000+ @ 0.19`) is charged the STANDARD schedule — `500 × 0.11 + 120 × 0.14 = ` **`$71.80`**; the seeded `CAP / low-income credit` programme (a `$25.00` fixed amount on the whole bill, City-funded, reported in the annual assistance filing) takes off **`$25.00`**; the customer pays **`$46.80`**, and BOTH numbers are printed. **The contrast is the point:** a `rate_override` onto a half-price subsidy schedule would have printed ONE line of `$35.90` and nothing on the bill would say a subsidy had been granted. **SO `Res Electric · Tiered (subsidy)` IS NOT A SCHEDULE SWAP.** `CR-013` part 3's sentence — _"Rate override is the mechanism behind 'Res Electric · Tiered (subsidy)'"_ — is **SUPERSEDED on that point** (stamped in `12-governance/11-change-requests.md` at the sentence itself). ⚠ **THE RULING RESTORES A RULE WE ALREADY HAD.** `BR-RAT-068` (CONFIRMED 2026-08-19) says a discount _"is an overlay, never a rate ... it therefore owns no tier, no schedule and no connection-size row"_ and that _"the bill shows the full tariff amount and the reduction as its own line, so subsidy granted is reportable"_. **The `rate_override` type contradicted that CONFIRMED rule from the day it was stored**, and nothing noticed because the type was never priceable. The PO's answer is `BR-RAT-068` applied. **WHAT THIS RULING REMOVES THE NEED FOR, each named so nobody rebuilds it:** no `override_tariff_id` column on `discount_versions`, no retired-schedule refusal and no mismatched-service refusal — all three were costs of a mechanism the PO has said we do not use here. `resolution.rate_override_target` therefore stays unimplemented and now names THIS RULING rather than an open question. `resolve_schedule_with_override` and `RateOverrideUnusable` are **KEPT and unreachable** — removing a discount type is its own change with its own blast radius and is not taken in the lane that closed the question (the precedent is `BR-RAT-094`: _"the columns are not dropped ... dropping it is a separate change and needs its own CR"_). | PO ruling 2026-08-31 (decision log, _FIVE PO RULINGS IN ONE REPLY_ §4) · `OQ-292` CLOSED · `BR-RAT-068` (the overlay rule this restores) · CR-013 part 3 (superseded on the rate-override sentence) · `BR-RAT-094` (the precedent for keeping a closed column) | **CONFIRMED (2026-08-31)** · BUILT (🔒 rating math — `resolution.py`, HUMAN-REVIEW-PENDING) | `tests/test_po_2026_08_31_discount_rulings.py` TC10–TC14 · `resolution.rate_override_target` |
| BR-RAT-962 | **NEITHER `rate_override` NOR `usage_allowance` BECOMES PRICEABLE ON THE STRENGTH OF THE 2026-08-31 RULINGS, AND THE REASON IS NOT THE ONE THE CODE USED TO GIVE.** `DISCOUNT_TYPES_PRICEABLE` is `(percentage, fixed_amount, charge_waiver)` and is UNCHANGED by `BR-RAT-960` / `BR-RAT-961`. **The blocker changed identity rather than disappearing, and saying so is the whole rule** — every comment in the tree that named `OQ-291` or `OQ-292` as the reason was TRUE yesterday and is FALSE today, which is hard-won law 1 (_a comment claiming a guarantee is not the guarantee_) pointed at a reason instead of a promise. **FOR `usage_allowance` THE REASON IS THE STEP, AND IT IS ARITHMETIC:** that set gates `discount_pricing._raw_reduction`, which is **R6 — a subtraction of MONEY from a subtotal**. An allowance is a deduction of **UNITS** before R3b's tier walk. Adding it to the set would price a `2 kgal` allowance as **`$2.00` off** where the ruled arithmetic makes it **`$14.40`** — a `$12.40` error on one bill, in the customer's disfavour, on a discount the utility believes it granted. The remaining true blocker is `DEF-438`: the R1–R7 chain has no production caller, so the deduction is reachable only through the ruled cutover's step 5, gated on its step 4 (`CR-052` task 20). **FOR `rate_override` THE REASON IS THAT THERE IS NOTHING LEFT TO PRICE** — `BR-RAT-961` removed its only stated use and `BR-RAT-068` forbids the shape. ⚠ **SO NO BILL MOVES AND NO GOLDEN FIGURE MOVES**, and that is the correct outcome for a lane that closed a DATA question: `10-scripts/03-golden-verify.py` reads **33 PASS / 0 FAIL** before and after. Anyone reading this rule as permission to widen the set is reading it backwards — widening it is a money change and needs step 4. | `DEF-438` · `CR-052` W5 tasks 17/20 · `CR-051`'s cutover method (PO 2026-08-27, _"run both, compare, rule, then switch. Never swap and hope"_) · `BR-RAT-960` · `BR-RAT-961` · hard-won law 1 · ⚠ **DATED NOTE 2026-10-03 — THE TYPE IS NO LONGER OFFERED; THIS RULE IS UNCHANGED.** PO ruling (decision log 2026-10-03, option b, verbatim *"recommend (b) - this is correct"*): because this rule keeps a usage allowance unpriceable, the New discount form stops offering *Usage allowance*, and the server refuses a NEW one by name (`rating.discounts.usage_allowance_not_offered`) — create, a new version, or an edit turning a draft into one. A usage allowance saved before the ruling stays readable on the list and details page, opens on Edit with its type read-only, and cannot be submitted (it could never be approved). | BR-RAT-1010 | **THE CHARGE CODE STATES WHICH CONNECTION SIZES ARE PRICED — THE RATE SCHEDULE DOES NOT. (PO ruling 2026-09-01.)** PO verbatim: _"rate schedule will not provdie the size, charge code will provide"_, said in the same message as _"What can actually release R3 — The refusal. Where a rate schedule doesn't price a connection's size at all"_. ⚠ **THIS COMPLETES A RULE ALREADY SIGNED RATHER THAN OPENING A NEW ONE.** `BR-RAT-088` (CONFIRMED 2026-08-20) moved the base AMOUNT onto the charge code and `BR-RAT-690` (2026-08-28) gave `charge_code_amounts` its writer, its screen and its three ratified readings. What was left behind is the MEMBERSHIP half: `BR-RAT-088`'s own consequence clause reads _"a size absent from the **schedule** still means not served"_, so from 2026-08-20 the product answered **what does a 5/8" water connection cost** off the charge code and **is a 5/8" water connection priced at all** off the rate schedule's `rate_size_rows`. A utility that priced `BASE-W` per size on the screen `BR-RAT-690` built could still have that meter refused by a schedule that knew nothing about it. **THE RULE, AND IT NARROWS — IT NEVER WIDENS:** where a service declares an `is_base` charge code stating a per-size table, **THAT table is R3's axis and the schedule's configuration is not consulted**; a size absent from it REFUSES BY NAME at R3 and never falls back to another size's rate. **THREE CASES IT LEAVES EXACTLY AS THEY WERE.** (1) A code pricing **FLAT** states no axis at all — `BR-RAT-690`: _"a per-size table IS the flat amount stated once per size"_ — so the schedule answers, unchanged. This is the shipped fixture (`BASE-W` is seeded flat at `$8.00` on purpose per `BR-RAT-088`), which is why **no tenant on this database changes axis today and no bill and no fixture moves**. (2) A service with **no `is_base` code** keeps the schedule's configured sizes — the SAME legacy ladder R4 already uses for the amount, not a second mechanism. (3) **Sewer** (ruled sizeless, `BR-RAT-015`/`BR-TEN-171`) and the **never-ruled bands** (battery `OQ-561`, thermal steam `OQ-560`) are untouched: _ruled to have none_ and _nobody has said_ stay apart. ⚠ **THE UNION ACROSS CLASS-SCOPED CODES IS DELIBERATE.** `BR-RAT-108` scopes a code to customer classes, so one service can hold `BASE-W` (residential) and `BASE-W-COM` (commercial) with different tables. R3 is asked per SERVICE and is handed no customer class — **it answers WHICH SIZE and R4 answers WHOSE CODE** — so the axis is the union and a size priced for one class and not the other passes R3 and refuses at **R4** by name. Narrowing at R3 would refuse a commercial meter for a size its own code prices, naming the wrong step to the person who has to fix it. ⚠ **AND `BR-RAT-690`'s MIDDLE STATE SURVIVES:** a row whose amount is NULL is a size that EXISTS on the axis and refuses at **R4** — _the utility does not serve this size_ — because collapsing it into _nobody priced this size_ would send a clerk to add a row that is already there. ⚠ **THE REFUSAL SENTENCE NOW NAMES THE OBJECT THE AXIS CAME FROM** (`ScheduleVersion.size_axis_source`, which carries no money and gates nothing): _"has no row on the base charge code pricing 'water'"_ against _"…on the rate schedule version …"_. `BR-RAT-120` accepted a real cost — _"Read the second way, the system refuses bills that price perfectly well today"_ — on the understanding that the refusal is CURABLE, and a refusal naming the wrong screen is not. **WHY THIS RULE EXISTS NOW: it is what releases R3.** `DEF-2878` proved R3's shadow comparison was comparing one value with itself (the adapter reads the meter's size, hands it to the chain, and the chain's size step returns what it was given), so **`47 of 47 agree` was never evidence and no wider population could have made it so**. The REFUSAL is the half that is not circular — the size is the CONNECTION's fact, the axis is the CONFIGURATION's — and this ruling points it at the object a utility can actually edit. **NOTHING IS SWITCHED ON AND NO BILL CHANGES:** `SERVED_BY_CHAIN_DEFAULT` is empty and no deployment names a step (`BR-RAT-120`). **SUPERSESSION, STATED:** `BR-RAT-088`'s clause _"a size absent from the SCHEDULE still means not served"_ is **SUPERSEDED at that word only** — a size absent from the **charge code** means not served, where the service has one. Everything else in `BR-RAT-088` and all of `BR-RAT-120` stands, the sequencing clause included (sizes are loaded first, the chain is switched in after). ⚠ **AMENDED 2026-09-01 (PO ruling, `CR-057`): THE *"NOTHING IS SWITCHED ON"* CLAUSE IN THIS RULE IS SUPERSEDED — R3 AND R4 NOW PRICE REAL BILLS.** `BILLING_RESOLUTION_STEPS` ships `"R3,R4"`, so `apps/billing/resolution_switch.py` is a production caller of the chain. Nothing else in this rule moves — what it says R4 or R3 WILL do is what they now DO. Measured before the flip: 3 utilities, 163 accounts, **163 unchanged, 0 moved, 0 refused**. Rollback is `SBH_BILLING_RESOLUTION_STEPS=` emptied, one line, no release. See `BR-RAT-120` and `BR-BIL-870` for the full amendment. | PO ruling 2026-09-01 (decision log) · **completes `BR-RAT-088`** (CONFIRMED 2026-08-20, superseded at one clause) · `BR-RAT-690` (the per-size table's writer and its three readings) · `BR-RAT-120` (narrows, never widens; the sequencing) · `BR-RAT-108` (class scoping) · `BR-BIL-872` (a switched rule's refusal refuses the ACCOUNT) · `DEF-2878` (why the refusal is R3's only independent evidence) | **CONFIRMED (2026-09-01) · HUMAN-REVIEW-PENDING** (rating money path — row in `12-governance/09-human-review-ledger.md`) | `apps/rating/resolution_catalogue.py` (`base_code_sizes_for`, `configured_sizes_for`) · `apps/rating/resolution.py` (`ScheduleVersion.size_axis_source`, `resolve_served_size`) · `10-scripts/cr052_r1r7_shadow_measure.py` (`what_the_run_carries` — the per-utility coverage report R3's release is read off) · `06-backend/tests/test_br_rat_1010_charge_code_states_the_size.py` (TC1–TC8) |
| BR-RAT-1011 | **THE LATE FEE COMES FROM THE CHARGE CODE THE PLAN NAMES, AND A PLAN NAMING NONE CHARGES ZERO. (PO ruling 2026-09-07, `CR-078`; FINANCE RE-SIGNED the same day under HUMAN-GUIDE §6 because a late fee is a ❄️ FROZEN B4/B5 figure.)** PO verbatim: _"this is wrong - you ahve to take late fees form charge code mentione din the plan - if plan doent provide you that charge code then you have to put zero on late payment. - Logic aplplied ot Minimum late fee $5- - you can link these defaults in Charge code - we have a charge code for late payment"_. **WHAT IT REVERSES:** `BR-BIL-001`'s two terms — the 1.5% and the $5.00 minimum — were read from Settings → Billing defaults (`tenant_settings.billing_defaults.late_pct` / `late_min_amount`), with an optional per-plan override on `plan_penalty_rules` and every NULL falling back to the tenant. **The tenant fallback is GONE for these two terms.** `rate_plans.late_fee_charge_id` names a charge code; `charges.percent` and `charges.min_amount` are the terms; **NULL is not a fallback, it is $0.00**. ⚠ **THE ❄️ FROZEN FIGURE DOES NOT MOVE, AND THAT IS WHY THIS IS A ROUTING CHANGE RATHER THAN A PRICE CHANGE.** The seeded `LATE` code carries `percent 1.5, min_amount 5` — the SAME two figures as the screen (`apps/tenancy/fixtures/lookup_defaults.json` against `settings_families.BILLING_DEFAULTS`) — so `max($5.00, 1.5% × $542.10)` is **$8.13** either way, and `GT-8`/`GT-10`/`GT-11` are untouched. What DOES change is that a plan pointed at a DIFFERENT code produces a different fee, so **$8.13 stops being universal**; the PO ruled that knowingly (_"you capturd it correctly - please apply them - raise CR if required"_). ⚠ **THREE ANSWERS, NOT TWO — COLLAPSING THE FIRST TWO IS THE DANGER.** (a) **No plan to read** — the id is absent, malformed or names no row of this tenant (HR-1): a DATA FAULT that **REFUSES BY NAME**, never zero, because an account whose plan cannot be read could not have been priced and a quiet "no fee" would make it free for life. (b) **The plan exists and names no live code**, or names a RETIRED one (IS-1): **$0.00**, the ruled case, no fee line drawn — a `$0.00` line would tell the customer a fee was calculated. (c) **A live code**: its two terms, and its `id` is also where the fee BOOKS (`BR-BIL-075` unchanged). ⚠ **THE ORDER IS PART OF THE RULE, NOT AN IMPLEMENTATION DETAIL.** Switching the engine before the column was populated turns every late fee on every live tenant to $0.00, silently, on real bills. `rating/0026` adds the column, `rating/0027` fills it per tenant under `app.tenant_id`, and only then does anything read it. **Measured 2026-09-07 on PostgreSQL 16: 27 of 27 plans across 9 tenants pointed, 0 left unpointed.** ⚠ **AND THE ZERO IS LAWFUL BUT MUST NEVER BE INVISIBLE.** A new plan starts pointed at the tenant's seeded code (`services.default_late_fee_charge`) so tomorrow's plans do not silently stop charging; `selectors.plans_missing_late_fee_charge`, `manage.py cr078_late_fee_codes` and gate **G121** each COUNT a plan in the zero state, and `G121` also refuses a re-introduced read of `late_pct`/`late_min_amount` on the late-fee path. ⚠ **WHAT THIS RULE DOES NOT DECIDE:** whether `plan_penalty_rules.late_fee_pct` / `late_fee_min` still override the charge code. They are no longer read by the fee path and the PO was not asked — **`OQ-RAT-1766`, OPEN**. ~~The GRACE half (`grace_days`, `grace_mode`) is untouched and still falls back to the tenant (`BR-BIL-008`/`BR-BIL-002`).~~ **SUPERSEDED 2026-10-06 (PO ruling 11 (a) → `BR-BIL-008` as amended): the grace is set on the same late-fee charge code (`charges.grace_days` / `grace_mode`, `rating/0066`), a plan override stated before the ruling still applies, and Billing defaults is the starting value. Built 2.551.0, `DEF-RAT-9816`, ~~pending finance re-sign~~ **FINANCE RE-SIGNED 2026-10-06** (HUMAN-GUIDE §6; PO *"S1–S8 approve"*, S8, on *"Due 1 Nov, grace 5 days on LATE, $100.00 unpaid after 6 Nov → $5.00 on the next bill; grace 10 days on LATE → no fee until after 11 Nov."*; decision log *"S1–S8 SIGNED"*).** | PO ruling 2026-09-07 (decision log) · `CR-078` · finance re-sign `12-governance/09-human-review-ledger.md` · **amends `BR-BIL-001`** (the two terms' SOURCE only — the arithmetic, the $25.00 balance floor and the grace clock are unmoved) · `BR-BIL-075` (where it books — unchanged; its TC-4/TC-5 refusals SUPERSEDED to the ruled zero) · `BR-RAT-035` (amounts are seeds a tenant edits) · `IS-1` · `HR-1` · `OQ-RAT-1766` (OPEN) | **CONFIRMED (2026-09-07) · HUMAN-REVIEW-PENDING** (rating money path — row in `12-governance/09-human-review-ledger.md`) | `apps/rating/models.RatePlan.late_fee_charge` · `apps/rating/migrations/0026` + `0027` · `apps/rating/selectors.plan_late_fee_terms` / `plans_missing_late_fee_charge` · `apps/rating/services.default_late_fee_charge` · `apps/billing/late_fee.assess_late_fee_for_plan` · `apps/billing/decisions._late_fee_charge_id_for_plan` · `10-scripts/gates/g121_plans_name_their_late_fee_code.py` · `06-backend/tests/test_cr078_late_fee_from_the_plans_charge_code.py` |
| BR-RAT-963 | **A DISCOUNT NAMES ONE OR MORE UTILITY SERVICES, AND `Specific services` WITH NONE CHOSEN IS AN UNFINISHED FORM (PO ruling 2026-09-10, `CR-091-BRC01`).** PO verbatim, asked how a discount should take services: _"Add a new choice. - one or more services from drop down; All services or sepecific services; this is correct"_, answering _"when we create discount - we will ahve to take 1 or more utility services - currently it is not available. also we will ahve to dsplay in detail view and also allow to enit it duing edit process"_. **The scope gains a PAIR — `all_services` and `specific_services` — APPENDED to the five it had; nothing is renumbered and no stored row is reinterpreted.** ⚠ **`one_service` IS NOT WIDENED**, and that is why two values join rather than one changing: the enum is literally named for the singular, so widening it would leave a name that lies about its own contents, and every discount already saved keeps exactly the meaning it had. **`applies_to_services` is a JSON list of service codes validated against the tenant's ENABLED services** — the TAX's own column, its validation and its serialisation, copied rather than re-spelled (`CR-091` §2 names it as the precedent). ⚠ **AN EMPTY LIST IS REFUSED HERE AND MEANS *EVERYWHERE* ON A TAX, AND THE TWO MUST NOT BE HARMONISED.** A tax has no `All services` choice on screen, so `NULL or []` is the only way it can say *every service*; a discount HAS that choice, so an empty list stops being an expression of intent and becomes a form the clerk has not finished. **The general lesson the PO drew from it: an absence only carries meaning while there is no way to state the thing explicitly** — add the explicit choice and the same empty value must change from a synonym to a mistake, or the screen silently applies a discount to every service on an unfinished form. ⚠ **AND IT IS THE OPPOSITE WAY ROUND FROM THE PLAN'S OUTSIDE-CITY SURCHARGE, WHICH LOOKS LIKE THE SAME CONTROL** (`BR-RAT-102`, decision log 2026-09-10 §12): that dropdown opens with EVERY service picked and an empty selection is ALLOWED, because its column starts as *nothing spared* while this list starts as *nothing chosen*. **A lane that unified them would silently change what a blank form means on one of the two.** ⚠ **THE REFUSAL IS THE SERVER'S.** `_validate_version` GATE 3 refuses it for every caller and the wizard renders the field error against the chip control — `PRB-14` and `DEF-384`: a client that cannot express a state is not a server that refuses it. ⚠ **THE MONEY HALF, WORKED:** `_scoped_base`'s `line.service == rule.applies_to_service` becomes a MEMBERSHIP test. The check with teeth is `tests/test_def_rat_5814_every_scope_saves_pg.py`, which **walks `DISCOUNT_SCOPES` itself and reads the stored column back**, so an eighth scope cannot ship without its migration and a silent fall-back to `whole_bill` cannot pass for a save | `tests/test_cr091_discount_names_its_services.py` (8 cases, every one asserting MONEY) · `discount_pricing._scoped_base` / `_wanted_services` · `discount_services._validate_version` GATE 3 · `selectors.discount_detail` · `DiscountWizard.tsx` (the kit's `ChipMultiSelect`) |

The threshold setting stays but starts unset, so nothing ever requires a second approval unless a utility deliberately turns it on."_ ⚠ **THIS CLOSES `PRB-13` / `OQ-290` WITHOUT CHOOSING BETWEEN TWO SIGNED POLICIES.** `BR-RAT-077` measures the discount's own **budget cap** against a tenant setting; `BR-RAT-091` measures a **projected monthly subsidy** against `$500`. Exposure is bounded per customer three ways and once more in total, so a second signature was checking something already bounded; and for a utility run by 10–20 staff a mandatory second approver may not exist at all. ⚠ **ONE CLAUSE OF `BR-RAT-077` GOES WITH IT OR THE FEATURE IS NOT OFF.** That rule says *a discount with no budget cap ALWAYS requires the second approval*, and that clause reads no threshold — so with the bar unset it would go on firing, and an assistance programme with no cap (the one a small utility is most likely to write) would still demand an approver it has not got. **It is disabled with the rest, and its reasoning is preserved for the day a utility switches the threshold back on.** ⚠ **NOTHING IS DELETED.** `second_approval_required` and `second_approval_threshold` stay on `discount_versions`; the `discount_second_approval_threshold_amount` Param stays in `FINANCE_CONTROLS`, now nullable and unset; and the freeze-at-submit behaviour (§A7.6 — an approver sees the requirement the submitter saw) is untouched.
