| BR-RAT-1016 | ⚠ **(2026-10-03) every `OQ-324` clause in this row is HISTORY — it is CLOSED (B): a plan version has a window (`BR-RAT-1066`), so a plan correction restates the version's number AND its window.** **THE THREE VERBS ARE THE SAME FOR EVERY PRICED OBJECT: EDIT while it has priced nothing · CORRECT over the SAME window when the figure recorded was wrong · CHANGE over a NEW window when prices move. (PO ruling 2026-09-17, verbatim, shown a table of what each of the five objects does today: _"please fix all these"_.)** ⚠ **THIS GENERALISES `BR-RAT-104` RATHER THAN INVENTING ANYTHING.** That rule came from `CR-013` part 4 under the heading **"shared rules"** and was built for TAXES ONLY, and the word *shared* is the only thing in the repo that ever said it should reach further. **The finding the PO was shown: five priced objects in one module, five different policies.** A rate schedule REFUSED the save outright once live; a plan edited in place, auto-bumping its version and demanding a reason; a **charge code edited freely — no version, no reason, no lock**; a tax refused the rate-history edit and offered `correct_tax`; a **discount could not be edited at all, not even a draft nobody had submitted**. ⚠ **THE DISTINCTION THAT MAKES IT WORK IS THE WINDOW, and it is `correct_tax`'s own property: a correction COPIES the corrected row's window VERBATIM rather than taking it from the payload.** Without that a "correction" is a backdated change wearing a correction's clothes, and it silently rewrites which bills the old figure was ever right for. ⚠ **WHAT IS BUILT PER OBJECT, BECAUSE THE VERBS ARE UNIFORM AND THE STORAGE IS NOT.** **Charge code** — verb 1 and its boundary: a code nobody uses edits freely; a code IN USE may not move `amount_rule`, `amount`, `percent`, `min_amount`, `max_amount` or the per-size table (`BR-RAT-690`, which IS the flat rule's amount stated once per size) without a written reason, and the move writes a `rate_change_log` row with before, after, reason and actor plus a `charge.repriced` audit entry. *In use* is three routes — a plan that NAMES it, a schedule that reads it as its service's base (`BR-RAT-088`, which has no join row at all), an issued invoice line — and **`invoice_lines is None` LOCKS**, `DEF-079`'s reading kept verbatim. **Worked:** `PAPER` at `$2.00` moved to `$5.00` is `$3.00` more on every bill carrying it, every cycle, for ever, and before this the only trace was an `audit_log` row that is pruned on a retention schedule. ⚠ **VERBS 2 AND 3 ARE NOT BUILT FOR A CHARGE CODE AND CANNOT BE**: `charges` has no `effective_from`, no `effective_to` and no version table, so both verbs — which turn entirely on a window — have nowhere to live. That is **`OQ-RAT-12408`**, named in `update_charge`'s own response so an operator is not left believing a price was scheduled. **Tax** — already complete (`BR-RAT-104`), untouched. **Plan** — `update_plan` keeps verb 3 exactly as `BR-RAT-037` rules it and gains verb 2, **BUILT 2026-09-18 in `c58b4e7ec` (`2.305.0`) once `OQ-RAT-12409` answered where a correction is STORED** (option A: `plan_versions` gains the same four columns `taxes`, `tariff_versions` and `discount_versions` carry, and its unique key becomes partial on `corrects IS NULL`). ⚠ **AND THE PLAN IS THE ONE OBJECT WHERE THE PROPERTY HELD FIXED IS NOT THE WINDOW.** A plan version has no window at all — `OQ-324` records that omission as deliberate — so the invariant that makes this a correction rather than a version bump is the **COUNTER**: `version_no` is copied from the row being corrected, `rate_plans.version` does not move, and a payload naming a version number is refused BY NAME. It matters here more than anywhere else because `unit_plan_assignments.plan_version` snapshots that counter at assignment (`BR-CUS-013`) and those rows outlive the plan — 75 of them on `SCW-RES` alone, every one at `plan_version = 1`. **Discount** — a draft version that has never been SUBMITTED edits in place; a live version is corrected over the same window. ⚠ **THE SUBMIT/APPROVE/ACTIVATE WORKFLOW IS UNTOUCHED** — `BR-RAT-077` and `BR-RAT-109` govern it and both are ratified; this adds an edit path and a correction path, and changes nothing about how a discount goes live. ⚠ **AND NOTHING ALREADY ISSUED IS TOUCHED, ON ANY OF THE FIVE** — CLAUDE.md hard rule 4. A correction changes what prices FUTURE bills; whether it must also restate bills already issued is `OQ-310`, open since 2026-08-21 and unchanged by this rule. ⚠ **THE BEHAVIOUR FLAGS ARE DELIBERATELY OUTSIDE IT** — `taxable_default`, `auto_apply`, `waivable`, `separate_line`, the code, the name and the GL pointer change how a line behaves or what it is called, not what it costs. The tax lane drew the same boundary at `TAX_RATE_HISTORY_FIELDS` and recorded why: a rule that demanded a filing record to tick a checkbox is the over-application that gets a rule quietly disabled by the next lane. | PO ruling 2026-09-17 (decision log) · `CR-013` part 4 **shared rules** · `BR-RAT-104` (the shape this generalises) · `BR-RAT-037` (the version discipline) · `BR-RAT-088` (the charge code owns the base) · `BR-RAT-690` (the per-size table) · `BR-RAT-035` / `DEF-079` (the audit lock and its fail-closed reading) · `BR-RAT-077` · `BR-RAT-109` · CLAUDE.md hard rule 4 · `DEF-RAT-5712` · `DEF-RAT-5709` · `DEF-RAT-5710` · `OQ-RAT-12408` (open) · `OQ-310` (open) | **CONFIRMED (2026-09-17)** | `services.CHARGE_MONEY_FIELDS` · `services.charge_is_in_use` · `services.charge_money_moves` · `services.update_charge` · `selectors.charge_row.used_by_schedules` · `ChargeCodeWizard`'s `Why this changed` box · `tests/test_br_rat_1016_charge_code_reprice.py` · `chargeRepriceReason.dom.test.tsx` · **the plan's verb 2:** `services.correct_plan_version` · `api.plan_corrections` (`POST /plans/{id}/corrections`) · `rating/0036` · `selectors.plan_versions` (the correction chain, and the second sort key `version_no` alone no longer provides) · `PlanWizard`'s `PlanEditIntentPicker` · `PlanDetail`'s `planVersionState` / `planVersionKey` · `tests/test_oq_rat_12409_plan_corrections.py` · `PlanDetail.test.tsx` |
| BR-RAT-1017 | **A SEASON OWNS ITS TIME-OF-USE WINDOWS, AND THE TWO ARE DRAWN AS ONE PANEL. A SEASON BOUNDARY IS A MONTH AND A DAY WITH NO YEAR, SO IT IS TWO `SelectField`s AND NOT A `DatePickerField` — A NAMED, NARROW DIVERGENCE FROM `BR-SHL-033`. (PO ruling 2026-09-18, closing `OQ-RAT-12410`, verbatim: _"this is good - Wave 4 — the seasonal / time-of-use editor"_, _"please design it - as per ui rules we have - fix these alos"_ and _"so first select the season and within season select time of use within a given day"_.)** ⚠ **THE MODEL WAS ALREADY RIGHT; ONLY THE DRAWING WAS WRONG.** A TOU window is per (season × day type), so a time-of-use schedule genuinely HAS seasons — but `TouEditor` rendered a `SeasonEditor` BESIDE itself, so a clerk who picked *Time-of-use* was shown a *Seasons* panel too and could not tell whether they had chosen wrongly. **Ruled: ONE panel, *Seasons and their windows*, and a window is added from INSIDE the season that owns it.** ⚠ **THE PER-WINDOW SEASON DROPDOWN IS REMOVED, AND THAT IS THE POINT RATHER THAN A TIDY-UP: it makes *forgot to pick a season* UNREACHABLE rather than merely refused** — the move `BR-RAT-104`'s sibling `DEF-RAT-5717` made for tiers, and the blueprint's own complaint on p047 that *"all three were server refusals discovered on the last step"*. Removing a season removes the windows inside it, because that is what nesting means. ⚠ **THE DRAFT CARRIES A MINTED `season_key`, NEVER THE LABEL, AND THE SERVER CONTRACT DID NOT MOVE.** A label is the one field a clerk retypes: renaming *Summer* to *Peak* used to turn every window pointing at it into `rating.tou.unknown_season` at activation, and two UNNAMED seasons were worse still — both labels `""`, so the two collections were indistinguishable. `structurePayload` resolves the key back to the label `_validate_windows` looks up, through one bridge so the ends cannot disagree. ⚠ **THE `BR-SHL-033` DIVERGENCE IS THE HALF THAT NEEDED A RULING AND IT IS NARROW.** That rule makes every date field a `DatePickerField`, whose value is a `YYYY-MM-DD` CALENDAR DATE — read off the control, not assumed. A season boundary RECURS, so it has no year and the control cannot express it. It was put to the PO with the alternative offered explicitly rather than decided by a lane. **Every other date in this wizard — effective from, effective to, the filing dates — is still a `DatePickerField`, and the dropdown half of `BR-SHL-033` is obeyed rather than diverged from:** the size picker measures `LONG_LIST_THRESHOLD` against the list actually offered and becomes a `SearchSelectField` past it. ⚠ **MONTH NAMES COME FROM `Intl`, NEVER FROM A CATALOGUE** (`DateCell.monthLabel`): B14 rule 35 says a catalogue is written in its own language, and twelve hand-written month names in eleven files would breach it the day a twelfth language lands. The day picker offers 28 days in February because the coverage walk uses the 2001 reference year `_season_intervals` uses — 2001 because it is NOT a leap year, so `date(2001, 2, 29)` raises and 29 February never participates. | PO ruling 2026-09-18 (decision log, *`OQ-RAT-12410` CLOSED*) · `OQ-RAT-12410` · `BR-SHL-033` (the rule diverged from, in one clause only) · `BR-RAT-033` (TOU coverage) · `BR-RAT-067` (a window names its register) · `B14` rule 35 (a catalogue in its own language) · `B14` rule 38 (our UI rules build a form) · `DEF-RAT-5732`→`5736`, `DEF-RAT-5733`, `DEF-RAT-5734`, `DEF-RAT-5735` | **CONFIRMED (2026-09-18)** | `SeasonsAndWindows` · `nextSeasonKey` · `seasonKeyForLabel` / `seasonLabelForKey` · `seasonCoverageIssue` · `seasonRangeEcho` · `daysInSeasonMonth` · `DateCell.monthLabel` · `07-frontend/src/features/rating/seasonsAndWindows.dom.test.tsx` (29 cases) |
| BR-RAT-1025 | ⚠⚠ **AMENDED 2026-10-09 (PO ruling, decision log *"PO RULING — A SERVICE MAY HOLD TWO OR MORE BASE CODES FOR THE SAME CUSTOMER CLASS; THE "ALREADY COVERS" REFUSAL GOES…"*; `DEF-RAT-10026`) — A SERVICE MAY HOLD TWO OR MORE LIVE BASE CODES WHATEVER THEIR CLASSES; EACH RATE SCHEDULE NAMES THE ONE IT BILLS.** Put to the PO as a reversal of their own words of 2026-10-06 — *"user will not hvae same base charge for same customer class"* — with BASE-E (Residential) and BASE-E-OUT (Residential, suburban) refused at City of Queen West; PO, verbatim: *"yes please do it"*. **SUPERSEDED, that one sentence only:** the 2026-10-06 amendment's *"The overlap guard (`services.base_codes_clash`, message `rating.charges.base_already_covered`) now refuses only two live base codes of one service that name a SAME class, or two every-class codes … The clash is warned on step 1 of the charge-code wizard … (`DEF-RAT-9799`, `GET /charges/base-clash`); the help line reads *"One per customer class."*"*. **Built 2.562.1:** the save-time refusal, `base_codes_clash`/`base_code_clash`, the step-1 warning and `GET /charges/base-clash` are removed (catalogue keys `rating.charges.base_already_covered` and `rating.chargeCode.baseClash` retired); code STRINGS stay unique; the help line reads *"Each rate schedule picks one."*; the rate schedule's base-code picker shows *code · name* with the classes each covers. **Billing was already safe and did not move:** `selectors.base_charge_from_charge_code` narrows to the schedule's named code, and a schedule naming none bills `$0.00` with *Choose a base code* (`DEF-RAT-9832`) — so with W-RES `$8.00` and W-RES-OUT `$11.50` both Residential, the schedule naming W-RES bills `$8.00`, the one naming W-RES-OUT bills `$11.50`, and one naming none bills `$0.00` + the warning, never *"2 base charge codes"* (`tests/test_def_rat_10026_two_base_codes_one_class.py`, PostgreSQL 16). Everything else in this row stands: one base code per schedule, the plan gets it through the schedule, B (a) splits by days. 🔒 HUMAN-REVIEW-PENDING (review ledger row 2026-10-09). ⚠⚠ **AMENDED AGAIN 2026-10-06 (PO ruling, decision log *"PO RULING — A RATE SCHEDULE THAT NAMES NO BASE CODE BILLS A `$0.00` BASE…"*; `DEF-RAT-9832`, closes `DEF-RAT-9829`) — NO POOL: A SCHEDULE NAMING NO BASE CODE BILLS `$0.00` WITH THE WARNING, FOR EVERY CLASS.** PO verbatim: *"You will take the code from plan -> rate schedule -> base charges - in that case if it is provided then you will take it if it is not provided then you will take it zeo ; but you cannot take W-COM because it is availabel in charge coede - I have provided this before also- you have asked again"* — restating 2026-09-30. Base = plan → rate schedule → the code the schedule NAMES → its price; **nothing is ever taken from the charge-code list by customer class.** Every sentence in this row or its citations saying *"where it names none, the code that declares itself the base"* and S7's *"Schedules already live that name none keep billing as today"* are SUPERSEDED. Worked: W-ALL `$8.00` + W-COM `$15.00`, schedule naming none → residential `$8.00`→`$0.00`, commercial refused→`$0.00`, both warned; schedule naming W-COM → `$15.00`. Built `selectors.base_charge_from_charge_code` (`_no_base_code_named`), `resolution.resolve_base_charge` (arm 2 retired), `plan_reads._base_block` (`not_named`), `schedule_reads`, the base seam. ⚠⚠ **AMENDED 2026-10-06 (PO ruling, decision log *"PO RULINGS — BASE CHARGES…"*; `DEF-RAT-9797`) — A RATE SCHEDULE NAMES EXACTLY ONE BASE CODE.** PO verbatim: *"Here we do base charges - rate schedule -> plan - so base charges will be atatched to rate schedule"* and *"B. … (a) From a date you choose … A bill straddling the date splits by days: WP-1 up to the date, WP-2 after."* Built: when a schedule version is activated (given a start date) naming no base code, the candidates are the service's live base codes whose class scope admits the schedule's class — exactly ONE is written onto the version (nothing to choose: it is the only code the bill could read; water with only W-ALL $8.00 names W-ALL, $8.00 either way), and TWO OR MORE are REFUSED, *"Choose a base charge code."* (`rating.schedules.base_charge_code_required`; W-ALL $8.00 beside W-COM $15.00 on an every-class schedule); a draft is not asked. **What is deliberately NOT done:** no stored schedule is changed and no code is chosen for it — a live schedule that names none keeps billing exactly as before (the pooled, class-narrowed code) and is LISTED for a System Admin (`GET /rate-schedules/base-to-choose`, a notice and a per-row *Choose a base code* on Rate schedules); the plan screen shows *Choose a base code* where it showed *Several base codes*. Retiring the pooled fallback outright would refuse or zero accounts on live schedules today (the seeded utility bills BASE-W residential $8.00 and commercial $15.00 by the account's class from ONE every-class schedule), so it stays for un-named schedules only; the seeders type `services.seeding_schedules_without_a_named_base()` to keep producing that shape. B (a) is the existing version mechanism: proved by `tests/test_def_rat_9797_wp1_to_wp2_splits_by_days.py` (WP-1 $30.00 to June 15, WP-2 $60.00 from June 16 → $15.00 + $30.00). 🔒 HUMAN-REVIEW-PENDING. ⚠⚠ **AMENDED 2026-09-30 BY `BR-RAT-088`'S AMENDMENT OF THE SAME DAY — A MISSING BASE NO LONGER REFUSES.** PO verbatim: *"if user did not define and bind base charges in rate schedule - then as a default you take 0 and complete the bill instead of refusing - if you want to show the warning you can display it dueing the process"*. Arm 2 answering *no code at all*, and a named code with no figure for this account's customer type, are cases (a) and (c) of `BR-RAT-088`'s amendment: **`$0.00` base + a warning, the bill completes.** The ORDER of the two arms, the route (*plan → rate schedule → charge code → base charges*) and the ambiguity refusal (two base codes, `DEF-468`) are unchanged. **NOT BUILT — PO: *"don't fix anything yet"*.** **THE RATE SCHEDULE NAMES THE CHARGE CODE THAT OWNS ITS SERVICE'S BASE. NAMED FIRST, DECLARED SECOND, AND A SCHEDULE THAT NAMES NOTHING BEHAVES EXACTLY AS IT DOES TODAY. (PO ruling 2026-09-19, `CR-120-BRC06`.)** PO verbatim, correcting the route after it was put back to them as *plan → charge code → base charges*: _"no this is not correct ; So: plan → rate schedule -> charge code → base charges - this is correct"_. And, asked whether this reverses `BR-RAT-088`, verbatim: _"we are not bindng base charges in rate schedule - you were taking it default; now we are making it explicit it is a same rule - change is default -> expliciate"_. And on the minimum, verbatim: _"base will come from base - min we can give in rate schedule - if it is not there then you ahve to add it at step 2 - deulta will be zero"_. ⚠ **IT REVERSES NOTHING AND IT CORRECTS ONE SENTENCE.** `BR-RAT-088` is CONFIRMED-AS-AMENDED: the CHARGE CODE still owns the base AMOUNT, and the schedule NAMES a code rather than holding a figure — *"base will come from base"*. What moves is the ROUTE, and the step it gains was always there. The PO had already said so on 2026-09-10 (_"Charge Code -> base charge -> rate schedule -> plans - that is the path"_), and the decision log of that day reads *"THE MIDDLE STEP IS THE RATE SCHEDULE, AND IT IS A ROUTE RATHER THAN A STORE"*. The sentence now SUPERSEDED is the PO's own of 2026-08-31, _"plan -> charges code -> base scharges"_, which is quoted verbatim inside `resolution.resolve_base_charge` and inside `selectors.base_charge_from_charge_code`; both are amended IN PLACE with the supersession stated rather than deleted, because a reader tracing that quotation needs the middle link. ⚠ **`tariff_versions.base_charge_code` IS A POINTER AND `tariff_versions.base_charge` IS A RETIRED FIGURE, AND THEY SIT SIDE BY SIDE IN ONE TABLE.** The retired decimal stays retired, stays NULL on every schedule this product can create, is read at exactly one place (`_legacy_schedule_base`, R4's fallback for a tenant provisioned before `BR-RAT-088`) and is **not repurposed by this rule**. A lane that reaches for one when it means the other has put a figure back on an object the ruling took it off. ⚠ **TWO ARMS, AND THE ORDER IS THE RULE.** (1) The schedule NAMES a code → that code is the base, with no `is_base` narrowing, because a person said. (2) It names none → today's lookup byte for byte, the code whose `is_base` is set for the service, refusing on ambiguity (`DEF-468`). **Every version that exists carries NULL**, so arm 2 answers everywhere and no bill moves. **The shape is `BR-RAT-1011`/`CR-078`'s, deliberately** — *"Two places a plan can name it, and the order matters"* — one pattern for two bindings rather than two that happen to agree. ⚠ **NAMING ONE OF TWO BASE CODES CURES `DEF-468` RATHER THAN REPEATING IT.** Two codes declaring themselves a service's base is billing by catalogue order, which the chain refuses; naming one is the answer to that, and a utility with a residential and a commercial base is the case. ⚠ **A NAMED CODE THAT DOES NOT REACH THE ACCOUNT REFUSES; IT NEVER FALLS BACK.** ⚠ **AMENDED 2026-09-30 AT *REFUSES* — it bills `$0.00` with a warning (`BR-RAT-088` case (c)); it still NEVER falls back to another code or a retired column.** Falling through would bill a figure the schedule's own review page does not show — `BR-RAT-088`'s failure shape (§A3, **$23,069.50 a month across 1,247 connections**) in the other direction, with neither row visibly wrong. ⚠ **THE COHERENCE GATE IS AT ACTIVATION, NOT AT SAVE AND NOT AT BILLING TIME** (`DEF-2923`'s reasoning one column across). `_guard_bound_base_charge_code` refuses a binding whose code is gone, is not ticked `is_base`, prices a different service, or is scoped to customer classes this schedule does not price. All four facts live on the CHARGE CODE and are mutable after the binding is written, so a database constraint would refuse an ordinary edit on a different screen; a draft is half-built by `BR-RAT-031`'s design, so refusing at save stops somebody setting a service up in the order that suits them. ⚠ **THE MINIMUM BILL IS THE SCHEDULE'S AND AN ABSENT MINIMUM IS ZERO — BUT IT IS STORED AS AN ABSENCE, AND THAT JUDGMENT IS THE LANE'S.** A minimum tops a bill UP to itself, so a floor of `0.00` and no floor produce the IDENTICAL bill, which is why the default can be zero without a value being written. It is the exact opposite of the BASE one column across, where `0` means *free* and NULL means *not served* (`CR-013` part 1 / `DEF-277`) and the two readings are `$0.00` and a refused bill. A pre-filled `0.00` would print `0.00` down r10's electric matrix, which draws an em-dash on every size — `RateScheduleDetail` renders `minimumBillDisplay ?? "—"`, measured. **Recorded rather than asked (hard-won law 13), and put in front of the PO as one line of the review-ledger row so they can overturn it in a sentence.** ⚠ **THE LIVE BILLING PATH DOES NOT YET RECEIVE THE BINDING — `DEF-RAT-5782`, FILED RATHER THAN MENTIONED.** `selectors.base_charge_from_charge_code` takes `named_code` and honours it; `billing.gather._base_charge_code_for` passes none, because those four lines are another module's file. **It cannot bill a different figure — it REFUSES**, and only on the two-base-code catalogue that already refuses today. | PO ruling 2026-09-19 (decision log) · `CR-120-BRC06` · **amends `BR-RAT-088`** (the ROUTE only — the source, the arithmetic and the retired column are unmoved) · `BR-RAT-1011` / `CR-078` (the named-then-inferred precedent) · `BR-RAT-1010` (the charge code states the sizes) · `DEF-468` (the ambiguity refusal) · `DEF-2923` (where a coherence refusal belongs) · `DEF-277` / `CR-013` part 1 (absent is not zero, and why the MINIMUM is the exception) · `DEF-RAT-5782` (open) · CLAUDE.md hard rule 2 | **CONFIRMED (2026-09-30) · the `$0.00`-with-a-warning amendment is NOT BUILT** — earlier: **CONFIRMED (2026-09-19) · HUMAN-REVIEW-PENDING** (rating money path — row in `12-governance/09-human-review-ledger.md`) | `apps/rating/models.TariffVersion.base_charge_code` · `apps/rating/migrations/0037_cr120_schedule_base_charge_code.py` · `apps/rating/services._validate_base_charge_code` / `_guard_bound_base_charge_code` / `_code_admits_class` · `apps/rating/seams.account_type_code_by_id` · `apps/rating/resolution.resolve_base_charge` (the `named_code` arm) · `apps/rating/resolution.ServiceResolution.base_source == "named_charge_code"` · `apps/rating/resolution_catalogue.schedule_version_facts` · `apps/rating/selectors.version_row` / `_base_charge_code_ref` / `base_charge_from_charge_code` · `RateScheduleWizard.BasicsStep`'s picker and `ReviewComparison` · `features/rating/baseCharge.boundBaseChargeFor` / `baseChargeIsNamed` · `tests/test_cr120_schedule_names_base_charge_code.py` · `10-scripts/cr120_base_route_measure.py` |

| **BR-RAT-1026** | **A CUSTOMER-CLASS ID A PLAN HOLDS MUST RESOLVE, AND AN ID THAT DOES NOT IS DROPPED RATHER THAN KEPT OR GUESSED.** `rate_plans.account_type_ids` is a JSON list with **no referential integrity at all**, by design (a deferred FK, B11 F-3), so the database cannot refuse an id whose row has gone. **It goes.** Any entry that does not resolve against `account_types` **for that plan's own tenant** is removed; the entries that do resolve are kept, in order; a plan left with nothing keeps `[]`. ⚠ **AND `[]` MEANS *EVERY CLASS* (`DEF-RAT-5768`), WHICH IS ALSO WRONG WHEN THE PLAN NAMED ONE — SO DROPPING IS NOT THE END OF THE REPAIR.** It is chosen because the alternative is worse in three ways at once: a dangling id is invented data, it renders as a raw 36-character UUID on screen against **B14 rule 31** (*"no Databse ID on screen - that is a rule"*), and every consumer already reads it as *names none* (`PlanWizard.addableCharges` drops it and `classCodes.size === 0` turns the narrowing off), so it switches the class narrowing off **invisibly** while `[]` does it visibly. **A plan whose class was dropped goes back to a human to re-state by name.** ⚠ **AND IT IS NOT RESOLVED BY CODE OR BY NAME.** `plan_versions.snapshot` carries only `account_type_id` and `account_subtype_id` — checked key by key across every snapshot on the beta, 2026-09-19 — so there is no code in it to match on, and the PLAN CODE is not evidence: `SCW-RES` reading like *residential* is the inference **`BR-MET-151` clause 3** forbids in its own domain (*"Whitfield Rentals LLC is not proof of a landlord"*). ⚠ **THE GENERAL RULE THIS IS AN INSTANCE OF, AND IT IS THE PART WORTH CITING: A SNAPSHOT PRESERVES A VALUE, NOT THE WORLD THE VALUE REFERRED TO.** `BR-RAT-111` keeps `plan_versions.snapshot` append-only and it did its job perfectly here; the rows it pointed at were deleted by `customers/0054` two days earlier, which listed three of the five columns in this schema holding an `account_type_id` and missed the two that have **no foreign key**. Any snapshot holding a foreign id has this hole and nothing in this repo checks for it. | `DEF-RAT-5823` · `DEF-RAT-5822` · decision log 2026-09-19 (*"THREE PLANS POINT AT A CUSTOMER CLASS THAT WAS DELETED BY ANOTHER MODULE"*) · supersedes the register row's own remedy, *"fall back to matching the account type by CODE"*, which cannot be done — there is no code to match on | **CONFIRMED (2026-09-19)** — implemented by `rating/0041`; the three plans' own classes are `OQ-RAT-12473`, OPEN | `06-backend/tests/test_def_rat_5823_unresolvable_classes_pg.py` (PostgreSQL-only, 9 cases) |

| **BR-RAT-1030** | **A SCHEDULE PRICED AS A PERCENTAGE OF ANOTHER SERVICE STATES *A PERCENTAGE OF WHAT* — ITS METERED QUANTITY OR ITS USAGE-CHARGE DOLLARS — AND THE ANSWER IS STORED, NEVER INFERRED. (PO ruling 2026-09-22, `CR-130-BRC07`.)** PO verbatim: _"we also need to create 2 option 5age of charges or % of usage - when we say charges - in that case if water charge is $100, and %age is 80% then sewer cahrge will be $80; if % of usage then if Water usage will be 100 gallan %age is 80% then sewer uage will be 80 gallan = sewer charge will be 80 X { flat rate or tiered rate} - this is how it should be done"_. **THE COLUMN IS `tariff_versions.percent_basis`, `'usage'` \| `'charge'`.** On `usage` the percentage is taken of the basis service's billed QUANTITY and the result is priced on THIS schedule's own rate — one unbounded block (flat) or a LADDER, walked by the `tiered` plugin so `BR-RAT-026`/`BR-RAT-032` exist once in the product. On `charge` it is taken of the basis service's usage-charge DOLLARS, being the Σ of that service's FINALIZED usage lines — base charge, tax and fees excluded by line KIND, which is `BR-RAT-015`'s own three exclusions restored with the arithmetic they belonged to. ⚠ **THIS SUPERSEDES NOTHING. `BR-RAT-015` ❄️ SUPERSEDED 2026-09-08 BY `CR-081` STANDS, AND STAYS THE DEFAULT.** The chain is `BR-RAT-015` CONFIRMED 2026-08-16 (of the MONEY) → ❄️ SUPERSEDED 2026-09-08 (of the VOLUME, six frozen figures re-signed under HUMAN-GUIDE §6) → `BR-RAT-1030` 2026-09-22 (BOTH, chosen per schedule). ❄️ `GT-17` **$36.00**, `GT-20` **$54.00**, `GT-25` **$27.00** and `GT-26` **$27.00** are all on the `usage` basis and NONE of them moves; all four are now asserted against the ENGINE in `tests/test_cr130_percent_basis.py` and not only against the document harness, which imports no application code and cannot see engine drift. ⚠ **EVERY ROW THAT PREDATES THE COLUMN IS WRITTEN `'usage'` BY NAME** (`rating/0043_cr130_percent_basis.py`: nullable add → explicit per-tenant backfill under `app.tenant_id` → `SET NOT NULL`, which is the assertion that the backfill reached every row). **On GT-17's own bill the other basis would have billed `$35.55`** — 90% of `$39.50` of water usage — which is 45 cents a month on a document that foots against itself either way, and is the whole reason the backfill is explicit. ⚠ **AN ABSENT OR UNKNOWN BASIS IS A REFUSAL AT EVERY LAYER AND NEVER A DEFAULT.** `services._validate_structure` requires it on a new or edited schedule; `selectors.pricing_inputs` publishes the stored value on every version; `billing.gather` and `billing.rating_seam` carry it; the plugin REFUSES a `None` by name, because a `None` means a hop dropped it and the two answers differ by real money. ⚠ **`unit_price` / `tiers` ARE REQUIRED ON `usage` AND REFUSED BY NAME ON `charge`, never ignored** — a percentage of money has no price per gallon, and a silently dropped rate is a number the tenant states, the screen shows and the engine discards. `_missing_structure` answers `None` for a complete charge-basis version rather than demanding a rate it would refuse. ⚠ **BOTH SEAM REFUSALS STAND, ONE PER BASIS, AND NEITHER EVER ANSWERS `$0.00`**: an unavailable quantity on `usage`, an unavailable amount on `charge`. A metered ZERO is a different fact and IS a real basis — a customer who used no water owes no sewer. ⚠ **WHICH DOLLARS IS `OQ-232`, WHICH IS OPEN — NON-BLOCKING, AND WAS LIVE THROUGHOUT.** The PRINTED (2dp) reading ships, which is the disposition the register itself records with its reasoning; the PO's `$100.00 → $80.00` is exact at both readings and does not distinguish them. `percent_of_service.py` called that question CLOSED BY SUPERSESSION from 2026-09-08 while the register's status column read OPEN — `DEF-RAT-6142`, filed and closed here. ⚠ **THE BASIS LIST IS DERIVED AND NAMES NO SERVICE** (PO, same message: _"dont hard code anything - use logic to derive it so that it is flexible and handle any scenario"_): on `charge`, every service the tenant has enabled except this one; on `usage`, those of them the utility declares it METERS, read from `tenant_services.billing_basis` through `seams.service_bills_from_a_register` (`BR-TEN-1180`). *Electric as a percentage of gas* is not forbidden — it is simply never configured. `selectors.percent_basis_options` is the one derivation and `GET /rate-schedules/percent-basis-options` serves it, so the front end does not build a second one. ⚠ **NO `rate_shape` FLAG AND NO SECOND COLUMN FOR THE RATE** — `BR-RAT-032` already says one price is ONE 0→∞ `rate_tiers` row, so flat versus tiered is how many blocks there are and the PO's _"{ flat rate or tiered rate }"_ is one representation with one block or with several. ⚠ **`selectors.pricing_inputs` READ `tiers[0].unit_price` AND DISCARDED THE REST UNTIL THIS DATE.** Correct while nothing could create a second block; a silent wrong-money bug the moment the ladder exists — on the PO's own ladder, **$80.00 where the answer is $150.00**, with no error anywhere. Fixed in the same change that opens the door, which is the only order in which that fix is worth anything. ⚠ **TAXABILITY IS NOT ON THIS STRUCTURE** and the reasoning is in `CR-130-BRC07` §5 and the decision log of 2026-09-22: it already exists on the TAX (`taxes.applies_to_services`), and putting it on the schedule would move ❄️ B4.2, which is a re-sign and never an inline edit. ⚠ **`BR-RAT-1027`–`BR-RAT-1029` ARE DELIBERATELY UNUSED** — this rule was numbered 1030 before the gap was noticed and renumbering it would have moved every citation in the plugin, the migration, the selector, the validator and the tests at once, which is the mass shared-file rewrite law 11 exists to prevent. |
| BR-RAT-1040 | **THE PLAN LIST SHOWS THE *REAL* MONTHLY BASE TOTAL, AND SORTING BY IT IS WITHDRAWN. (CONFIRMED 2026-09-27 by PO ruling, option (a) of two, answered _"a is good"_ — closing `DEF-RAT-6927`.)** ⚠ **THIS ROW IS THE RULING REACHING RATING'S OWN REGISTER.** It landed in `12-governance/02-decision-log.md` on 2026-09-27 and in no module's business rules at all; a ruling is always in both, and a lane reading only this file would have met it as a review comment. **THE PROBLEM, AS SHOWN TO THE PO:** `Monthly base total` read **`$0.00` on every plan**, because it summed `plan_services.base_charge` — the column `BR-RAT-088` (PO 2026-08-20, *"the CHARGE CODE owns the base amount; a plan displays it, never defines it"*) retired and `DEF-422` removed the control for. The column is still WRITTEN, on purpose, so nothing was null and nothing raised: the total was arithmetically correct over its own input and the input was wrong. **THE WORKED CASE:** *"A clerk opens Plans & Rates, sees ten plans all reading `$0.00`, and clicks the column to sort by base charge. Today it sorts by a retired column — so the order is meaningless too, it just looks plausible."* **THE RULE, IN TWO PARTS. (1) THE FIGURE IS RESOLVED FROM THE CHARGE CODES**, down `BR-RAT-1025`'s ruled chain — plan → rate schedule → charge code → base charges — through `selectors.base_charge_from_charge_code`, the LIVE path, narrowed by the plan's customer class and the schedule's named-code binding. `selectors._plan_base_total` is it. **Worked, on the repo's own signed figures** (`core.sample_fixture`, `[W] §1 "Base/mo … Water $8 · Electric $12"`): a plan carrying electric and water resolves to **`$20.00`**, where the retired column on that same fixture said `$30.50` and on a live tenant says `$0.00`. **(2) `base_charges` IS SORTABLE NO LONGER.** `PLAN_SORTS` is `("name", "accounts", "status")` on the server and in `endpoints.ts`, and the column carries no `sortable` flag. A client still asking for the withdrawn sort falls back to `name` rather than erroring — a bookmarked URL should show the list — and never to the retired column. ⚠ **`NFR-3` IS NOT WEAKENED AND NOBODY MAY READ IT THAT WAY.** The Plans list still PAGES and SORTS in SQL; ONE sort OPTION is withdrawn because the value behind it cannot be computed there. Nobody may read this as licence to sort a list in Python — `tests/test_br_rat_1040_plan_base_total.py` TC-4 asserts the remaining `ORDER BY` for exactly that reason. **Why it is safe, in the PO's terms:** a plan list is ten or twenty rows at a utility of this size, so sorting by base charge is a convenience; a wrong number on a pricing screen is not. ⚠ **AN ABSENCE IS `null` AND A NAMED REASON, NEVER `$0.00`** — `no_rate_schedule`, `no_base_charge_code`, `base_charge_refused`, `customer_class_not_resolvable`, `differs_by_customer_class`. `$0.00` is a claim about the utility's pricing; `null` is a statement that the product cannot tell them. `impact.summarise` already takes this posture, in its own words: *"never `0.00`, which reads as 'no effect' to every person and every screen that sees it"*. **The reason is for the CODE and the register only** — B14 rule 33 puts rationale at zero words on screen, and every reader of this key already renders `null` as a dash. ⚠ **A PLAN NAMING SEVERAL CLASSES IS RESOLVED AT EACH AND MUST AGREE.** `rate_plans.account_type_ids` is a LIST since `DEF-RAT-5768` and `[]` means EVERY class; where the classes disagree there is no single monthly base and picking one would be `DEF-468`'s shape, billing by list ORDER. Ruling: `12-governance/02-decision-log.md`, 2026-09-27. Built `bea9b25e4`, `v2.381.1`. |
| BR-RAT-1044 | **A CORRECTED RATE SCHEDULE VERSION RECORDS THE DAY IT STOPPED BEING USED. (CONFIRMED 2026-09-29 by PO ruling, option (a) of three, verbatim: _"i will take this and use superseded_by in filter - so that it is always unique and even if we see in audit it is clear"_.)** ⚠ **THIS NARROWS `BR-RAT-104` / `BR-RAT-1016` VERB 2 BY EXACTLY ONE COLUMN AND NOTHING ELSE.** A correction still restates the SAME window — that single property is what makes it a correction rather than a rate update — and the corrected row still keeps its rate, its start date, its filing detail and its audit trail for ever. **THE PROBLEM, AS SHOWN TO THE PO:** the corrected row carried no end date at all, so the schedule Versions & filings table printed `02/01/2026 →` — this screen's way of writing *still running* — on a row that had not priced one bill since it was corrected. **THE RULE.** When `correct_rate_schedule_version` supersedes a version: `old.effective_to = max(the correction date, old.effective_from)` · `old.superseded_by = the new version` · `new.effective_from` = the original's, unchanged · `new.effective_to` = open. **THE TWO WORKED CASES, BOTH TESTED:** a version priced from 2026-02-01 and corrected on **2026-03-01** ends **2026-03-01** — *in use a month, then replaced*; the same version corrected on **2026-01-15** ends **2026-02-01**, the `max` — *replaced the day it would have started*. ⚠ **THE `max` IS NOT THE "CORRECTED BEFORE ACTIVATION" CASE.** A DRAFT has no dates and is edited in place; the service refuses a correction of one by name (`rating.schedules.correct_draft`) because no version exists and there is nothing to supersede. The edge case is a version **ACTIVATED WITH A FUTURE DATE** and corrected before that date arrives: without the `max` its end lands 17 days before its start, which is refused by name as `window_inverted`, and **the whole correction fails — the clerk loses the only way they have of fixing a typo.** ⚠ **IT MOVES NO PRICING AND CANNOT.** `superseded_by` is what stops a corrected row pricing, not its window: `selectors.in_force_version` has filtered on it since `DEF-RAT-5715`, `resolution_catalogue.schedule_version_facts` since `DEF-RAT-8002`, and `rating/0047`'s `ex_tariff_versions_no_overlap` carries the same predicate so the overlapping pair stays legal. Each is asserted, one test per reader plus the constraint on real PostgreSQL 16. Hard rule 4 is checked as *this change against the old behaviour* — the corrected row's `effective_to` is put back to NULL and both readers asked again — not as *before the correction against after*, which would compare the correction working with the correction not having happened. ⚠ **A STATED END DATE IS NEVER MOVED, AND THAT CLAUSE IS OURS RATHER THAN THE PO'S** (decision log 2026-09-29): a version already closed by a later rate change stopped being used when its successor started. **Worked:** 2026-02-01 → 2026-03-31, superseded on 2026-04-01, corrected on 2026-06-01, keeps **2026-03-31**; the naive rule would write 2026-06-01 and claim two months it never priced. Same rule as `rating/0046`'s own. **THE CORRECTION DATE IS THE UTILITY'S DAY** — `seams.tenant_today()`, `BR-TEN-081` read event-time under `BR-TEN-043`. ⚠ **FORWARD-ONLY. NOTHING IS BACKFILLED** — rows corrected before 2026-09-29 keep their open end, and whether they should is a SECOND ruling the PO has not taken. The figure it needs is `10-scripts/rat_schedule_overlap_census.py --corrected-open`: **0 on every database reachable from the build container, which holds no production data.** Ruling: `12-governance/02-decision-log.md`, 2026-09-29. Tests: `06-backend/tests/test_br_rat_1044_correction_closes_the_window.py` (8 service cases + 3 on `latest_version`) and `test_br_rat_1044_correction_window_pg.py` (4 on PostgreSQL 16). Built `v2.410.13`. |
| BR-RAT-1045 | **ONE DISCOUNT VERSION IS IN FORCE PER DISCOUNT PER DAY, AND THE DATABASE IS WHAT SAYS SO. (LANE JUDGMENT 2026-09-29, `DEF-RAT-8050`; decision log of the same date. NOT a PO ruling — it collides with no signed rule, moves no ❄️ FROZEN figure and needs no fact only the PO holds, so under CLAUDE.md hard-won law 13 it is ours to decide and write down. The review-ledger row puts it to the reviewer with its worked figure.)** ⚠ **THIS IS `BR-RAT-007`'s GUARANTEE FOR RATE SCHEDULES, ASKED OF DISCOUNTS — AND THE STORAGE IS DIFFERENT IN ONE WAY THAT DECIDES EVERYTHING.** On `tariff_versions` supersession is a FOREIGN KEY (`superseded_by_version_id`) and `rating/0047`'s constraint keys off it. On `discount_versions` **supersession is a STATUS** — `discounts.py`, verbatim: *"`superseded` is what a later version does to an earlier one"* — so the rule keys off `status = 'active'` and **nothing is added to model the same fact twice**. **THE RULE.** At most one `active` version of one discount may contain any given date; `effective_to` is INCLUSIVE; activating a version **closes every older open window** (`effective_to = new_from − 1 day`) before superseding it; and **a reader that meets two refuses and names both rather than picking.** **THE WORKED FIGURE, WHICH IS WHAT THE RULE IS FOR:** a senior discount whose v1 says **15%** and whose v2 says **1.5%**, both live from 1 January, on a **`$196.37`** bill — one row takes off **`$29.46`**, the other **`$2.95`**, a difference of **`$26.51` per household per month** decided by whichever row sorted first. ⚠ **`scheduled` AND `pending_approval` ARE OUTSIDE THE RULE, DELIBERATELY, AND THAT IS THE CLAUSE MOST LIKELY TO BE 'TIDIED' AWAY.** A live discount's `effective_to` stays NULL until its successor activates, so **every** approved successor overlaps the incumbent's open window by design; binding them would refuse every approval on every live discount. A proposal is not a price. Established from the code and not from the names: the only three readers that feed money or a money screen — `discount_services.active_version`, `resolution_catalogue._discount_facts`, `discount_enrolments_read` — all filter `active` and there is no fourth. ⚠ **A STATED END DATE IS NEVER MOVED, ONLY A NULL ONE FILLED** — same clause as `BR-RAT-1044` and `rating/0046`, for the same reason: somebody typed it. ⚠ **IT MOVES NO BILL ON THIS OBJECT AND THE REASON IS SPECIFIC** — every row the close touches is flipped to `superseded` one statement later by the same function, and a `superseded` row is already excluded by every reader, so the close makes the DATA say what the STATUS says and changes no answer. Measured, not assumed: `rat_schedule_overlap_census.py --discounts --reprice-check` found **0 grants on an issued invoice** on every database available, and says in those words that it therefore checked nothing. ⚠ **AND `invoice_lines` CARRIES NO DISCOUNT VERSION COLUMN AT ALL** — the stamp is `discount_grants.discount_version_id` (NOT NULL), which is where the reprice question has to be asked. **MEASURED 2026-09-29, PostgreSQL 16.13, 449 migrations:** `discount_versions` carried **0** exclusion constraints; **8** write paths touch a discount version and **0** of them closed a predecessor's window; **6 discounts, 6 versions, one each, 0 overlaps** on the databases reachable here, which hold no production data. | `DEF-RAT-8050` · `BR-RAT-007` (the version in force at the service date) · `BR-RAT-1016` verb 2 (a correction restates the SAME window) · `BR-RAT-037` · `BR-RAT-077` / `BR-RAT-109` (the approval workflow, untouched) · B2 addendum r26 (the signed constraint) · CLAUDE.md hard rule 4 · hard-won laws 1, 7 and 14 · `DEF-RAT-8051` (the taxes sibling, OPEN) · `DEF-RAT-8052` (`G137` could not read the ALTER form) | **CONFIRMED (2026-09-29, lane judgment — 🔒 HUMAN-REVIEW-PENDING)** | `rating/0048_def_rat_8050_close_discount_windows` · `rating/0049_def_rat_8050_discount_no_overlap` (`ex_discount_versions_no_overlap`) · `discount_services.active_version` · `discount_services._close_open_windows_before` · `discount_services._validate_version` gate 5 · `resolution_catalogue._discount_facts` · `10-scripts/rat_schedule_overlap_census.py --discounts` · `tests/test_def_rat_8050_discount_overlap_pg.py` |
| BR-RAT-1046 | 💰 **A COUNTED SERVICE'S PER-ASSET CHARGE (A TRASH CART, A FIRE LINE) PRORATES ON A PART MONTH EXACTLY AS THE BASE DOES (`DEF-BIL-8566`).** PO verbatim: *"A · Cart shrinks too: $9.00"*. **Worked:** an 18-of-30-day final bill, $15.00 a cart → 18/30 × $15.00 = **$9.00** (base 18/30 × $3.00 = $1.80); July cut at a price change on the 16th → 15/31 × $15.00 = $7.26 and 16/31 × $15.00 = $7.74. Applies to first, final and catch-up bills and to each part of a month split at a price change; an interim one-time bill is not prorated (OQ-242 withdrawn) and keeps the whole charge. The COUNT is never scaled, and a METERED tiered service is untouched (BR-RAT-002 scales its bound, not its quantity). | PO 2026-10-01 · decision log 2026-10-01 *"SIX PO RULINGS IN ONE MESSAGE"* | CONFIRMED | tests/test_def_bil_8566_cart_prorates_like_the_base.py |
| BR-RAT-1047 | **THERE IS NO CUSTOMER-TYPE DEFAULT PLAN (`OQ-RAT-12782` CLOSED AS A DELIBERATE ABSENCE).** PO verbatim: *"we ahve to show the warning that no plan on unit, please fi the plan first"*. The only plan that prices a bill is the UNIT's own (BR-MET-007); no table, setting or column holds a plan per customer type, and none is to be built. `rating.selectors.default_plan_for_customer_type` is deleted rather than stubbed. A unit with no plan is BR-BIL-1063. | PO 2026-10-01 · decision log 2026-10-01 *"SIX PO RULINGS IN ONE MESSAGE"* | CONFIRMED | tests/test_po_20261001_no_plan_no_default.py |
| BR-RAT-1048 | 💰 **A FLAT CHARGE-CODE AMOUNT IS ONE *ALL SIZES* ROW OF `charge_code_amounts`; `charges.amount` IS NO LONGER WRITTEN OR READ.** PO verbatim, option A: *"BASE-S $9.00 is stored as one row in charge_code_amounts with size \"All sizes\". An edit closes that row and adds a new one, the same as for sizes. The old field on the charge code is no longer written; today's values move into rows once. No new table."* The reserved size is `rating.constants.ALL_SIZES` (`"All sizes"`), one row per customer class where the code is priced by class. `rating/0051` moved every set `charges.amount` into an every-class *All sizes* row effective from `2000-01-01` (`PRICE_HISTORY_EPOCH`) and cleared the column; the model renames it `legacy_amount` and `Charge.amount` REFUSES a read. *Flat OR sized, never both* (`CR-013` part 2) stands: an *All sizes* row beside a sized row is refused at save (`rating.charges.amount_and_size_table`) and at billing. **Worked:** PAPER $2.00 → one row `(All sizes, every class, 2.0000, from 2000-01-01)`; `charges.amount` NULL. | PO 2026-10-01 · decision log 2026-10-01, rows whose last column reads *"charge-code redesign"* · `CR-156-BRC10` | CONFIRMED | tests/test_cr156_charge_prices.py · tests/test_cr156_charge_prices_pg.py |
| BR-RAT-1049 | 💰 **CHARGE-CODE PRICE ROWS ARE DATED AND NEVER PHYSICALLY DELETED ON EDIT — A CHANGED OR REMOVED PRICE IS LOGICALLY KILLED.** PO verbatim: *"why are you detleting it and not killing it"*, then *"logical kill"*; and *"we will need start and effective date"*. Every row carries `effective_from` (required) and `effective_to` (blank = no end), and `killed_at` once a later edit closes it. A save: an unchanged (size, class) row is left untouched (same id); a changed or removed one gets `effective_to = new Effective from − 1 day` and `killed_at`; the new price is a NEW row. Uniqueness is among LIVE rows; PostgreSQL's `ex_charge_code_amounts_no_overlap` refuses two rows for one (code, size, class — NULL class compared as every class) in force on one day, killed rows included. *Effective from* is required on Add and on every Edit whose prices move (⚠ INTERIM, coordinator 2026-10-01, `2.429.1`: the service takes the tenant's TODAY when a payload names none, so the live wizard keeps working until the dated screens ship; the screens still require it); a reason is still required only when the code is in use (`BR-RAT-1016`); `rate_change_log` is written for EVERY money change. Deleting a code no plan, schedule or bill ever used removes its rows; a used code is retired. **Worked:** BASE-T on 10/15 for 11/01, CART-64 $12.00 → $13.00, CART-96 newly $15.00 — the $12.00 row kept and closed 10/31/2026, $13.00 and $15.00 rows from 11/01/2026, CART-35 $9.00 and DUMP-2 $40.00 untouched (same ids); *"what was CART-64 on 09/01?"* → $12.00 from the table. Supersedes `BR-RAT-690`'s storage half. | PO 2026-10-01 · decision log 2026-10-01, rows whose last column reads *"charge-code redesign"* · `CR-156-BRC10` | CONFIRMED | tests/test_cr156_charge_prices.py · tests/test_cr156_charge_prices_pg.py |
| BR-RAT-1050 | 💰 **ONE LOOKUP ANSWERS A CHARGE CODE'S PRICE ON A DATE — EXACTLY ONE ROW OR A NAMED REFUSAL; A BILL PERIOD CROSSING A PRICE CHANGE IS SPLIT BY DAYS.** PO GO verbatim: *"19 read places must return exactly one price row"* through *"ONE shared lookup price on date (code, size, class, date)"* and *"a repo gate failing any direct query of the price table"*; option A (4): *"billing picks the row in force on the bill period's dates, and a period crossing a change is split by days"*. `apps/rating/charge_prices.py` is the lookup and the writer; gate `G141` fails any other `ChargeCodeAmount.objects`, `.amounts.<manager>()`, `legacy_amount` or raw SQL on the table. A class-specific row beats the every-class row; a row for another class is not a candidate. Billing passes the service date it prices; the cycle bill (`BR-RAT-114`'s split), the catch-up (`backbill.groups_split_by_days`) and the interim bill (price cuts only, each part over the account's cycle on that part's last day, `BR-BIL-1066`) are cut at every day a base code's price changes. ⚠ **AMENDED 2026-10-01 (PO option A, `DEF-BIL-9012`): the cut applies ONLY to the lines of the service whose base code's price changed — every other service on the bill is ONE part over the bill's period, exactly as with no cut.** PO verbatim: *"A · Split only the service whose price changed. Electric stays $12.00, and only water is split."* Worked: bill 10/16–11/15, water $8.00 → $9.00 from 11/01, electric $12.00 unchanged → electric **$12.00** (was 16/31 × 12 + 15/30 × 12 = $6.19 + $6.00 = **$12.19**), water 16/31 × 8 + 15/30 × 9 = **$4.13 + $4.50 = $8.63**. A tariff-version or plan boundary still cuts every service (`BR-RAT-114`, unchanged). **Worked:** BASE-S $9.00 → $10.00 saved 10/15 effective 11/01 — catch-up 09/01–09/30 = **$9.00**; interim 10/16–11/15 = 16/31 × 9.00 + 15/30 × 10.00 = **$4.65 + $5.00 = $9.65**; November = **$10.00**. No ❄️ B4/B5 figure moves (B4.3 is applied per part). | PO 2026-10-01 · decision log 2026-10-01, rows whose last column reads *"charge-code redesign"* · `CR-156-BRC10` | CONFIRMED | tests/test_cr156_charge_prices.py · tests/test_cr156_charge_prices_pg.py · `G141` |
| BR-RAT-1051 | **CHARGE CODES HAVE THREE KINDS — BASE, PLAN, OTHER — SET WHERE THE CODE IS CONFIGURED, NEVER ON SETTINGS.** PO verbatim: *"can we have 3 kinds base charges, plan charges and others , we will have them from setting - so in setting we will not make any change"*; *"we will need 10,11, 12, 12, 14, 23 - all as part of plan"*; *"all these will go in plan - PAPER … HYDRANT … LATE … NSF … TAMPER"*; *"we need penalties in plans … we need deposite in plans"*; *"in other add application fees + 15 RE-READ … 27 BULK-WATER … - these are all others"*. `charges.kind` ∈ base · plan · other (NULL = minted on Settings, not yet configured). Default classification (`constants.default_kind`, used by the backfill, the platform seed and an Add with no kind): `is_base` → **base**; CONN, DISC, RECON, TRANSFER, MOVE-OUT, CC-CONV, PAPER, HYDRANT, LATE, NSF, TAMPER, DEPOSIT, every penalty and every refundable deposit → **plan**; everything else (application fees, RE-READ … BULK-WATER) → **other**. `kind = base` and `is_base` are one fact and are kept in step. | PO 2026-10-01 · decision log 2026-10-01, rows whose last column reads *"charge-code redesign"* · `CR-156-BRC10` | CONFIRMED | tests/test_cr156_charge_prices.py |
| BR-RAT-1052 | **THE ADD SCREEN'S CODE PICKER OFFERS ONLY CODES NOT IN USE.** PO verbatim: *"when user select code from list show what are not in use only"* — a code in use is left out entirely, not shown disabled. `selectors.charge_code_picker(kind, service)` lists the tenant's `charges` rows (the Settings codes ARE these rows) that are not retired, of the asked kind or of no kind yet, for a base of the asked service or of none, and **not in use** — read as `BR-RAT-1016`'s existing boundary (a plan names it, a schedule reads it, a bill carries it), not as *has a price* (every seeded code has one; the PO's worked list leaves out exactly the plan-named codes). **Worked:** plan picker → TRANSFER, MOVE-OUT, CC-CONV, PAPER, HYDRANT; the in-use CONN, DISC, RECON, LATE, NSF, TAMPER, DEPOSIT absent. | PO 2026-10-01 · decision log 2026-10-01, rows whose last column reads *"charge-code redesign"* · `CR-156-BRC10` | CONFIRMED (the *in use* reading is this lane's, recorded in the decision log) | tests/test_cr156_charge_prices.py |
| BR-RAT-1053 | **A PENALTY MAY BE PRICED BY CUSTOMER CLASS — ONE PERCENT AND ONE MINIMUM PER CLASS, DATED AND LOGICALLY KILLED LIKE EVERY PRICE ROW; THE LATE FEE USES THE ACCOUNT'S CLASS.** The PO-approved charge-code mock-up (images 31, 61, 67, 77; GO ruling 2026-10-01) prices `LATE` at **1.50% minimum $5.00 for Residential and 2.00% minimum $10.00 for Commercial**. Stored as *All sizes* rows of `charge_code_amounts` naming the class with `percent` (a FRACTION, `0.015`) and `min_amount` and no `amount` (`rating/0054`; `ck_charge_code_amounts_amount_or_terms`), through the one writer `charge_prices.save_prices` — a changed class's row is closed the day before the new Effective from and marked killed. The late fee reads `charge_prices.class_terms_on(code, class, assessment day)` (a row naming the class beats an every-class row); a code with **no** class rows reads its own two columns exactly as before, so **GT-8 / GT-10's ❄️ $8.13 does not move**. A code priced by class that states nothing for the account's class and no single percent **refuses by name** (`LateFeeClassNotPriced`) — never a silent $0.00. **Worked, as tested on PostgreSQL 16:** Residential $200.00 overdue → max(1.50% × 200 = $3.00, $5.00) = **$5.00**; Commercial $2,000.00 → max(2.00% × 2,000 = $40.00, $10.00) = **$40.00** (on the single 1.5% it would have been $30.00). | PO 2026-10-01 (the approved mock-up, decision log *"GO ON THE CHARGE-CODE BUILD"*) · `DEF-RAT-8753` · `CR-156-BRC10` §10 | CONFIRMED (storage and the refusal for an unpriced class are this lane's, recorded in the decision log) | tests/test_def_rat_8753_class_terms_and_per_case.py (sqlite + PostgreSQL) |
| BR-RAT-1054 | ⚠ **AMENDED 2026-10-06 (PO ruling, option (a); `DEF-RAT-9807`) — SUPERSEDES the clause *"NO PRICE STORED"* and *"a `per_case` code states no price row (a posted amount … beside it is refused)"* below, AND NOTHING ELSE.** PO verbatim: *"when amount set now , enterd when used option provide default value for set in pricingvtab … when user chose enterde when use clark may be override it"*, then, shown (a) *"Entered when used may carry an optional default price, which a clerk may change each time the fee is used"* against (b) no stored price: *"You may enter a default, e.g. $10.00, or leave it blank - take all (a) Default allowed - options"*. **Now:** a `per_case` code MAY carry ONE optional default — its *All sizes* amount, entered on the Pricing step, blank allowed; a size table, a per-class table or a percent beside `per_case` is still refused (`rating.charges.per_case_states_no_price`). The default is served as `default_amount` and never as `amount`, so no door that charges a code's own fixed price picks it up. Wherever a clerk uses the fee (One-time bill fee, move-in Fees, New connection *Add a fee*) the amount starts at the default and the clerk may change it; the fee bill still takes the clerk's figure, so the bill carries the amount the clerk ended with. A blank default behaves exactly as before. Changing the default of a code in use needs a reason (`BR-RAT-1016`). TAP, IMPACT, DAMAGE and BULK-WATER keep no default (`rating/0055`, no data migration). **Worked:** App-Fee *Entered when used* with a default of $10.00 — a clerk adding it sees $10.00, types $25.00, and the bill shows $25.00; with no default the clerk types it every time. Supersedes chain: CR-010 A2 (2026-08-17, no stored price) → `DEF-RAT-8753` (2026-10-01, `per_case` built, no price row) → **this amendment (2026-10-06, optional default)**. 🔒 HUMAN-REVIEW-PENDING (review ledger). **— The rule as written before the amendment, kept for the chain:** **AN OTHER CODE'S AMOUNT MAY BE *ENTERED WHEN USED* — `charges.amount_rule = 'per_case'`, NO PRICE STORED, AND A FEE BILL TAKES THE CLERK'S AMOUNT FOR THAT CODE AND FOR NO OTHER.** CR-010 A2 (PO 2026-08-17) priced TAP, IMPACT, DAMAGE and BULK-WATER at *"actual cost, entered per case"*; the PO-approved mock-up draws it as *"Amount is entered when the fee is used"* (images 41, 64, 70). `per_case` is the fifth `amount_rule`; a `per_case` code states no price row (a posted amount, size table or percent beside it is refused, `rating.charges.per_case_states_no_price`), and moving a code to it closes its prices from Effective from. **Billing:** `fee_bill.charge_code_line(code, entered_amount=…)` takes the clerk's figure for a `per_case` code only — positive, at most four places, never multiplied by a count; a `per_case` code with no amount is refused, and an entered amount beside any code that states its own price is refused by name, so **`BR-BIL-020` (*"free-typed amounts never exist on any bill"*) still holds for every such code**. The One-time bill's fee kind shows an *Amount* field only when the picked code is `per_case`. R4 refuses a `per_case` code by that name (`DEF-2340`'s two readings now told apart). **Worked:** a TAP fee entered at $1,250.00 bills one line of $1,250.00; RECON (flat $75.00) with a typed $1.00 is refused. | CR-010 A2 (PO 2026-08-17) · PO 2026-10-01 (the approved mock-up) · `DEF-RAT-8753` · `CR-156-BRC10` §10 · **PO 2026-10-06 option (a) · `DEF-RAT-9807`** | **AMENDED 2026-10-06** (optional default; was CONFIRMED) — ⚠ the narrowing of `BR-BIL-020` to codes that state a price is this lane's reading of CR-010 A2 against it, recorded in the decision log and reported to the PO | tests/test_def_rat_8753_class_terms_and_per_case.py |
| BR-RAT-1055 | 🔒 **ONE RATE-SCHEDULE VERSION PER SERVICE DATE, BY ONE RULE, ON EVERY PRICING PATH — AND THE BILL-RUN CHECK FLAGS AN OVERLAP.** PO verbatim (`DEF-RAT-8004`, option A): *"two rate versions in force on the same day - how come that will happen - we have to return only 1 row; A · Use the newest everywhere, and flag it on Check"*. Where a LEGACY overlap (written before `rating/0047`'s `ex_tariff_versions_no_overlap`; a new one is refused at COMMIT and by the `CR-157-BRC10` save lock) leaves two or more live versions in force on a service date, the version that prices is chosen by `resolution.in_force_precedence`: not superseded, window contains the date, **newest `effective_from` wins, tie broken by newest `created_at`, then id**. `selectors.in_force_version` (the nightly run and everything on `pricing_inputs`) and R2 `resolution.resolve_schedule` (one-time bill, move-out final bill, corrections) both pick through that ONE function; R2's `AmbiguousSchedule` refusal and its override twin are retired with their clerk keys. The Check raises **Versions overlap** (`schedule_versions_overlap`, Plans & rates, WARNING — no sign-off tick, the account bills) naming the schedule, the overlapping versions and the one used; it reaches Sign off's warnings note and Send's count. The schedule detail serves `versions_overlap` for the View details band. `rat_resolve_schedule_overlap` is how the overlap is removed. **Worked (tested on PostgreSQL 16):** versions at $18.40 (from 1 Jan 2026) and $21.60 (from 1 Jun 2026), both in force in June → bulk run base **$21.60**, one-time interim **$21.60**, move-out final 12/30 × $21.60 = **$8.64** (never $7.36) — all three REFUSED on the base commit, the bulk run included, because the switched chain runs R2; both starting 1 Jan, the later-saved wins on every path even when it has the smaller id. | PO 2026-10-01 · decision log 2026-10-01 *"DEF-RAT-8004 RULED"* and *"ONE VERSION PER DATE — BUILT"* | CONFIRMED | tests/test_def_rat_8004_one_version_per_date.py · tests/test_def_rat_8002_two_readers_agree.py |
| BR-RAT-1056 | **A METER WITH NO RECORDED SIZE GETS NO MINIMUM, AND THE BILL-RUN CHECK NAMES THE METER.** PO 2026-10-01, option A (decision log *"A METER WITH NO RECORDED SIZE GETS NO MINIMUM, AND THE BILL-RUN CHECK FLAGS IT"*): on a schedule with per-size minimums, a connection whose meter has no recorded size bills NO minimum (no fallback, no guess — `selectors._size_minimum`, 2.433.0) and the Check raises **Meter size not recorded** (`meter_size_not_recorded`, Assets & connections, WARNING — no sign-off tick, the bill goes out) naming the meter; it reaches Sign off and Send. A unit with no meter for the service (sewer following its basis) is never flagged. **Worked (tested on PostgreSQL 16):** Water — Residential, size not recorded, $8.00 + 1 kgal × $4.4096 = **$12.41** billed, warning names `M-9001`; size recorded as 5/8" → **$15.00** and no warning. ⚠ **AMENDED 2026-10-01 — THE COLLISION WITH `BR-RAT-120` IS RULED (`DEF-RAT-8822`, PO option A, verbatim: _"Hold back only where the price depends on the size. If the base is one price for all sizes, the bill goes out at $12.41 with the warning. If the base differs by size, it's still held as today, so nobody is billed the wrong size's rate."_).** Until this amendment the $12.41 half held only with R3/R4 switched off: at the shipped `R3,R4` the ruled path refused every unsized meter on a banded service. **THE RULE NOW:** (1) the base charge code is priced *All sizes* only and the meter has no recorded size → **BILLED**, no minimum, Check warning *Meter size not recorded*, account READY; (2) the base code is priced per size → **HELD exactly as before** (`BR-RAT-120`, narrowed, not superseded); (3) once the size is recorded, that size's minimum applies. **Worked at the shipped `R3,R4`, PostgreSQL 16, bulk run AND one-time bill:** `BASE-W` *All sizes* $8.00, no size → **$12.41** (was: held); size recorded 5/8" → **$15.00**, no warning (unchanged); `BASE-W` per size (5/8" $8.00, 2" $34.00), no size → **held**, *"This connection's size is not recorded."* (unchanged). Built in `resolution_catalogue.base_is_one_price_for_all_sizes`, asked by the ruled path's adapter (`resolution_shadow.adapt`) and by R3 (`ScheduleVersion.base_priced_by_size`). | PO 2026-10-01 · decision log 2026-10-01 (two rows) · `DEF-RAT-8822` (CLOSED) | CONFIRMED (amended 2026-10-01) | tests/test_def_rat_8004_one_version_per_date.py |

| BR-RAT-1066 | 🔒 **A PLAN VERSION HAS DATES AND OWNS WHAT IT BINDS; THE SERVICE DATE PICKS EXACTLY ONE.** Each plan version carries `effective_from`/`effective_to` and owns its services, rate schedules, charge codes (late fee included), taxes, discounts, deposits, minimum bill, outside-city percent and budget billing; the plan keeps only its identity. A bill is priced from the version in force on its service date — a period crossing a version start is split there (`BR-RAT-114`); two versions that can price never share a date (database lock `ex_plan_versions_no_overlap`). *Change from a date* writes a new version starting no earlier than the utility's today and after the start of the version in force; the version in force then ends the day before. Units stay on the plan and nothing moves them. A plan with ONE version prices exactly as before (no dateless plan is reinterpreted, `DEF-438`). Worked: v1 09/01/2026 – 12/31/2026, v2 from 01/01/2027 — 12/31/2026 is priced by v1 and 01/01/2027 by v2, never both and never neither. | PO-approved drawing 2026-10-03 (`CR-167-BRC10` ruling 1, image 41) closing `OQ-324` as (B) | CONFIRMED (PO 2026-10-03) | `CR-167-BRC10` §9 |
| BR-RAT-1067 | 🔒 **A LIVE PLAN'S NAME AND DETAILS ARE SAVED ON THE SAME VERSION; EVERY PRICE CHANGES FROM A DATE OR BY A CORRECTION.** Name, code, description, customer classes, intended-for ticks and notes price nothing, so a live plan's *Name and details* (ruling 1) saves them on its current version with a before → after Audit line and no reason asked. Any other field sent to `PATCH` on a live plan is refused by name (`rating.plans.field_needs_new_version`); a price changes through *Change from a date* (`BR-RAT-1066`) or *Fix a mistake* (`BR-RAT-1016`). Narrows `BR-RAT-037` for these six fields only. Worked: renaming *Residential City Plan* keeps it v1; its minimum bill $10.00 → $12.00 from 01/01/2027 makes v2. | PO ruling 1 of the plans redesign, 2026-10-03 (*"Name and details (same version)"*), coordinator-agreed `CR-167-BRC10` §7.5 B2 | CONFIRMED (PO 2026-10-03) | `CR-167-BRC10` §9 · `tests/test_cr167_plans_round2.py` |
| BR-RAT-1068 | 🔒 **A CAPACITY LINE PRICED AT A $0 RATE IS NOT ON THE BILL.** When a capacity charge (solar, battery or any service whose installed sizes sum, or a `capacity`-model schedule) prices to $0.00 because its RATE is exactly $0, the engine does not emit the line. It is never stored, and so never appears on the bill-run review, the bill, the document or the portal. The base charge and tax are unchanged. **Only a $0 rate hides it:** 0 kW at a real rate still prints `0 kW × $4.00 = $0.00`. Equipment with no recorded size is still held before pricing. Counted services (trash, fire) are out of scope, because their per-item charge shares one line with the base. Already-issued bills are not re-rendered (hard rule 4). Worked: solar 6 kW at $0/kW + BASE-SOL $25.00 + 4% tax → one line $25.00, tax $1.00, total $26.00; Municipal Solar 6 kW × $4.00 = $24.00 + $10.00 + $1.36 = $35.36 (unchanged). | PO 2026-10-17, option B (*"please start these changes"*); decision log *"A $0.00 CAPACITY LINE IS LEFT OFF THE BILL"* and *"BUILT: THE $0.00 CAPACITY LINE IS NOT EMITTED AT ALL"* | CONFIRMED (PO 2026-10-17); the rate-not-amount test, the scope and the issued-bill reading are lane judgments, decision log (2)–(4) — built 2026-10-17 (`DEF-RAT-10210`) | `tests/test_def_rat_10210_zero_rate_capacity_line.py` |
